# CreateStoryline — Product & System Specification

**Status:** normative draft. This document is the build contract. Requirement ids (`P1`, `D-3`, `T-1`, `L-4`, …) are stable: assign work by id, cite ids in commit messages and tests.

**Audience:** a swarm of agents building the Server and the Dashboard in parallel. Each workstream (§12) is independently buildable against the interfaces in §5–§9. Where a section says MUST, a build that omits it is wrong; where it says SHOULD, it is expected but a reasoned deviation is acceptable if recorded in §14.

---

## 1. Product thesis

An **Author** sits at the top of a story stack and wants many stories moving at once. They write the thing only they can write — the **Seed**, the intention of the story, and later the **lock** on each phase — and then the system does the hundreds of small, tedious, consistency-preserving jobs that make a story hold together, forever, until the Author says stop.

The system is a **loop engine**. It constantly scans every Story for unfinished work, and for each piece of work it **opens a fresh thread** (one employee with fresh context), hands it the story id and one job, and tears it down when it reports. The Author's screen is a queue of decisions, not a chat box.

**Rapid** is the product goal. Rapid means:

- **R1** — the Author never faces a blank page. Employees draft; the Author signs.
- **R2** — a decision costs the Author one interaction (pick, approve, reject, one line of direction).
- **R3** — work happens in parallel across stories and across chapters.
- **R4** — nothing waits on the Author that does not need to.

**Authorship is preserved.** The Author is the only terminal authority: the loop ends when the Author stops it, a chapter is done when the Author says it is done, the Seed is untouchable by agents, and so is the Summary once the Author has locked it. Speed that costs the Author their book is not speed.

**The target is fidelity, not quality.** The objective is _a story this Author would have written by hand_ — not the best possible story, which is subjective and unachievable on purpose. Every template is a piece of the Author's own judgment, automated: what they like in prose, what they notice, what they would fix. A system that produces a technically better book that isn't theirs has failed. This is a requirement, not a philosophy (§5.3).

**The product is three phases, each ending in a lock.** Idea → **Summary** → **Plotlines** → **Chapters**. Each phase is a crew working until its Goal passes, then stopping for the Author's signature; the signature locks the artifact, and the next phase builds only on locked ground. The loop does not run endlessly by design: it runs _to_ a sign-off, not _until_ it gives up (§6.3a, §6.6).

**There is no deadline.** Runs may take days. Nothing in this product is gated on tokens or wall-clock (§11). The only failure the loop recognises is **going in circles**, and its response is to stop and tell the Author, not to give up.

---

## 2. Principles

These are load-bearing. Every later section derives from them.

**P1 — The Dashboard owns memory. Threads are stateless workers.**
Every Run opens a brand-new thread with no history. Therefore **the only memory in the system is the Dashboard's durable store.** An employee acting as an agent that cannot write a fact into the store has not produced it. A thread that dies mid-run loses nothing except its own uncommitted output.

**P2 — Fresh context every turn.**
A Run MUST never reuse a previous run's thread or transcript. This is the anti-context-rot mechanism: reviewers are independent because they have never seen the last review. It also means every turn pays a full re-read, which is why the **Digest** (§4.8) is mandatory rather than an optimisation.

**P3 — A story is its records.**
Nothing that is not in the store is part of the story: not a chat message, not an agent's reasoning, not a run's stdout. Prose lives in `chapters`. Structure lives in `plotlines`. Judgment lives in `verdicts` and `findings`. Direction lives in `tasks`.

**P4 — Every unit carries a contract, and the loop asks one question.**
A chapter carries a **Plotline**: what must happen in that chapter. The loop's question is never "is this good?" but **"does this artifact deliver its contract?"** — answerable by a fresh reviewer with a cited line of evidence.

**P5 — The idea is sacred; the Summary becomes sacred at the lock.**
The Author's **idea** is the seed and is read-only for every agent, forever. The **Summary** is Phase 1's _output_: five named groups — premise, promise, theme, tone, ending — each written and revised on its own line, composed into the Overview the loop measures everything against, and **locked** by the Author (§4.19, R1/R2). A Writer pass rewrites the groups a defect named, and what it accepted becomes the current text the moment it is accepted — the text it replaces goes into that group's history, where it stays (R2/R5). Before the lock the Summary is a draft like any other; after it, it is law, and the only way in is the Author unlocking, which backtracks (§6.3a). (A book whose `summary_mode` is `'sampling'` — every Story written before 2026-11-24 unless the Author imported it — still runs the older whole-Summary round: N competing samples, Critics screen them, the engine keeps the least-issue one and deletes the rest, §16.)

**P6 — Pass/fail criteria, not scores.**
A fresh context grading 1–10 is noise. A boolean with a citation is stable. The Author controls the goal by editing a list of criteria and a threshold, never by dragging a quality slider.

**P6's one exception, and it is a display exception only (D-65, §17.14).** A grouped Story may show the Author a Reader's 0–100 score per Summary section — because "how consistent is this Overview?" is a question the Author wants answered in the same units they'd ask it, and a progress surface they can watch move over revisions is information, not a decision. The exception is bounded by what the number may NOT do, stated as the mechanics actually implement it: **no score value, and no scoring seat in any owed count, enters the criteria engine, drain threshold, hill-climb, sign-off gate or goal math**; the pass files no finding and opens no task, and no gate ever WAITS on a score. The one thing that does count is the run's existence: `wi_summary_score_` is in `phrase/exclusion.ts:GROUP_PIPELINE_RUN_PREFIXES`, so an **in-flight** scoring run still counts as in-flight group-pipeline work in the drain's open-thread line (`loop/status.ts`) — and once it closes it holds nothing, because `groupPipelineOwed` has no scoring field and `isGroupHoldingRun` refuses the kind. `grep -rn "summary_scores" src/server/loop` is empty apart from the one place that WRITES the rows — the rule is mechanically checkable, not just asserted here. **The one read the loop has since added (R-lock, §17.18) stays inside that boundary and does not move it:** `loop/editor-lock.ts` reads a section's score row for its RATIONALE — the seat is not armed at all when there is no reason written down — and its number is compared to NO threshold anywhere, so the P6 sentence above still holds word for word; the collection's snake-case name still appears under `loop/**` in no file but the one that writes it.

**P7 — Escalate, never silently halt.**
When a loop cannot proceed — no progress, a contradiction only a human can resolve — it parks a **question for the Author**. It does not stop and go quiet, and it does not spin.

**P8 — The Author can always pull the cord.**
A Stop on a Story or a global Stop halts dispatch immediately and kills in-flight runs. A stopped Story never auto-resumes.

**P9 — Agents propose against Author prose.**
An agent never silently overwrites text the Author wrote. It files a proposal. (See §7 permissions.) This is the one respect in which the Writer's monopoly (P10) is limited: the Author's own words outrank the Writer's.
**P9a — The Author writes prose too, in the UI, and only while the Story is paused.**
The chapter editor is not a read-only view: the Author edits the book with their own hands. To keep that from colliding with a crew mid-sentence, the edit requires the Story to be `stopped` (D-37). The pause is the lock; the resume is the hand-back.

**P10 — The Writer writes everything.**
Only the **Writer** employee kind writes story artifacts: the Summary, the plotlines, chapters, prose, cast, ledger. (Two named exceptions, and only two: the **Digest** is not part of the book but a derived index of it, and the engine builds it from commits — D-12a; and in phase ① the engine persists each validated structured answer of a tool-less run on the same engine-writes principle — a `GroupDraft` install across the groups of the Summary on a grouped book (§4.19, R5), a `SummarySample` `revisions` row on a legacy one (§16.5). A run authored the text; no tool wrote it.) Every other employee — **Editor**, **Critic**, **Reader**, or **Researcher** — reads, judges, files findings, opens tasks, proposes Ideas, writes Knowledge, and requests the Author; none of them writes a story artifact. A reader that thinks it can improve a sentence files the task and lets the Writer write it. This is what keeps a hundred independent fresh reviewers from producing a hundred different prose styles: one voice, many auditors.

**P11 — Every change is attributable.**
Every mutation records who made it and carries a one-line `why`. The Author must be able to see, for any paragraph, which run changed it and for what reason — and undo it.

**P12 — Templates are the Author, not best practice.**
A Writer, Editor, or Critic role in a template encodes _this Author's_ taste and _this Author's_ habits — the prose they like, the things they look out for, the calls they would make. A template is not a generic expert; it is the Author's own editorial judgment, externalised and automated. Built-in templates are starting points to be edited into the Author's likeness, never authorities to be deferred to.

**P13 — Revisions are ephemeral; one survives.**
Multiple attempts at the same chapter or plotline exist **at the same time**, side by side, and are compared by an Editor (on a legacy `'sampling'` Summary the samples are ranked by the engine, §16.6). When the Editor selects one — or merges several, or on a legacy book the engine picks — **every non-selected revision is deleted.** Losing the alternatives is deliberate: a pile of rejected drafts is exactly the context rot that P2 exists to prevent, and a fresh run that reads four versions of chapter 7 has already lost the thread. The chosen text is the only thing that persists; the reasoning for the choice persists as a record, not as the drafts. **The grouped Summary is the one place this principle does not delete anything** (R2, §4.19): a superseded group text is `archived` rather than destroyed, because a grouped install has no alternative on offer for anyone to be confused by — every reader, prompt and review reads the composed Overview, which is one text per group — and the history is where the Author's "what did it say before?" is answered without a journal dig. P2's rule is about what a fresh context READS, and a grouped reader still reads exactly one thing.

**P14 — A Story has a status, and the loop is allowed to say "I'm stuck."**
A Story is `ready` (employees may work on it), `pending` (the loop has stopped dispatching and the Author is the next mover), or `completed` (the Author has accepted the book as it stands, leftovers and all). `pending` has flavours and they are not the same news: **awaiting the Author** — a phase passed its bar, Ideas need picking, findings need approving, or the Story is **drained** and the crew has run out of work it is authorised to do — and **stalled**, the only flavour that means trouble: the loop stopped converging and hit a wall. Both are normal; neither is an error (§6.6, L-34, L-37, L-37a). A loop that quietly spins for three days on a contradiction only a human can see is worse than one that stops after an hour and asks.

**P15 — This is a build contract for the first build, not a constitution.**
The engine is expected to change. The design is settled enough to build (§15) and deliberately not settled enough to be final: the Author's own position is _"we build it out, test it in building, and then revise the engine."_ Two consequences for the build: the loop, the store, and the tool surface are built so that a phase model, a role set, or a criterion can be **replaced without rewriting the store**, and anything the spec marks as deferred (recipes, sequel access, §14) stays a **named hole** rather than being quietly designed in. A section that survives contact with a real book unchanged is the surprise, not the norm.

---

## 3. Glossary

### 3.1 The actors — Author, employee, agent, thread

These words are not synonyms, and using one for another is the wording defect this section exists to prevent:

- **Author** — the human user of the product, and the only terminal authority in it. The Author owns the Seed, the Goal, the locks, every approval, and the decision to stop or complete a Story. The Author is never an employee and never an agent.
- **Employee** — a standing member of the global roster: **Writer**, **Editor**, **Critic**, **Reader**, **Researcher**. An employee has a kind, a rank, a model, and a §7 permission row. It is configured once, exists across every Story, and does nothing on its own.
- **Agent** — an employee **while a thread is executing**. "Agent" is an execution noun: it names the runtime actor inside `@underload/harness`, never a tile, a roster entry, or a template role. An idle employee is not an agent; an agent exists only for the life of its thread.
- **Thread** — one execution of one employee for one Work Item: one Run, one brief, one fresh context. A thread lives inside the Story phase's **session** and is destroyed when the Run reports. The Run row is the bookkeeping record; the thread is the engine object; they are one-to-one.
- **Phrase** — two things wear this word, and the split is deliberate. (a) The UI's name for a Story phase's **session** (P1 Summary, P2 Plotlines, P3 Chapters): threads run inside a phrase; a Run is a thread, never a phrase. (b) The **Summary Phrase**: the §4.19 record kind (`phrases.key = 'summary'`) that holds the composed Overview the five Phrase Groups produce. **Amended 2026-11-26 (U-20, §18.8): the rail stopped showing "exactly one phrase of sense (a) — Summary" when §18 gave the desk a second enabled phrase — it shows the phrases the book has, Summary ① and a started Plotlines ② (`components/story-nav.ts:STORY_PHRASES`, pinned at two rows by `components/StoryOutline.test.tsx`), and Chapters ③ carries that number in `ALL_STORY_PHRASES` and in its pane title rather than on a rail row.** Inside Phrase ① the Author edits **groups**, not phrases, which is the half of this row that still rules. Nothing in the store, the loop or the data model branches on sense (a).
- **Phrase Group** — one of the five named, separately revisable parts of the Summary Phrase: **Premise**, **Promise**, **Theme**, **Tone**, **Ending** (`shared/phrase.ts`, §4.19). A group is a row (`phrase_groups`), it has its own revision line, and it is the unit of a Writer install, a live hold and a finding's target.
- **Group Revision** — one text of one group, numbered from that group's own line (§4.19, R2). Exactly one group revision is `latest`; the ones it replaces are kept as `archived`. A group the batch did not write gets no revision row and keeps both its text and its number.
- **Overview** — the composed Summary text: the five groups' latest texts, in catalog order, under `## <title>` headings (§4.19). It is generated, never hand-edited, and it is what phase ②/③ and the deep review read.
- **Template** — a crew definition: roles, prompts, `selection_rules`, `attempts`, done-definition. A template role says _which employee kind_ takes a job and _how_; the template is neither an employee nor an agent.
- **Rank** — the harness engine's delegation authority, `A` highest. Delegation is strictly downward, so same-rank peers cannot delegate to one another. **Only a Rank A employee may own a dispatched Work Item, take action on a phase's requirements, and report to the loop.** A Rank B employee MAY be added to a harness, but only as a delegation target inside a Rank A thread: it never receives a Work Item, never gets a Run row of its own, and never reports to the loop independently. Rank is not a quality grade, never gates a tool (T-6a), and never changes a §7 permission row.

**V-1** The meanings above are the only ones used in this document. Roster language says **employee**; execution language says **agent**; the unit of execution is a **thread**; the phase's session is a **phrase**. The **Editor** is the Author's one channel: a directive is addressed to the Editor, and the Editor's review turns it into queued tasks. "Entry" is not a roster concept and is never shown to the Author; the harness's internal session-routing name is the Editor.
**V-2** The loop dispatches Work Items only to Rank A employees. A Rank B employee is legal only inside a Rank A thread's delegation, and that thread remains the one that acts and reports.
**V-3** (Summary Phrase, 2026-11-24.) The phrase/session word and the Summary-Phrase word are not interchangeable: a document, a comment or a screen that means the writable parts of the Summary says **group**, **group revision**, **Overview** or **group vector**, and never a bare "phrase". A group is addressed in code and on the wire by its `phrase_groups.id` (a UUID); the catalog key (`premise`, `promise`, `theme`, `tone`, `ending`) is for humans and prompts. "World rules" is neither a group nor a revision line: it is §4.2's deprecated field, and no writer, reader or install may mint a group for it (R1).

| Term                 | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| -------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Author**           | The human user of the product and its only terminal authority: owns the Seed, the Goal, the locks, approvals, stop and completion. Never an employee and never an agent.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **Story**            | One work of fiction the Author is building. The unit of the loop. Identified by `storyId`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **Employee**         | A standing roster identity — Writer, Editor, Critic, Reader or Researcher — with a kind, a rank, a model and a §7 permission row. It becomes an **agent** only while a **thread** executes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **Agent**            | An employee in execution: the runtime actor of one **thread**. Never a roster entry, a tile, or a template role.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **Thread**           | One execution of one employee for one Work Item: one Run, one brief, one fresh context, inside a phase's **phrase** session.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **Phrase**           | (a) The phase session a thread runs inside (P1 Summary · P2 Plotlines · P3 Chapters). A Run is a thread, not a phrase. (b) The §4.19 `phrases` record — the Summary Phrase — holding the composed Overview. **Amended 2026-11-26 (U-20, §18.8): the rail shows the phrases a book has — Summary ① and a started Plotlines ② (`components/story-nav.ts:STORY_PHRASES`, pinned at two rows by `components/StoryOutline.test.tsx`) — and Chapters ③ carries that number in `ALL_STORY_PHRASES` and in its pane title rather than on a rail row.** The writable units inside Phrase ① are still groups. See V-3. |
| **Summary Phrase**   | The `phrases` row of kind `summary` (§4.19): the phrase kind every Story is born with, whose content is the generated Overview of its five groups. Until 2026-11-26 this was THE one phrase kind the build enabled, and the sentence "a second phrase kind is a catalog entry, not a loop change" was a promise; §18 keeps it, and the catalog's second ENABLED kind is `plotlines` (§4.20) — an entry the Author must START. The catalog holds exactly two definitions; the desk's "Characters" and "Chapters" panels are panels, not phrase kinds. R1 keeps the catalog data-driven; what the parts of a phrase MEAN is the job of the Phrase Controller, not of a branch in the loop. |
| **Phrase Group**     | One of the five writable, separately revisable parts of the Summary Phrase (`phrase_groups`, §4.19): Premise, Promise, Theme, Tone, Ending. The unit of an install, a live Writer hold (R4) and a finding's target (R3). |
| **Group Revision**   | One text of one group, numbered from that group's own line (§4.19, R2). One `latest`, the superseded ones retained as `archived`. Premise at revision 2 beside Promise at revision 1 is the model working, not an inconsistency. |
| **Overview**         | The Summary Phrase's composed content: every group's latest non-empty text under its own `## <title>` heading, in catalog order (`shared/phrase.ts:composePhraseContent`). Generated; never hand-edited. |
| **Group vector**     | The `{group_id: revision_number}` map of the whole Summary as installed, hashed to a short digest (`phrase/revisions.ts:groupVectorHash`). It is the identity of a Summary state, and it is what the reading seats and the deep review are keyed by — an install moves the vector, so review re-arms by itself. |
| **Crew**             | The employees a template's roles put to work on a phase, together with the threads they cause. A working set, not a persistent identity.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **Rank**             | The engine's delegation authority: `A` highest, delegation strictly downward. Rank A employees own Work Items; Rank B employees may only be delegated to (V-2).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| **Seed**             | The Author's own idea for a Story, in their words, held in `stories.idea`. Written once by the Author, read-only for every employee — and therefore every agent — forever. The contract Phase 1 is measured against. **A store word AND a desk word since 2026-11-26 (U-25, §10.2 / §18.13):** the word also labels a row under EVERY phrase, and the text that row opens is the PHRASE's own — Phrase ①'s Seed row opens the read-only pane over `stories.idea`, a started phrase's Seed row opens its `phrases.seed` copy (§4.20, D-72). **The bytes never moved:** `stories.idea` is still the only place the Author's idea is held, and nothing was copied into the Summary phrase's `seed` column — what changed on 2026-11-26 is where the desk SHOWS a Seed, not where its text lives.                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| **Summary**          | Phase 1's artifact: the **five named groups** — premise, promise, theme, tone, ending — each with its own revision line, composed into the **Overview** the Writer employee expands from the Seed and the Author locks (§4.19, R1/R2). Once locked it is the contract everything downstream is measured against. §4.2's sixth prose field, `world_rules`, is DEPRECATED and is not a group: the legacy sampling pipeline wrote six fields, the group model writes five (R1, R6). |
| **Idea**             | A proposal contributed by a Critic (or the Author) at the current phase: a claim, a possibility, a "what if", with its sources. The Editor verifies it; the Author selects it or discards it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **Phase**            | One of the three stages: `summary`, `plotlines`, `chapters`. A Story is in exactly one at a time.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| **Lock**             | The Author's signature ending a phase and freezing its artifact for all employees. Only an Author **backtrack** unlocks it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **Backtrack**        | The Author's decision to reopen an earlier phase. Everything later phases produced is scrapped, hard, and the reopened phase starts fresh.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **Plotline**         | The per-chapter contract: what must happen, what must change, what must be set up or paid off.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| **Chapter**          | The prose for one Plotline. Has a `state` in the pipeline.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **Cast**             | Characters, each with want / need / arc / voice.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **Ledger**           | The continuity facts: setups and their payoffs, timeline, world rules, named facts.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **Digest**           | The compressed standing view of a Story (§4.8) that lets a fresh reviewer judge one chapter without re-reading the book. **Derived by the loop engine from committed changes — no employee writes it** (D-12a).                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| **Pause & edit**     | The Author's way of writing prose themselves: stop the Story, edit the book in the UI, resume. The pause prevents the race; the resume re-derives the Digest and re-checks dependents. (P9a, D-37, D-37a, U-17)                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| **Prequel access**   | Deferred, not built: giving a sequel's phase ① employees read access to the finished previous book. The only planned exception to single-Story scoping. (D-39, §13)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **Goal**             | The Author-owned criteria list + threshold that defines "consistent enough".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **Criterion**        | One pass/fail check with a checker kind (`code` or `judge`) and a `hard`/`soft` flag.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| **Finding**          | A named defect against a specific artifact, with evidence and a fingerprint. From a Critic or reader it starts `unconfirmed` and is worth nothing until an Editor confirms it (D-33).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| **Verdict**          | One run's pass/fail report against a set of criteria.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| **Task**             | Work the loop or the Author has queued toward the Goal.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **Work Item**        | A claimable unit of dispatch: one job, one story, one employee kind. Opens one Run — one thread.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **Run**              | One thread inside a phase session: one employee executing one Work Item for exactly one `storyId`, with one brief and no history. The Run row is its bookkeeping record.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **Harness Template** | A reusable crew definition: roles and their kind (Writer / Editor / Critic / Reader / Researcher), prompts, which phase it serves, its done-definition, its `selection_rules`, how many attempts it spawns. A role assigns an employee kind to a job; the template is itself neither an employee nor an agent.                                                                                                                    |
| **Recipe**           | A named sequence of stages + templates + done-definitions.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **Directive**        | A line of Author direction addressed to the Editor; the Editor reviews it and queues the tasks, and the loop must propagate them through the story.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **Writer**           | The Rank A employee kind with write access to story artifacts. It writes the Summary, the plotlines, the chapters, and the prose.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| **Editor**           | The Rank A employee kind that **chooses**: it compares competing revisions of the same unit and selects one or merges several, by the Author's stated rules. It then deletes the losers (P13).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| **Critic**           | A Rank A employee kind that reads as the Author reads and is allowed to _propose_. In phase ③ it is a **judge of effect**: does this chapter diverge from its plotline, does this reference hold up, is this mystery spent too early — and it says so. In phase ② it contributes **Ideas**, researched from outside the store where it needs to be. In phase ① on a grouped Summary it has two runs: a tool-less **group reading seat** (`review_groups`) that returns a `GroupReview` — every issue naming the groups it is about, an input to routing and not a finding — and a tool-using **deep review** of the Overview as installed, whose findings must carry `group_refs` and are suspicions until an Editor confirms them (D-33, R3, §17). On a legacy `'sampling'` book the two runs are the `IssueList` screening pass and the deep review of the selected draft (§16.5, §16.7). |
| **Researcher**       | The fifth Rank A employee kind (§16.3): it researches the facts the Seed Brief depends on, reads everything, and writes only **`knowledge`** — plus a queue task when the work is not its own (D-11). It never writes a story artifact, and a write that reaches for one is refused naming P10. Its output is what the `critical`/`high` triage filter carries into a Writer's first turn — the **group draft** prompt on a grouped Summary, the sampling prompt on a legacy book; the gate itself is the SAME `researchGate` predicate under both models (R5, §16.5, §17).                        |
| **Reader**           | The Rank A employee kind that reads and judges, files findings/tasks/verdicts, and never writes a story artifact.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| **Revision**         | One competing attempt at the same unit — a plotline or a chapter, and on a legacy Summary — held side by side until an Editor selects or merges (P13); in the LEGACY phase ① pipeline the engine selected deterministically and hard-deleted the losers (§16.6). On a grouped Summary there is nothing to select: the unit is a **group revision** (§4.19), an accepted Writer answer becomes `latest` immediately, and what it replaces is archived, never deleted (R2, R5). |
| **Install**          | The group model's verb that replaces `select`: one commit writes each written group's next `latest` revision, archives the rows it replaces, recomposes the Overview and rewrites §4.2's mirror fields — and touches neither `chapter_count` nor `world_rules` (`phrase/revisions.ts:installGroupRevisions`, R2/R5/R6). The batch, not the Story, is the unit of the commit. Its phrase-② sibling is the same commit writing §4.3's contract from the entry's plotpoints (`phrase/revisions.ts:installGroupRevisions` → `phrase/plotpoints.ts:syncPlotlineContract`, §18). |
| **Phrase Controller** | (2026-11-26, R11, §18) What a phrase KIND means to the loop, as one plain object of functions bound to its catalog definition: whether the lane is managed, where the phrase row and its groups are, how the lane is STARTED, how its groups are materialised, what answer contract its jobs carry, how an accepted answer is parsed and INSTALLED, how one entry's text is rendered, what claim family its drafts use, and what its Writer/review/score/lock prompts are told (`src/server/phrase/controller.ts`, one controller per ENABLED kind, reached through `phraseController(definition)` / `phraseControllerOf(store, storyId)`). The rule it exists to enforce is checkable: a loop or prompt change that supports a THIRD phrase must not need a new `if (phrase === 'summary')`. An **unknown phrase id is refused, never guessed** — "nobody owns this phrase", not "treat it like the Summary". |
| **Plotline Brief**   | (2026-11-26, R11, §4.20) The Editor's structured projection of Phrase ②'s **copied seed** — the chapter count, the per-chapter seeds and optional notes, in `plotline_briefs`. §3.3's Seed-Brief shape one phrase down, with the same `draft → accepted` lifecycle; acceptance is the irrevocable act, because that is where the count becomes `summaries.chapter_count` and the chapter set becomes `chapter_1…N` entry rows (§18, D-72). The Author never types into it and no Writer may touch it. |
| **Plotline Contract** | (2026-11-26, R11, §4.20/§18) A phase-② group row's text: the machine-read parts a chapter's beat plan commits to — `must_happen` and `must_change` required, `heading`, `pov`, `setups`, `payoffs` optional — under a `chapter_<n>` group key. On a live lane this text, not a `plotlines` row, is what phase ② WRITES; §4.3's row is the generated artifact of accepting it. |
| **Status**           | A Story's dispatch state: `ready`, `pending`, `completed`, `stopped`. Distinct from `phase`, which is where it is in the pipeline. `pending` is qualified by `pending_kind`, and only `stalled` means anything went wrong.                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **Drained**          | The state in which the crew has run out of work it is _authorised_ to do: no task, no unconfirmed finding, no competing revision, and a fresh round of Critics found nothing at or above the dial threshold. The loop stops dispatching and the Author has two moves — work the leftovers, or complete. It is not completion and it is not a fault. In phase ① the drain also owes the pipeline's own stages — on a `'sampling'` book, the Seed Brief, the closed research pass, every screening pass, and every deep-review pass of the selected revision (§16.7); **on a grouped Summary those stages are the GROUP pipeline** — `loop/status.ts:phase1Unmet` takes a group branch that reports live group-pipeline runs, the groups still owing a draft or a fix pass, the Critic seats of the CURRENT group vector, and its deep-review passes — all from `phrase/exclusion.ts:groupPipelineOwed`, the same predicate the queue is generated from (L-37, L-37a, §17.8). A `'sampling'` book takes the sampling branch instead and never sees a group line; a grouped book never sees a sampling line. A group that has spent its write allowance is PARKED, and a parked group is not owed — otherwise the drain could never close (§17.6).                    |
| **The dial**         | `stories.auto_approve` — which finding costs flow to a Writer without asking the Author. The same setting is the **drain threshold**: it decides what still counts as work, and so when the crew stops. (D-35, D-35b, L-37)                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

---

## 4. Data model

The store is the Dashboard's, not the harness's. Collections below are normative in name and field; a build MAY add fields, MUST NOT rename or drop one.

### 4.1 `stories`

| field              | type           | notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------ | -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `title`            | text, required | Author-set.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `phase`            | enum           | one of the three phases: `summary`, `plotlines`, `chapters`. There is no other value; `completed` is a `status`, not a phase.                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `idea`             | text, required | **Author-written.** The seed. Read-only for every agent, at every phase, forever (P5). If the Author changes it, the Story starts again from scratch (D-25a).                                                                                                                                    |
| `summary_locked`   | boolean        | true = Phase 1 signed off; `summaries` is read-only for every agent. Author-set only.                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `plotlines_locked` | boolean        | true = Phase 2 signed off; `plotlines` are propose-only for every agent. Author-set only.                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `pending_kind`     | enum           | `phase_signoff`, `idea_selection`, `finding_approval`, `drained`, `stalled`, `none`. **The first four are the loop waiting for the Author by design**, not a fault (§6.6). `drained` is the one that means "we are out of authorised work" (L-37); `stalled` is the only one that means trouble. |
| `status`           | enum           | whether agents may work on it: `ready`, `pending`, `completed`, `stopped`. **The sweep dispatches only `ready` Stories.** `stopped` has two causes and they are the same mechanism: the Author pulled the cord (P8), or the Author **paused it to edit the prose themselves** (D-37).            |
| `status_reason`    | text           | why it is `pending` — the criterion that will not move, the contradiction found, the level it fell to, or (when `drained`) what the crew stopped finding and what is left awaiting the Author. Rendered on the Pending review screen (U-1a, U-1c).                                               |
| `consistency`      | text           | the measured levels as a sentence: `idea→summary 4/4 · summary→plotlines 6/6 · plotlines→chapters 4/6`.                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `trend`            | enum           | `improving`, `flat`, `regressing`, `unknown`. Drives the `pending` transition (§6.8).                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `goal_ref`         | text           | id of the `goals` record this Story measures against.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `auto_approve`     | strings        | which finding costs skip the Author's approval gate — a subset of `critical` / `major` / `minor` / `taste`. Defaults to `critical, major, minor` (D-35). Author-set.                                                                                                                                    |
| `author_stop`      | boolean        | true = no dispatch, no auto-resume. Set by the Author only; distinct from `status: pending`, which the loop sets and the Author clears.                                                                                                                                                          |
| `progress`         | text           | one-line human summary the loop maintains ("7 chapters · act 2 has a hole · 3 setups unpaid").                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `blocked_on`       | text           | what it is waiting for, or empty. The tree renders this.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `digest_rev`       | integer        | revision of the current Digest.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `summary_sampling` | integer        | phase ①'s **batch size**, under BOTH readings, and the name did not change when the meaning did (R5, §17). Grouped Summary (`summary_mode: 'groups'`): how many **groups** one tool-less Writer pass may rewrite in one `draft_groups` answer (standard 3). Legacy `'sampling'` Summary: how many competing one-turn drafts one round produces (standard 3, §16.5). Author-set; absent means the standard; bounded by `SUMMARY_REVIEW_LIMIT`. |
| `summary_critics`  | integer        | phase ①'s **reading seats**, under both readings (R5, §17). Grouped Summary: how many tool-less `review_groups` passes judge each installed Overview (the group vector), and how many tool-using deep-review passes follow them (standard 5). Legacy: how many screening passes judge each sample and how many deep-review passes the selected revision owes (§16.5, §16.7). Author-set; absent means the standard; bounded by `SUMMARY_REVIEW_LIMIT`. **Reading seats are never attempts against a group's Writer allowance.** |
| `summary_mode`     | enum           | **Summary Phrase (2026-11-24).** Which Summary authoring model the Story uses: `'groups'` (the five §4.19 groups are the writable truth, an accepted answer installs immediately, and §4.2's prose fields are a generated mirror) or `'sampling'` (the pre-redesign §16 pipeline: N whole-Summary drafts compete and the engine selects). **ABSENT means `'sampling'`**, which is what makes a legacy book readable with no migration (R6). Only two things write `'groups'`: `POST /stories` (every new Story is born grouped, with its catalog rows in the same commit) and the Author's explicit `POST /stories/:id/phrases/import`. A read never writes it, and nothing flips a Story back on its own. `phrase/catalog.ts:isGroupManagedStory` is the single predicate every guard reads. |
| `editor_group_locking` | boolean    | **R-lock (2026-11-25, §17.18).** Whether the Editor may put a Summary group into the locked state. **ABSENT means OFF**, and OFF means `loop/editor-lock.ts` produces nothing at all — no seat, no run, no lock; the same read gates the prompt and the settlement, so a Setting can never be ON for one and OFF for the other (`phrase/catalog.ts:editorGroupLockingEnabled`, `=== true`). ON arms ONE judgement seat per group per CLEAN vector (`wi_group_lock_<cleanVectorHash>_<groupId>`, §17.18) in which the Editor weighs that section's clean text, the Critic's open claims and the Reader's rationale, and answers whether the text is finished. It is a PERMISSION, not a threshold: no number appears in the seat's eligibility rules or its copy, and turning the Setting OFF lifts nothing — a lock lives on the group row, and the Author's unlock is the only ACT that lifts one, though the flag also goes wherever the revision rows it names go (a Seed reset, or the scrap that deletes them, §17.18). Written only by `PATCH /stories/:id` as a strict boolean. Not `summary_locked`, which is §6.3a's whole-Summary sign-off on a different record. |
| `plotlines_mode`   | enum           | **Plotlines Phrase (2026-11-26, §18, R11).** Which phase ② authoring model the Story uses: `'groups'` (Phrase ②'s `chapter_<n>` entries are the writable truth, an accepted `ChapterDraft` materialises §4.3's rows, and the classic plotline tools are closed on the lane) or `'classic'` (§6's phase-② machinery — a Writer expansion, an Editor's `selection_rules` choice, and the crew's `createPlotline`/`editPlotline`). **ABSENT means `'classic'`**: the no-migration rule R6 states for `summary_mode`, applied to this flag (`phrase/catalog.ts:isPhraseGroupManaged(story, 'plotlines')` is the single predicate every guard reads, and nothing derives the answer from the presence of rows). Written by `POST /stories` — every Story created since this date is born `'groups'` unless its caller asks for `'classic'` (`loop/engine.ts:2887`) — and by `PATCH /stories/:id`, both checked by the same two-words rule, though each copy names the two pipelines in its own words (`POST /stories` 864-871, `PATCH /stories/:id` 1814-1822). **The dial alone does not open the lane:** it is live only when the Story ALSO has a started `plotlines` Phrase row (`phrase/plotpoints.ts:plotlineLaneIsLive`), so a book created after this date and never started keeps its classic tools. Once the phrase row exists the dial no longer moves — a PATCH that would change it in EITHER direction is a 409 naming the row that made it unmovable (a write that changes nothing stays legal) — rather than silently re-pointing the entries that carry the work; the dial plus the Start verb are the two facts, and a refusal that named only one would send the Author to a door that does not open (§18). |
| `sampling_round`   | integer        | phase ①, **legacy mode only**: the count of COMPLETED sampling rounds (§16.2). Engine-maintained (`loop/types.ts:LoopStoryAdditions`); a grouped Story never advances it — its accepted state is the group vector, not a round (§17). |
| `sampling_wave`    | integer        | phase ①, **legacy mode only**: the claim-key generation the retry counter is scoped to (§16.5), also `loop/types.ts:LoopStoryAdditions`. Never written by a grouped Story. |
| `sampling_history` | list           | phase ①'s P11 audit, **legacy mode only**: one line per completed round — every sample's revision id and `critical`/`high` counts, plus the selected id. The losers' rows are hard-deleted, so this line is the stored record of what competed (§16.6). A grouped Story writes nothing here: its history is the five archived group lines (§4.19, R2).                                                                 |

**D-1** A Story's `phase`, `status`, `pending_kind`, and the two lock flags are owned by the loop engine and the Author, never by an agent tool. An agent has no tool that locks, unlocks, or backtracks.
**D-2** The Dashboard MUST support any number of Stories in any phase, simultaneously, and dispatch work across all of them.
**D-2a** `status` transitions, and who owns them:

| from → to                              | trigger                                                                                                                                                          | owner                          |
| -------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ |
| `ready → pending`                      | the consistency trend is `flat` or `regressing` (§6.8)                                                                                                           | loop                           |
| `pending → ready`                      | the Author reviews and clears it, or issues a directive                                                                                                          | Author                         |
| `* → stopped`                          | the Author presses Stop                                                                                                                                          | Author                         |
| `stopped → ready`                      | the Author presses Resume                                                                                                                                        | Author                         |
| `ready → completed`                    | Phase 3 is signed off: every hard criterion passes at every level and the crew concurs (§6.9)                                                                    | loop proposes, Author confirms |
| `completed → ready`                    | the Author edits anything, or a `needs_recheck` lands                                                                                                            | either                         |
| `ready → pending` (`phase_signoff`)    | a phase's Goal passes and its artifact awaits the Author's lock (§6.3a)                                                                                          | loop                           |
| `pending → ready` (`phase_signoff`)    | the Author locks the phase, or declines and lets the crew keep working                                                                                           | Author                         |
| `ready → pending` (`idea_selection`)   | Critics have filed Ideas that need the Author's pick before the phase can advance                                                                                | loop                           |
| `ready → pending` (`finding_approval`) | the only remaining blockers to the phase's Goal are `confirmed` findings the Story's switch does not auto-approve (L-36)                                         | loop                           |
| `ready → pending` (`drained`)          | a full pass leaves no task, no unconfirmed finding, no competing revision, and one fresh round of Critics produced nothing at or above the dial threshold (L-37) | loop                           |
| `pending` (`drained`) `→ ready`        | the Author approves any leftover finding, or issues a directive — either re-opens work                                                                           | Author                         |
| `ready` (unchanged)                    | some findings await approval but other work is still possible (L-36)                                                                                             | loop — **no pause**            |
| `* → earlier phase`                    | the Author **backtracks**: the later phases' artifacts are scrapped and the reopened phase starts fresh (D-25)                                                   | Author, alone                  |

**D-2b** A `pending` Story MUST NOT receive new work, but its records stay fully readable and the Author can inspect every verdict and finding that led there. `pending` is a pause, not a failure.

### 4.2 `summaries` — one record per Story; Phase 1's artifact

| field           | type              | notes                                                                                                                    |
| --------------- | ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `story_id`      | text, required    |                                                                                                                          |
| `chapter_count` | integer, required | **Author-declared** at `POST /stories` — and since 2026-11-26 there is ONE other hand that moves it, and it is not a Writer: accepting Phrase ②'s brief writes the count that brief carries (§4.20, §18's D-72). With the prose fields below, this is the Story's source of truth. D-3b is unbent: the structuring pass, not the drafting pass, owns the number. |
| `premise`       | text              | **What the story is.** On a grouped Story this field is a **generated mirror** of the Premise group's latest text, written in the same commit as the install (§4.19, R6) — no agent writes it directly any more, and every old reader keeps reading it. Legacy: the Writer's prose. |
| `promise`       | text              | the promise made to the reader; the ending must pay this. Mirrored from the Promise group on a grouped Story. |
| `theme`         | text              | Mirrored from the Theme group on a grouped Story. |
| `tone`          | text              | voice, POV, tense, register. Mirrored from the Tone group on a grouped Story. |
| `ending`        | text              | known or intended. Mirrored from the Ending group on a grouped Story. |
| `world_rules`   | text              | **DEPRECATED (R1, 2026-11-24).** The sixth §4.2 prose field, kept exactly where it is for legacy books and for the Digest. It has **no group, no revision line and no writer**: the group model has FIVE groups and the mirror never touches this field. On a grouped Story nothing writes it at all — the install skips it (`phrase/revisions.ts`), the tool that used to (`tools/writer.ts:editSummary`, phase `summary` only) is never dispatched because a grouped book's Writer runs are tool-less, and `POST /stories/:id/summary/revise` answers 409 there (R5). A legacy `'sampling'` book keeps writing all six fields (`engine.ts:SUMMARY_PROSE_KEYS`). A continuity read of world rules belongs to `ledger` (§4.6), and that is where new work goes. |
| `locked`        | boolean           | mirrors `stories.summary_locked` (L-7a). true = even the assistant cannot propose an edit without an Author backtrack.   |

**D-3** While `phase = summary` and `summary_locked` is false, `summaries` is writable by the **Writer** — expanding the Seed is Phase 1's whole job. In the shipped group model the five prose fields are not written by any agent at all: they are the **mirror** of the group install (§4.19, R6), and the hand that moves them is the engine's commit (R5). In the legacy `'sampling'` build the expansion is a tool-less sample the engine installs in the selection commit (§16.6). The record is the Story's source prose either way. It is also writable by the Author at any time, and by an **assistant run the Author explicitly invoked on the Summary field only** — with the one exception R5 draws: on a grouped Story the Author's Summary verb is the per-group `POST /stories/:id/phrases/groups/:groupId/revise`, and the whole-Summary verbs answer 409. Once `summary_locked` is true, every agent write is refused with a message naming P5, and the only way to change it is an Author backtrack (D-25). A locked Summary also makes the install skip the mirror write rather than fail the install (`phrase/revisions.ts`, R6).
**D-3b** Two things are source of truth, and they are different. `stories.idea` is **Author truth**: immutable to every agent at every phase, never proposed, never inferred, never drifted. `summaries.chapter_count` is **Author truth too**: declared by the Author, never chosen by the Writer, and a hard fact — Phase 2 produces exactly that many plotlines and a deviation is a failing criterion (C-0), not a creative choice. The Summary's _prose_ is the **Phase 1 artifact**: it is negotiated until the lock and law after it.
**D-3d** The Summary is never accepted as its first draft, and phase ① reaches that conclusion without the chapter machinery in either of its two readings. On a **grouped** Summary (R2/R5, §17) the discipline is not "N versions of everything, keep one" but **"the parts a defect named, rewritten by name"**: a batch is refused unless every group in it is answered (`parseGroupDraftAnswer`), the accepted text installs and is immediately read by `summary_critics` independent seats, a group gets two rewrite attempts per text, and nothing that was written is ever deleted. On a **legacy** Summary the divergence is **sampling**: N tool-less one-turn Writer samples per round, M tool-less screening passes per sample, and an engine selection that is deterministic — fewest `critical` → fewest `high` → fewest total → the earliest sample — with the losers hard-deleted (§16.1, §16.6). Phases ② and ③ keep §6.2a's divergence-and-selection shape (N Writer expansions, readers, an Editor applying `selection_rules`, losers deleted under D-22), and the principle is unchanged: the Seed is short and the thing the whole book hangs off is not a place to accept the first draft. An Editor's phase-① choice existed in the 2026-11-22 build and was removed by the research pipeline (§16.8).
**D-3c** A change to `chapter_count` after the plotlines lock MUST reopen Phase 2 by backtrack (D-25): added chapters enter the queue as `outlined`, removed chapters are deleted with their prose held for the Author's decision, and every dependent chapter is marked `needs_recheck`.
**D-4** The locked Summary is the root contract. Every Plotline MUST be traceable to it; the loop's first consistency check is Plotline-vs-Summary, before any chapter exists. Above the Summary sits the Seed, which the Summary itself must be traceable to (C-14) — the chain is `idea → summary → plotlines → chapters`, and every artifact names the one above it.
**D-5** Changing an **unlocked** Summary MUST mark every dependent Plotline and Chapter `needs_recheck` (§6.5). A _locked_ Summary cannot be changed by an agent at all; the Author changes it by backtracking, which scraps the dependents rather than rechecking them (L-11, D-25).

### 4.3 `plotlines` — one per chapter, the chapter's contract

**Phase ② has two writers since 2026-11-26 (R11, §18).** On a classic Story — `stories.plotlines_mode` ABSENT or `'classic'`, or a Story with no started Phrase ② row — this record is written by §6's phase-② machinery exactly as it always was, and the crew's `createPlotline`/`editPlotline` are its only agent writers. On a live Plotlines lane (the dial AND a started phrase row) these rows are a **generated mirror of Phrase ②'s chapter entries**: `phrase/revisions.ts:installGroupRevisions` writes one row per accepted chapter entry through `phrase/plotpoints.ts:syncPlotlineContract` — create-or-update by chapter `order`, from the SAME plotpoints the entry text was composed from, engine-owned flags set on create only — the Author's per-group revise route writes one when a contract is edited by hand, and `tools/writer.ts:guardPlotlinePhraseOwnership` refuses a crew `createPlotline`/`editPlotline` whose `order` an entry covers. **The record's shape and every downstream reader are untouched:** the criteria that grade a chapter's contract (C-3 and C-5…C-8, the same fields `createPlotline` takes) and the L-11/L-11a consistency rules read §4.3 rows whether a Writer typed them or an install copied them, and phase ③ never learns which lane produced its contract.

| field             | type              | notes                                                                                              |
| ----------------- | ----------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `story_id`        | text, required    |                                                                                                    |
| `chapter_ref`     | text              | the chapter this plotline governs.                                                                 |
| `order`           | integer, sortable | chapter sequence.                                                                                  |
| `heading`         | text              |                                                                                                    |
| `must_happen`     | text              | the events that must occur.                                                                        |
| `must_change`     | text              | what must be different by the end — state, knowledge, relationship, position.                      |
| `pov`             | text              |                                                                                                    |
| `setups`          | text              | planted here.                                                                                      |
| `payoffs`         | text              | collected here.                                                                                    |
| `contract_locked` | boolean           | mirrors `stories.plotlines_locked` (L-7). A locked plotline is read-only for agents; they propose. |
| `aligned`         | boolean           | last verdict's result against the Summary.                                                         |
| `needs_recheck`   | boolean           | set by invalidation (§6.5).                                                                        |

**D-6** Plotlines are agent-writable until `contract_locked`, then propose-only. The Author locking the plotlines is the gate from phase `plotlines` to phase `chapters` (§6.3, L-7).

### 4.4 `chapters`

| field              | type              | notes                                                                                                   |
| ------------------ | ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `story_id`         | text, required    |                                                                                                         |
| `plotline_ref`     | text              |                                                                                                         |
| `order`            | integer, sortable |                                                                                                         |
| `title`            | text, required    |                                                                                                         |
| `summary`          | text              | short synopsis.                                                                                         |
| `content`          | text              | the prose.                                                                                              |
| `state`            | enum              | `outlined`, `drafting`, `in_pass`, `in_review`, `needs_author`, `done`, `needs_recheck`                 |
| `current_revision` | text              | the revision an Editor selected; the only one that survives (P13). Empty while revisions are competing. |
| `author_locked`    | boolean           | true = agents propose only, never rewrite.                                                              |
| `bar_override`     | text              | per-chapter goal override: a criterion id to drop, or a threshold delta. Author-only.                   |
| `round`            | integer           | passes run on this chapter.                                                                             |
| `last_score`       | text              | `n/m criteria passed`, rendered not scored.                                                             |

**D-7** `content` MUST be section-addressable: a long chapter is stored as ordered sections so a reviewer can change one paragraph without rewriting the chapter. A whole-chapter rewrite MUST be an explicit, separate operation from a section edit.
**D-8** Every write to `content` records `author_ref`, `run_ref`, and `why` (P10).

### 4.5 `cast`

`story_id`, `name`, `want`, `need`, `arc`, `voice`, `state_now`, `traits`, `gender`. **D-9** A character's `state_now` is a tracked fact: a reviewer checks behaviour against `arc` + `state_now`, not against vibes.

### 4.6 `ledger`

`story_id`, `kind` (`setup`, `payoff`, `fact`, `timeline`, `world_rule`), `subject`, `detail`, `chapter_ref`, `status` (`open`, `paid`, `conflict`, `stale`), `fingerprint`.

**D-10** Ledger checks are `code`-checked wherever the check is mechanical (a `setup` with no matching `payoff`; a `timeline` entry out of order; a `fact` contradiction on a shared key). Mechanical continuity MUST NOT be delegated to an LLM's judgment.

### 4.7 `tasks`

`story_id`, `kind`, `title`, `detail`, `owner_role`, `state` (`open`, `claimed`, `blocked`, `done`, `wontfix`), `priority`, `criterion_ref`, `finding_ref`, `attempts`.

**D-11** Tasks are the loop's work queue and are agent-writable: a reviewer that finds a defect files the task that fixes it. This is how the goal is reachable "regardless of number of turns" — the queue, not the transcript, holds the plan.

### 4.8 `digests` — one current record per Story, plus history

`story_id`, `rev`, `body` (bible recap + per-chapter one-line summary + open threads + cast state + last change notes), `stale`.

**D-12** The Digest is rebuilt or patched on every commit that changes story state.
**D-12a** **The Digest is derived, not authored.** The **loop engine** rebuilds it deterministically from the committed change history — never a crew, never the Writer (P10 excepted here deliberately). The reason is that the Digest is the one artifact every fresh run trusts without checking: if the same agent that wrote chapter 9 also summarised it, a wrong chapter 9 becomes a wrong bible and the error is invisible downstream forever. So: the engine composes it from commits and the records they touched; a crew may only file `digest_gap` (D-13) to say the derivation is insufficient; and because it is a pure function of committed history, **any past Digest is reconstructible** — the Author can see what a reviewer was actually told at the time it judged chapter 20. Its `rev` advances with the commits it summarises, and it carries no `why` because no one chose it.
**D-13** A run's brief is: `Digest + the artifact under review + the criteria`. A run MUST NOT be required to read the whole book; if the Digest is insufficient for a judgment, the run files a `digest_gap` finding and the loop repairs the Digest.
**D-14** A stale Digest blocks a review, not a fix. The loop refreshes then re-dispatches.

### 4.9 `verdicts`

`story_id`, `target_ref`, `target_kind`, `run_ref`, `template_ref`, `round`, `criteria_passed` (list of `{criterion, result, evidence, finding_ref}`), `hard_pass`, `soft_count`, `recommendation` (`advance`, `fix`, `stuck` — stuck means the reader believes the Story should go `pending`).

**D-15** A verdict with no `evidence` string on a failing criterion is invalid and MUST be rejected by the tool.

### 4.10 `findings`

`story_id`, `target_ref`, `criterion_ref`, `title`, `detail`, `evidence`, `cost` (`critical`, `major`, `minor`, `taste`), **`kind`** (additive: `issue`, `proposal` — see D-33b for what each claims and D-33c/D-33d for which one the loop answers FIRST; absent means `issue`), `fingerprint`, `state` (`unconfirmed`, `confirmed`, `approved`, `fixed`, `disputed`, `discarded`), `confirmed_by` (an Editor run, or empty), `approved_by` (the Author, or `auto` — D-35), `seen_count`, **`group_refs`** (additive, Summary Phrase R3: the `phrase_groups.id` UUIDs this defect is about).

**D-33b** **A finding says which kind of claim it is** (2026-11-25). `cost` answers "how much does the repair cost"; nothing in §4.10 answered "is this a defect, or a better idea?" — and that gap had a behaviour: a Critic comparing the text to the Seed found that the Seed says nothing about X, filed it, and the Editor's admissibility rule (D-33, §6.4) disputed it, correctly, because the Seed never promised X. Meanwhile a genuine improvement to the story had nowhere to go except dressed up as a defect. So the record carries `kind`:

- **`issue`** (the default, and the reading of every row raised before the field) claims the artifact **fails a commitment**, and its proof stays a quotation: name what the Seed or the artifact states, show it broken. The absence rule is its admissibility test;
- **`proposal`** (the token, the code's `FINDING_KINDS`, and the only alternative to `issue`) proposes a change the Seed never had to promise. The title **proposes** rather than accuses, `evidence` quotes the passage the change would improve, and `detail` says what to change and why the Writer pass is worth taking.

**The pairing rule, in both directions (`tools/reader.ts:raiseFinding`, refusal id `D-33b`).** The two kinds own opposite ends of the cost ladder, and each end-combination is REFUSED rather than quietly re-triaged:

- **`proposal` + `critical`** — "`critical` is a contradiction the text cannot live with — of itself, of the Seed, or of critical Knowledge — and a proposal the Seed never promised is not one." `critical` also sits on the auto-approve dial by default (D-35), so a wish wearing `critical` would buy a Writer pass and skip the Author's gate. The `fix` says: `cost: major` at most with `kind: proposal`, or `kind: issue` when the draft contradicts something it states;
- **`issue` + `taste`** — "an Issue is rated `critical`, `major` or `minor` — a preference is a Proposal (`kind: proposal`), not a defect." This is the mirror, and the half that keeps the first rule from being one-sided: without it the taxonomy leaks the long way round, because a Critic files a word-choice wish as a `taste` ISSUE, it reads as a defect in the Author's queue, and the Editor's absence gate then disputes a claim that should never have been called a defect. The `fix` says: `kind: proposal` with `cost: taste` when it is a preference, or `cost: minor` at least when it really is a defect.

An OMITTED `kind` takes the `issue` reading, so it falls under the second rule too — a `taste` finding with no `kind` is refused, which is the point: absence may not smuggle a preference into the defect column.

**Neither rule reaches `verifyFinding`.** The Editor's verification stays kind- and cost-neutral and may re-cost a row to any rung it can justify, because **D-34a makes that triage final** — a `proposal` the Editor confirms at `minor`, or an `issue` it re-rates to `taste` on the Author's read, is a correct call the raise-time rule never had to predict. What the rule protects is the REVIEWER'S copy: the ladder a Critic may claim from, before anything is triaged. (The Editor IS reached by D-33d — but at `approveFinding`, one step later, and not by either cost pairing. D-33c and D-33d below.)

**D-33c** **Issues are filed before proposals, and never in the same pass** (2026-11-25). D-33b separated the two kinds; it said nothing about their ORDER, and the order turned out to be the part the loop needed. A review pass used to answer both questions at once, and the round paid for it: the Critic filed three defects and one improvement in the same run, the dial approved whatever cost it covered kind-blind, and a Writer pass went to the suggestion while the crew's own agreed defects sat unworked. `raiseFinding` now refuses the two shapes that make that possible (both under refusal id **`D-33c`**), and both read ONE predicate — `loop/records.ts:openIssueFindings`:

- **a PROPOSAL WAITS FOR THE ISSUES.** `kind: 'proposal'` is refused while the Story holds any unresolved issue, and the refusal counts them (`open_issues`, `open_issue_refs`) and names the first few ids so the crew can see what it is waiting behind. The `fix` says: resolve every open issue — verify it (confirm or dispute), approve, fix or discard — then file the proposal, or file this as `kind: issue` if the draft actually breaks something. **An issue is unresolved exactly while its state is in `loop/types.ts:ISSUE_HOLDING_STATES` — `unconfirmed`, `confirmed` and `approved`** (2026-11-25), which is §4.10's own shorthand below ("Open means any of `unconfirmed`, `confirmed`, `approved` that is not yet `fixed`") and the set both dashboard open lists draw.
