**L-8** After the plotlines lock, **all chapters are worked on together by default** — the whole chapter set is in flight at once, not one at a time. Sequential expansion is an option the Author may choose for a short Story, not the default.
**L-8a** Because chapters run together, **consistency is a cross-chapter problem and is handled automatically**: a defect found in chapter 12 that touches something chapter 1 asserts marks chapter 1 `needs_recheck` in the same commit, and the sweep dispatches a Writer to fix whichever side the plotline says is wrong. A Story whose chapters are only ever checked against its own past is a Story that discovers its contradictions at the end (C-4, C-6).
**L-9** The Story MUST carry a declared dependency set (which chapters depend on which) — not to _sequence_ the work, which is what it was for before, but to say **which chapters a change must propagate to**. A chapter with no declared dependency is a chapter nobody will notice has moved.
**L-10** A chapter reaches `done` when its Goal passes **and** the Author accepts, or when the Author marks it done outright ("good enough"). The Author's word outranks the criteria in both directions.
**L-10a** Divergence and selection run concurrently for every chapter, not one chapter at a time (C-B). The unit of concurrency is the chapter; the unit of correctness is the whole chapter set.

### 6.5 Invalidation

**L-11** Within a phase, any commit that changes a Story-level artifact (a world rule, chapter order, an unlocked Summary) MUST mark every dependent plotline and chapter `needs_recheck`, and MUST reopen any chapter already `done` that the change touches. **A change to a _locked_ artifact is not a commit — it is a backtrack** (L-31): the Author unlocks, the later phases are scrapped, and nothing is "rechecked" because there is nothing left to recheck.
**L-11a** Propagation runs **both directions in time**: a contradiction between chapter 1 and chapter 12 marks _both_ `needs_recheck`, and the loop fixes the side the plotline says is wrong. "Fix the later one and hope the earlier one still reads" is not a rule, it is a hope (L-8a).
**L-12** A `needs_recheck` chapter is re-entered into Loop B automatically. Phase ① carries no `needs_recheck` marker: an Author Seed edit or a backtrack re-arms the pipeline from gate 1 (§16.1, D-25a/D-25), because the Summary's samples are rounds, not re-checkable chapters. Without this rule, already-approved chapters rot silently — the exact failure P2 exists to prevent, relocated.

### 6.6 What ends work

There is **no round limit, no token limit, and no time limit** on a loop. A Story may be worked on for days. What ends work, in order of how normal it is:

1. **A phase sign-off** (`pending_kind: phase_signoff`) — the loop reached the bar the Author set and stopped. This is the designed stop, and it happens three times per Story (L-29).
2. **A decision from the Author** (`pending_kind: idea_selection` or `finding_approval`) — Critics have put alternatives on the table, or the Editor has confirmed findings the Story's switch does not auto-approve, and the phase cannot advance until the Author picks or approves (D-29, D-34). Note the qualifier: this wait only begins when **nothing else is dispatchable** (L-36). Until then the pile just grows on the Author's desk while the crew keeps working, which is the intended shape — the loop runs continuously and the Author drains it at their own pace.
   2a. **Drained** (`pending_kind: drained`) — the loop has run out of work it is authorised to do: no task, no unconfirmed finding, and a fresh round of Critics produced nothing at or above the dial threshold (L-37). This is the normal end of a phase's work and the entry point to completion. The Author either picks up the leftovers or marks the Story done.
3. **Author stop** (per Story, or global). Immediate: no new dispatch, in-flight runs killed. A stopped Story never auto-resumes. (P8)
4. **Author marks a chapter or Story done**, overriding a failing Goal.
5. **Completion** — the Story is **drained**, every hard criterion passes, and the Author accepts — which discards whatever findings and Ideas are still open (L-25, L-25b) → `completed` (§6.9). Note that drained (2a) is the loop _stopping_; this is the Author _closing_ it. The loop never completes a Story by itself.
6. **The loop detects it is going in circles** → `pending` with `pending_kind: stalled` (§6.8). Work pauses for the Author; it does not fail.

**L-36** **An unapproved finding does not halt a Story by itself** (D-34). A `confirmed` finding sitting in the queue is a pending _decision_, not a blocked _Story_: the Story stays `ready`, the sweep keeps dispatching everything that does not depend on that decision, and the loop keeps raising, verifying, and queueing more underneath the Author's pile (L-25b).
**L-37** **The loop runs until it is drained, and then it stops.** A Story is **drained** when, on a single full pass over the Story, all of the following hold:

1. no `approved` or `open` task remains for a Writer;
2. no finding remains `unconfirmed` — every suspicion a Critic or reader raised has been confirmed or disputed (D-33);
3. a fresh round of Critics and readers produced **nothing at or above the dial threshold** (D-35b) — the crew is no longer finding anything it is authorised to work on;
4. no unit still has `competing` revisions awaiting an Editor (D-23).

At that point the sweep sets **`status: pending`, `pending_kind: drained`** and dispatches nothing further. The Story is not complete and not stuck: the crew has run out of work it is allowed to do. The Author now has exactly two moves, and both are theirs to make — **work the leftovers** (approve a sub-threshold finding, which spawns a task and returns the Story to `ready`), or **mark it completed** (L-25, L-26). Condition 3 is what makes drained a real state rather than a momentary gap between sweeps: one clean pass at threshold, with no cost constraint to make it expensive (E-0).
**L-37a** **Drained is checked before stalled, and it wins.** A drained Story has exactly the `trend: flat` signature that L-21 reads as "going in circles" — levels unchanged, no fingerprint closing — so without this precedence a book that has simply run out of problems would be reported to the Author as **stuck**. It is not stuck; it is finished by the standard the dial set. `pending` therefore means "the Author is next" in every case except `stalled`, which is the only one that means trouble.
**L-34** Items 1 and 2 are the loop **working**; item 6 is the loop **stuck**, and the two MUST be distinguishable on screen at a glance. A Story waiting for a signature the Author has not got round to is not the same colour as a Story that has stopped converging.

**L-13** Condition 6 MUST produce an Author-facing explanation on the Pending screen, not a log line.

### 6.7 Loop detection — the measurement

A fresh reviewer every turn (P2) makes "is it improving?" a hard question, because a new reviewer will happily re-report a finding that was already fixed and it will read as a regression. So progress is measured on **stored, deduplicated facts**, never on a run's self-report.

**L-14** The Dashboard maintains, per Story, one **consistency level per phase boundary** (the table in §5.2):

| level                | measured as                                                  |
| -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `idea→summary`       | C-14, C-15 passing, over the Summary draft                   |
| `summary→plotlines`  | hard criteria C-0…C-6 passing, over the plotline set         |
| `plotlines→chapters` | hard criteria C-7…C-9 passing, over the expanded chapter set |

A level for a phase the Story has not reached is `n/a`, not `0/n` — a Story in Phase 1 is not failing at chapters.

**L-15** `trend` is computed from the level history, which is retained per round:

- `improving` — any level rose, or an open finding fingerprint closed;
- `flat` — **every** level unchanged for `flat_rounds` consecutive rounds **and** no finding fingerprint closed;
- `regressing` — any level fell, or a previously-closed fingerprint reopened.

**L-15a** Only **confirmed** findings count in `trend` (D-33). An amnesiac fresh reviewer that keeps raising the same _unconfirmed_ suspicion is not evidence that the Story is stuck, and confirming it is the Editor's job — not a reason to page the Author.

Only levels the Story has actually reached are consulted: a Story in Phase 1 cannot be judged on a chapter level that does not exist yet.

**L-16a** A re-reported finding is **not** a regression: it increments `seen_count` on the existing fingerprint (D-16). Only a fingerprint that had been _closed_ and came back counts as going backwards. Without this rule, an amnesiac reviewer makes every long-running Story look like it is failing.

### 6.8 Going in circles → `pending`

**L-21a** `pending` has two causes and they are different machines: an **awaiting-the-Author wait** — sign-off, Idea pick, finding approval, or **drained** (L-29, D-29, D-34, L-36, L-37) — which is planned and expected, and a **stall** (L-21), which is not. Only the stall consults `trend`; the wait consults nothing but the queue, and it ends the moment the Author acts.
**L-21** Only after L-37 has been ruled out: when `trend` is `flat` for `flat_rounds` rounds, or `regressing` beyond `regress_tolerance` — counting only **confirmed** findings (L-15a) — the Story moves to **`status: pending`** and the sweep stops dispatching it (D-2a). The Author's tree shows it as needing review, with the reason.
**L-22** `status_reason` MUST name the specific stuck thing (for a sign-off wait, the artifact awaiting the lock and the empty Ideas tray count): which level, which criteria will not move, which finding fingerprints have been open the longest **and are confirmed rather than disputed**, which chapters are involved, and what the crews tried. "Consistency low" is not a reason; "C-4 has failed on chapters 6–9 for 11 rounds; the crews keep moving the mentor scene and the reader keeps objecting to it" is.
**L-23** The stalled screen MUST offer the Author the four exits, as buttons: **edit the unlocked contract, or backtrack to the phase that owns it** (the contract was wrong), **edit a template's instructions or `selection_rules`** (the crew's taste or its brief was wrong), **issue a directive** (the crew needed a decision), **lower the bar** (this criterion does not matter to me). Every one of those re-arms the Story to `ready`.
**L-24** Neither kind of `pending` is an error state, and neither MUST be rendered as one. A Story waiting for a signature is the product working; a Story that stopped converging is the product being honest. A Story that goes pending after four days of good work is the system working correctly: it found something only the Author can settle.

### 6.9 Completion

**L-25** The loop **proposes** `completed` when the Story is **drained** (L-37) and, simultaneously: every **hard** criterion passes at every level; the ledger has no `conflict` or `open`-past-deadline entries; and the soft-pass count is at or above `threshold_soft`. Drained is the trigger — the loop stops looking for work and tells the Author so — and the criteria are what makes the stop worth accepting rather than just quiet.
**L-25b** **Completion does not require an empty findings list.** The loop runs continuously and will always find something; a bar of "zero open findings" is a bar no book clears, and it would leave the Story working forever on `minor` and `taste` items the Author does not care about. What it needs is the **Author's acceptance** (L-26). Whatever is still open at that moment — findings, `proposed` Ideas — is **discarded by the act of completing**, and the count is recorded and shown, so the Author can see what they waved away rather than pretend it was never raised.
**L-25a** `completed` **is Phase 3's lock**: the Author's confirmation locks the chapters exactly as the Summary and plotline locks work, and the sweep stops dispatching. There is no fourth phase.
**L-26** Completion is then **confirmed by the Author** — the loop proposes it, the Author accepts it, and the acceptance is what closes the remaining findings and Ideas (L-25b). This is the last place the machine gets to overrule a human, and the first place a human is allowed to overrule a very long to-do list.
**L-27** `completed` is not permanent. Any Author edit (made from a pause and applied on resume — D-37), any `needs_recheck`, or any directive returns the Story to `ready`, and the sweep picks it up again. A finished book the Author keeps writing by hand stays policed forever — which is the point of a loop that never runs out of time.
**L-28** "All the agents agreed" means the _hard_ criteria pass under independent fresh review, not that no reviewer has an opinion. A Critic with a `taste`-cost finding open does not block completion (D-16, G-8).
**D-39** **A completed Story is a dead end on purpose.** Nothing is exported from it and nothing is inherited by the next Story: no taste record, no "what this Author always rejects", no carry-over of the discarded Ideas the lock already deleted (D-36). The records — verdicts, findings, `selection_note`s, the run history — survive for audit and for the Author to read, and they are read by nobody automatically. A sequel is **not** a continuation of state: it is a new Story, and the feature it needs (deferred, §13) is giving its phase ① crews **read access to the prequel's artifacts** so the Writer, Editor and Critics can form the new book against the old one. That is the whole design reserved for later, and it is deliberately not built now: it is the one case that breaks the rule that a run is scoped to exactly one Story (T-3), so it gets an explicit exception when it is designed rather than a half-built second scoping path now. The Seed stays the only cross-book thing the product offers in v1 — and copying it by hand is already how siblings work (D-25a).

---

## 7. Permissions

The table's role columns name **employee kinds**, not agents: a permission attaches to the employee's kind and is exercised only by the thread that kind is currently executing (V-1). The fifth kind — the **Researcher** — is not given a fifth column here because its row is already written: `read` everywhere the table reads, with the exceptions §16.3 states (T-1c). Only a Rank A employee owns a Work Item and may act on a phase's requirements; a Rank B employee has no column of its own because it never acts outside a Rank A thread's delegation (V-2). No rank changes a row below, and no tool is hidden by rank (T-6a).

| Artifact                                                        | Author                                            | Assistant (Author-invoked)          | **Writer**                                                                   | **Editor**                                              | Reader / **Critic** (read + report)                                                                             |
| --------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `stories.idea` (the Seed)                                       | write                                             | read                                | **read only**                                                                | read only                                               | read only                                                                                                        |
| `summaries` (prose)                                             | write until locked                                | propose, one field, only when asked | **write in phase `summary` while unlocked**; read only once `summary_locked` | read only                                               | read only                                                                                                        |
| `summaries.chapter_count`                                       | write                                             | propose                             | **read only, always**                                                        | read only — EXCEPT the one structuring act: accepting Phrase ②'s brief writes the count that brief carries (§4.20, §18's D-72). D-3b is about the WRITER, and that half is unbent | read only                                                                                                        |
| `ideas`                                                         | write, **discard**                                | read                                | read                                                                         | **verify** (fact + guidelines, D-31)                    | **write** (`proposeIdea`)                                                                                        |
| `ideas` — a `discarded` or `selected` one                       | read, keep                                        | read                                | **read** (D-32)                                                              | **read** (D-32)                                         | **read** (D-32)                                                                                                  |
| `plotlines`                                                     | write                                             | write                               | write until `plotlines_locked`, then propose — **on a live Plotlines lane this row is the install's mirror, not a tool write: the crew's `createPlotline`/`editPlotline` are refused on an `order` a chapter entry covers, and the row moves when that entry's `ChapterDraft` is installed (§4.3, §18's D-75)** | **read only**                                           | **read only**                                                                                                    |
| `chapters.content` (agent-written)                              | write **only while `stopped`** (D-37)             | write                               | write                                                                        | **read only**                                           | **read only**                                                                                                    |
| `chapters.content` (Author-written span)                        | write **only while `stopped`** (D-37)             | propose                             | **propose only**                                                             | read only                                               | read only                                                                                                        |
| `chapters.title/summary`                                        | write                                             | write                               | write                                                                        | read only                                               | read only                                                                                                        |
| `revisions`                                                     | read, restore                                     | read                                | **create** (via `editChapter`; a grouped Summary has no rows here at all — its texts are §4.19's `group_revisions`, engine-installed; on a legacy book the samples are engine-created, §16.5)  | **select / merge / delete**                             | read only                                                                                                        |
| `cast`, `ledger`                                                | write                                             | write                               | write                                                                        | **read only**                                           | **read only**                                                                                                    |
| `digests`                                                       | read                                              | read                                | **read only**                                                                | read only                                               | read only — **no agent writes it**; the engine derives it (D-12a), and a crew's only move is `digest_gap` (D-13) |
| `tasks`                                                         | write                                             | write                               | write                                                                        | write (files the fix)                                   | write (files the fix)                                                                                            |
| `verdicts`, `findings`                                          | read; **approve / discard** (D-34)                | read                                | write                                                                        | write, **and confirm / dispute / rewrite** (D-33, D-34) | write (raises `unconfirmed`)                                                                                     |
| `selection_notes`                                               | read                                              | read                                | read                                                                         | **write**                                               | read                                                                                                             |
| `goals`, `criteria`                                             | write                                             | propose                             | read only                                                                    | read only                                               | read only                                                                                                        |
| `stories.phase`, `stories.status`, `*.state`                    | write — **Pause & edit is a status write** (D-37) | read                                | **read only**                                                                | **may recommend `done`**                                | read only                                                                                                        |
| `stories.summary_locked`, `stories.plotlines_locked`, backtrack | **write, alone**                                  | read                                | read only                                                                    | read only                                               | read only                                                                                                        |
| `templates`, `recipes`                                          | write                                             | —                                   | read only                                                                    | read only                                               | read only                                                                                                        |
| `directives`                                                    | write                                             | write                               | read                                                                         | read                                                    | read                                                                                                             |
| `proposals`                                                     | accept/reject                                     | read                                | write                                                                        | write                                                   | write                                                                                                            |

**T-1b** The last column covers two kinds with one shared limit: a **reader** and a **Critic** both read and report, and neither writes a story artifact. A Critic's extra right is exactly one — `proposeIdea` — and it is not a write (D-26, T-9). Web access is not a right at all: everyone has it (T-6b). What actually distinguishes the two is that a **Critic is expected to propose**, and a reader is not.
**T-1a** The columns are not a soft convention. Every tool is registered everywhere (T-6a), but the **server** enforces the row: a Critic's `editChapter`, an Editor's prose write, and any agent's write to a locked artifact are refused at the call, naming the rule and the artifact (T-1). Permission is enforced at commit time, not by hiding the tool — the refusal is a teaching message inside the run, and the run changes tactic.
**T-1c** **The Researcher's column** (§16.3): it reads the Seed, the Summary and every other story artifact, and it **writes `knowledge`** and may file a **`tasks`** row (D-11); it also holds the `proposals` write every kind holds (P9's propose-only direction) and `recommend` on `sub_agent_runs`, but it **reads** `verdicts`, `findings` and `ideas` because it does not judge prose. A Researcher write to a story artifact is refused naming P10, exactly like a Reader's or a Critic's. The three phase-① collections follow the same rule: `seed_briefs` is the **Editor's** write and read for every other kind; `knowledge` is the Researcher's; `sample_reviews` is **written by the engine** from the screening answer and read by every kind (§16.2, §16.5).

**T-0** Role kind on the template (`roles[].kind`) names the **employee** the role assigns, and selects the brief and the permission row — all kinds get the full tool catalogue (T-6a). **How many roles of each employee kind a Story has is the Author's choice** (D-38a): the loop never hard-codes one Writer or one Critic, and `max_concurrent` / `attempts` / `agents` are template settings, not engine constants. Every built-in employee is Rank A and may own a Work Item; a Rank B employee may be added only as a delegation target (V-2). The only hard requirements are structural: at least one Editor available once `attempts > 1` **or once any Critic or reader is assigned** — an Editor is not optional when there is more than one draft to choose between, and it is not optional when somebody else is filing findings the loop is about to act on (D-33) — and a unit with competing revisions waits for a selection (D-23).
**T-1** The tool layer enforces this table. A tool call that violates it is refused with a message naming the rule and the artifact, and the refusal is visible to the agent so it can change tactic rather than retry.
**T-2** Author-written spans are tracked at section granularity (D-7), so P9 is enforceable without freezing the whole chapter.

---

## 8. Tools

Tools are the only hands the crew has (P1, P3). **Every session is built with the whole catalogue** — §8.1 through §8.5 and §8.7's knowledge group, web tools included — and the server refuses the calls an employee kind may not make. What separates a Writer from a Critic from an Editor is therefore **its instructions and its permission row, not its tool list** (D-17a, T-1a). A crew that can see `editChapter` and is told not to use it behaves better than one that cannot see it and invents a workaround.

One execution mode is a **named exception to uniform registration**, and it is a subtraction at dispatch, never a permission: a run may be dispatched with `toolMode: 'none'` — a single assistant turn with an empty offered-tool list and a `response_format` contract. The catalogue stays uniform (T-6a); only that run's offered list is empty. Phase ①'s sampling and screening runs are the only users (§16.4).

Two obligations come with that, and they are build obligations, not prompt policy: the catalogue must **reach everything a brief can ask for** (T-12), and it must be **readable from inside the run** (T-6c). A Writer, a Critic, and an Editor all start from the same place — the whole surface, listed, describable, and complete enough that the job in front of them is always something they can actually do.

**T-3** A run is spawned with `storyId` bound into its tool context. No story tool accepts a `storyId` argument — the runtime supplies it — so an agent physically cannot read or write another Story's records. A run exists for exactly one Story for exactly one Work Item.

### 8.1 The Writer surface

Every session sees these tools (T-6a); the **Writer is the only role the server lets use them** on a story artifact. It writes the Summary, the plotlines, the chapters, and the prose — everything that becomes part of the book.

| tool             | args                                                                           | notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `editSummary`    | `<any prose field>, expects, why`                                              | **phase `summary` only, and only while `summary_locked` is false** (D-3). Refused with P5 named at any other time. It cannot touch `chapter_count` (D-3b). **Refused `R6` outright on a group-managed book** (D-61): the five prose fields are the install's mirror, and the hint names the `draft_groups` job and the per-group revise route. A `'sampling'` book keeps the tool as it was. |
| `createPlotline` | `heading, order, must_happen, must_change, pov, setups, payoffs, expects, why` | propose-mode once `plotlines_locked` (L-7).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `editPlotline`   | `plotline_id, <any field>, expects, why`                                       |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `createChapter`  | `plotline_id, title, summary, order, expects, why`                             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `editChapter`    | `chapter_id, section_id?, content?, title?, summary?, expects, why`            | a section edit is the default; a whole-chapter `rewrite` is a separate explicit flag (D-7).                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `deleteChapter`  | `chapter_id, expects, why`                                                     | only meaningful after an Author changes `chapter_count` (D-3c).                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `updateDigest`   | `body, expects, why`                                                           | **registered on every session, refused for every role** (D-12a): the Digest is derived by the engine from committed changes. It stays in the catalogue so a Writer that reaches for it is refused with the reason, instead of silently writing a summary nobody asked for. |
| `proposeChange`  | `target_ref, target_field, patch, why`                                         | the only path into an Author-written span (P9).                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

**T-3c** `editSummary` is the Writer's phase-① artifact write, and it is refused the moment `summary_locked` is true (D-3, L-30). Phase ① never calls it, in either of its two readings: on a **grouped** book the Writer runs are tool-less and the engine installs the accepted `GroupDraft` (§4.19, R5), and on a legacy one the sampler holds no tools and the engine persists its validated sample (§16.5). And the door is now closed from the inside as well as unused from the outside: on a group-managed book the tool answers **`R6`** for ANY caller, because the five fields are the mirror the install recomposes and a hand write there is a second write path the group model exists to remove (D-61, §17.9). A Writer that wants the Summary changed after the lock files a finding; on a grouped book, a Writer that wants it changed at all files a finding — with `group_refs` naming the groups it thinks are wrong — and the Author decides whether that is worth a backtrack (D-25).
**T-3a** The Writer MUST be able to run to completion without the Author: it keeps the task queue as its work list and keeps writing until the queue is empty or it must escalate (S-4). "Regardless of number of turns" is a property of the queue, not of a single run.

### 8.2 The Editor surface

The Editor chooses. It reads the competing revisions, applies the Author's `selection_rules` (D-17c), and commits to one — or asks the Writer to merge several. It never writes prose (L-19). Phase ①'s Editor does not choose a draft: the engine selects deterministically (§16.6), and the Editor's phase-① work is the Seed Brief, finding verification, Idea verification and directive review.

| tool               | args                                                        | notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `listRevisions`    | `unit_ref`                                                  | the competing drafts, side by side, with their verdicts (D-21).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `selectRevision`   | `unit_ref, revision_id, selection_note`                     | the server deletes the losers in the same commit (D-22). **Refused `R5` when `unit_ref` is the Summary of a group-managed book** (D-61): each group installs its own revision, so a hand-picked draft would install text the group rows do not hold — the hint is `raiseFinding` with `group_refs`, not another selection. Chapter and plotline selection are unchanged. |
| `requestMerge`     | `unit_ref, revision_ids[], parts, why`                      | hands the merge to a Writer; the Editor does not compose (L-19). On the Summary in phase ① it is refused `D-23` on both books, and the structured `fix` now BRANCHES by pipeline like the message always did: a grouped book is sent to `raiseFinding` + `group_refs`, a legacy book to `listRevisions` → `selectRevision` (§17.9). |
| `rejectAll`        | `unit_ref, why, re_brief`                                   | no draft is good enough; the brief or the contract is wrong (L-18).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `compareRevisions` | `revision_ids[], dimension`                                 | the differences on one axis — pacing, fidelity to `must_happen`, voice — as a report, not a judgment.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `verifyIdea`       | `idea_id, verdict, evidence, meets_guidelines, why`         | **the Editor's independent check of a Critic's claim** (D-27, C-15): re-read the cited source or find a second one, then `verified` / `refuted` / `unverifiable` — **and** whether the idea fits the Author's guidelines at all (D-31). The Author may not select an unverified sourced Idea until this exists.                                                                                         |
| `verifyFinding`    | `finding_id, verdict, cost, title?, detail?, evidence, why` | **confirm or dispute a Critic's or reader's suspicion** (D-33), and **rewrite it into the Author's reading surface** (D-34): one clear claim, the evidence line, and the cost it actually deserves — the Editor may downgrade a `critical` to a `minor` as part of confirming it. Only a confirmed finding can be approved, can spawn a task, can invalidate a chapter, or can count toward `pending`. |
| `approveFinding`   | `finding_id, decision, why`                                 | **the Author's gate** (D-34): approve → a task is created for the Writer; discard → the finding is closed with no work done. `auto` performs the same call when the Story's switch covers that cost (D-35). **Refused `D-33d` when the row is a `proposal` and the Story still has an unresolved issue**: the refusal names the open rows and the rule, and `decision: 'discard'` is never refused — declining a change is not an approval (§4.10). |
| `listIdeas`        | `phase?, state?, unit_ref?`                                 | what the Critics put forward, with their verification state.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `delegate`         | `role, task`                                                | fan out inside this run to fresh sub-contexts to reach a conclusion (D-17e). Distinct from a template's declared `agents: N` (D-38): delegation is ad-hoc reasoning the run chooses to do; `agents` is the shape the Author gave the role. Both stay inside one run and produce one answer.                                                                                                             |

**T-3b** The Editor verifies Ideas and selects revisions; it does not judge them into the story. An Editor that thinks an Idea is good and writes it in has broken P10 twice — once by writing, once by deciding without the Author (D-29).

**T-2a** `selection_note` is required on every selection, and it must name the rule applied and what the Editor preferred in each draft (L-17). It is the only window the Author has into the automated taste, and it is what makes P12 tunable.
**T-2b** Delegation stays inside one run and one Story: a delegated sub-context is not a Work Item, does not appear in the crew panel as a dispatched run, and cannot write anything the parent could not.

### 8.3 The reader surface

The role kind `reader`, and `critic` apart from what §8.5 adds. Every session holds these tools; what differs is what the server **accepts** from this role: a reader's or Critic's `create*`/`edit*` call on a story artifact is refused naming P10, and their accepted output is verdicts, findings, and tasks. Phase ①'s screening Critic is this surface **minus every tool**: the run is `toolMode: 'none'` and answers with a structured `IssueList` instead (§16.4, §16.5).

| tool              | args                                                                     | notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `submitVerdict`   | `target_ref, criteria_passed[], recommendation`                          | the run's report; must satisfy D-15.                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `raiseFinding`    | `target_ref, criterion_ref, title, detail, evidence, cost, group_refs?`               | `cost` is a **required enum** — the reviewer must triage it (T-11). Deduped by fingerprint (D-16). On a grouped book a phase-① finding on the Summary MUST carry `group_refs` — the `phrase_groups.id` UUIDs it is about — and an unknown id or an omitted list is refused naming **R3** (§4.10, §17.7); the structured `fix` branches by book, because only a legacy Story may omit the list at all (D-61). Refused **D-33b** on a kind/cost pair the ladder forbids (`taste` issue, `critical` proposal). Refused **D-33c** in two directions: a `kind: proposal` call while the Story has an unresolved issue, and an `issue` call in a pass whose run already filed a proposal (§4.10). Lands `unconfirmed`, then waits for an Editor's `verifyFinding` (D-33) and the Author's approval (D-34) before any Writer touches it. |
| `createTask`      | `title, detail, owner_role, criterion_ref?, finding_ref?, priority, why` | the fix a reader wants, handed to the Writer (D-11).                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `editTask`        | `task_id, state?, detail?, expects, why`                                 | close what you saw closed.                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `requestAuthor`   | `question, options[], why`                                               | escalation (P7). Creates the queue entry.                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `markChapterDone` | `chapter_id`                                                             | a _recommendation_; only the Author finalises (L-10).                                                                                                                                                                                    |

### 8.4 Shared plumbing

Present on every session — Writer, Editor, Critic, reader and Researcher alike. The `edit*` variants are **permitted to the Writer only**: the tool is registered everywhere, and the server refuses anyone else's call (T-1a).

| tool                            | args                               | notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `readSeed`                      | —                                  | the Author's idea, **read-only for every agent at every phase** (P5, D-3b).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `readSummary`                   | —                                  | read-only for every agent once `summary_locked`; writable by the Writer during phase `summary` (D-3).                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `readIdeas`                     | `phase?, state?, unit_ref?`        | what the Critics put forward and how the Editor verified it (D-27) — **including the ones already selected, discarded, or deferred**, which every Critic and Editor brief must carry (D-32).                                                                                                                |
| `readPlotline`                  | `plotline_id` \| `order` \| `list` | one plotline, or the whole outline in order — the contract the Writer must satisfy (P9).                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `readChapter`                   | `chapter_id, sections?`            | returns Digest context headers so the agent knows what it is standing on.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `readCast` / `editCast`         |                                    | `editCast` is Writer-only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `readLedger` / `editLedger`     |                                    | `editLedger` is Writer-only; ledger writes are what make C-5/C-6 checkable.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `readDigest`                    | —                                  | the standing view (D-13).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `readGoals`                     | —                                  | the criteria list and threshold (G-4).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `listTasks`                     | `state?, owner_role?`              | the queue is agent-writable (D-11); writes go through §8.1/§8.2.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `readVerdicts` / `readFindings` | `target_ref?`                      | what the last passes concluded, so a fresh run does not re-litigate a settled point.                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `listTools`                     | `?kind?`                           | **the catalogue this session holds**, by name and one-line purpose (T-6c). Present on every session: what a role can reach is answerable from inside the run, not only from the harness config.                                                                                                             |
| `describeTool`                  | `name`                             | the exact shape of one tool — required fields, enum values, what `expects` to carry (T-6c). Cheaper than guessing and being refused (T-11).                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `reportGap`                     | `job, needed, tried, why`          | **the capability-gap record** (T-12): "this brief asks for something no tool reaches." Not a write to any artifact; it lands on the Author's queue as a tool or template request, and it is what turns an improvisation into a visible defect. Distinct from `requestAuthor`, which asks about the _story_. |

**T-4** Every mutating tool takes `expects: [{id, rev}]` compare-and-swap. A rejected write returns the current record so the agent re-reads and re-applies instead of forcing. Concurrent crews are expected, not exceptional.
**T-5** Every mutating tool takes `why` and records it (P10).
**T-6** Tools MUST be registered on the harness at session build (host-side `registerTool`/`addTool`), declared `core` so a fresh thread sees them on turn one with no discovery round trip — a fresh thread every turn (P2) cannot afford a discovery turn.
**T-6a** Registration is **uniform**: every session, whatever its `kind`, gets §8.1 + §8.2 + §8.3 + §8.4 + §8.5 + §8.7. `kind` selects the **brief and the permission row**, not the tool list (D-17a). One execution mode subtracts the list at dispatch — `toolMode: 'none'`, phase ①'s `draft_groups`/`review_groups` passes (§17.2, S-8) and, on a legacy book, its samples and screens (§16.4) — and it is a mode, not a permission. This is deliberate: a fresh context every turn (P2) that cannot see a tool does not abstain, it improvises — and an improvised workaround is invisible to the Author, while a refused call is a record.
**T-6c** **The catalogue is self-describing inside the run.** Every session carries `listTools` and `describeTool`. Presence is free — the names are already visible at turn one (T-6) — so discovery is never "does this tool exist"; it is "what exactly does it take", answerable in one call instead of by guessing and being refused (T-11). This is not the discovery round trip T-6 forbids: presence costs nothing, only detail is on demand.
**T-12** **The catalogue must cover the brief.** No Work Item is dispatched whose job has no reachable tool: before a template may ask for an action, §8.1–§8.5 and §8.7 must contain a call that performs it. When a run finds its brief asking for something the surface cannot do, it calls `reportGap` — naming the job, what it needed, and what it did instead — and the Story is not penalised in loop detection for the shortfall. A gap is a defect in the **build**, not in the crew. The alternative is the failure T-6a already describes: the agent improvises, the workaround looks like a choice, and the Author never learns a tool was missing.
**T-13** **Capabilities are registered tools, not prompt prose.** Everything §8 names is a host-side registered tool with a schema the server validates (T-6, T-11), present in every session (T-6a). Nothing in the story surface may be "the agent should know how" or "the instructions tell it to": a rule with no tool behind it is a rule a fresh context will eventually violate, and P2 guarantees a fresh context every turn. The Author's assistant (§8.6) and the `toolMode: 'none'` execution mode (§16.4) are the only exceptions to uniform registration, and both are subtraction.
**T-6b** **Web tools are available to every role**, and what governs their use is the template's instructions (D-30). A Critic is told to research in order to form and check ideas; a Writer may look up whether a revolver in 1880 could actually fire six times; a reader may confirm a period fact before it files the finding. Cost is no object (E-0), so the constraint is not _who may browse_ but **what counts as evidence**: a claim that reaches a locked artifact needs an Editor's verdict (C-15), whoever fetched it.

### 8.5 Research and proposal tools

Available to **every** role (T-6b); governed by the role's instructions (D-30). A Critic is the role these tools were written for — it reads the story as the Author reads it, rates it, and looks things up in order to form and check its ideas — but a Writer may verify a period fact before it writes one, and a reader may confirm before it files.

| tool               | args                                           | notes                                                                                                                |
| ------------------ | ---------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `searchWeb`        | `query, maxResults?`                           | leads, not facts. Results are untrusted excerpts; the Critic cites and the Editor checks (D-27).                     |
| `fetchPage`        | `url, query?`                                  | read one source properly before citing it. A search snippet is not evidence.                                         |
| `proposeIdea`      | `phase, unit_ref?, body, kind, sources[], why` | a Critic's only contribution mechanism (D-26). Never an edit. Deduped by `fingerprint` against decided Ideas (D-32). |
| `markUnverifiable` | `idea_id, why`                                 | "cannot be checked from here" is an honest verdict, not a shrug; the Idea reaches the Author marked unverified.      |

**T-9** A Critic MUST NOT write a story artifact — it _can_ call `editChapter`, and the server will refuse it (T-1a). Its accepted output is `proposeIdea`, `raiseFinding`, and `submitVerdict` (D-26, P10). A Critic that rewrites chapter 4 has destroyed the independence the phase model buys it, and the refusal is what stops it from doing so quietly.
**D-30** A Critic role's instructions name **what it reads for and what it may propose**: rate the chapter as the Author reads it, flag anything that diverges from the plotline, anything that references something that does not hold up, anything that spends a mystery before it is earned — and propose freely, including ideas that are wrong. The **Editor** is what keeps those proposals inside the Author's guidelines (D-31), not a limit on what a Critic is allowed to think about.
**T-11** **The tool validates the request body, and the agent sees the error.** `cost` is a required enum on `raiseFinding`, so a Critic cannot file an untriaged finding; `evidence` is required on a failing criterion (D-15); `selection_note` is required on a selection (T-2a). A malformed call is refused naming the field and the expected shape, and the run corrects it in place — schema enforcement belongs in the tool, not in the prompt, because a prompt is something a fresh context can be tired of. **The same doctrine covers what a call is ALLOWED to do, not only how it is shaped:** the kind/cost ladder (D-33b), the one-kind-per-pass rule and the issues-before-proposals filing gate (D-33c), and the approval gate that keeps a Proposal from being approved over an open issue (D-33d) are all enforced on the call, and the prompt carries only the explanation of a refusal the agent would otherwise hit blind.
**T-10** Every `sources[]` entry records `url_or_title`, the specific `claim` it supports, and `accessed_at`. An Idea citing a page for a claim that page does not make is `refuted` by the Editor, not "mostly right" (C-15).
**T-7** Tool results are bounded and structured; a tool that hands a whole book to a reviewer breaks the reading economy (§11) and is a defect, not a convenience.
**T-8** Every tool result names the record revision it was read at, so a follow-up call in the same run can send a valid `expects` without re-reading.

### 8.6 Assistant tools

The Author's assistant (invoked by the Author, on a Summary or a Directive) gets the Author's write rights for the duration of that one run, and nothing else. It does not get the Critic's web tools, and it never writes a locked artifact.

### 8.7 The knowledge surface (phase ①)

Registered on **every** session like the rest of the catalogue (T-6a); §7's permission rows and T-1c decide who may call what. The group is its own section because it is the research pipeline's surface rather than a widening of §8.1–§8.5.

| tool              | kind       | args                                                                                                                                     | writes        | notes                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ----------------- | ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `structureSeed`   | editor     | `seed_hash, logline, protagonist, want, need?, obstacle, stakes, setting, world_rules[], promise, tone_hints?, constraints[], open_questions[], research_questions[], expects?, why` | `seed_briefs` | Refuses a `seed_hash` that is not the current Seed's, naming P5. One accepted brief per Story: a repeat returns the existing row, a new hash supersedes the old in one commit. `research_questions` is REQUIRED (T-11 when omitted) and `[]` waives the research leg — D-51, §16.9.1. A blank entry is a T-11 refusal naming the index and `D-51`, never a whitespace clean that turns `['']` into a waiver nobody stated; trimming and dedup still apply to real entries. The idempotent repeat echoes the STORED decision (`research_questions: null` when the row states none, so a legacy row is never read as a waiver) rather than the call's args. |
| `writeKnowledge`  | researcher | `topic, kind, claim, detail?, importance, sources[], phase?, why`                                                                        | `knowledge`   | Deduped by `fingerprint` (D-16): a repeat bumps `seen_count`, merges the sources and takes the new importance. At least one T-10 source is required.                      |
| `readKnowledge`   | every kind | `importance?, kind?, phase?, topic?`                                                                                                     | —             | Bounded to 40 rows (T-7), ordered `critical` → `low`, newest first within a level. `truncated` says a filter is needed.                                                   |
| `retireKnowledge` | researcher | `knowledge_id, expects, why`                                                                                                             | `knowledge`   | Stale facts are marked, not deleted: importance becomes `low` and the `detail` carries the `(retired)` mark (P11). Retiring an already-`low` row is refused.              |
| `reportNoResearch`| researcher | `rationale`                                                                                                                              | — (zero writes) | D-51, §16.9.1. Closes a dispatched research pass that found nothing to record: no Knowledge row, no task, no invented fact (P7). It is a READ tool (a panel agent answers it, D-38) and a REPORT tool, so the run ends with `no_op === false` instead of a counted silent no-op (S-4). The pass's questions came from the brief's `research_questions`; the `rationale` is kept in the run log. |

`structureSeed` and `writeKnowledge` carry `why` and record it (T-5); `retireKnowledge` is the one read-modify-write and therefore requires `expects` (T-4). The no-tool runs of §16.5 call none of these: they call nothing at all, and the engine's own `why` (the loop's attribution, with no `run_ref`) is what records the answer it persists.

### 8.8 The Plotlines surface — `structurePlotlines`, and the two refusals that guard the lane (2026-11-26, R11)

Like §8.7, this post-dates the pinned §8.1–§8.6 tables and is therefore recorded as its own catalogue group: `tools/registry.ts` registers `structurePlotlines` under the group `plotlines` rather than folding it into `editor`, for the same reason the knowledge group exists — the §8 tables are the first build's frozen record, and a new surface gets its own row rather than an edit to a pinned table. `tools/catalogue.test.ts` pins the catalogue at **53 tools** (52 through the research surface plus `reportNoResearch`, and this as the 53rd), with no shadowing.

| tool | kind | write | the contract |
| -- | -- | -- | -- |
| `structurePlotlines` | Editor only (`kinds`/`onlyKinds: ['editor']`) | `plotline_briefs` | Phrase ②'s chapter decision over the **phrase's own copied seed**, and its acceptance, in ONE commit. Required args `seed_hash`, `chapter_count`, `why` (T-5); optional `chapters` (`{order, seed}` pairs), `research_questions`, `notes`. It computes the current hash from `phraseSeedOf(phrase, story)` and COMPARES the Editor's `seed_hash` rather than trusting it (§4.20). Idempotent the D-16 way: an accepted brief for `(phrase, seed_hash)` is returned as a `repeat`, never doubled. |

**Its four refusals, in the order they are asked** (T10/F9's rule, the same ordering `structureSeed` follows: every value is validated BEFORE the idempotence read, so a malformed call can never be absorbed by a `repeat` of the row already on the record):

1. **`T-11` — there is no Phrase ② to structure.** `starts_on_demand` means the row does not exist until the Author hands the phrase text, and a chapter count over no seed is a number with no book under it; the `fix` names the actor, because an Editor that invents a seed is the failure mode this guards ("the Author starts the Plotlines Phrase… before the Editor can structure it").
2. **`L-7` — the Plotlines contract is locked.** A new chapter set under `plotlines_locked` would silently retire contracts the Author signed; the `fix` is unlock-then-re-structure.
3. **`P5`, one phrase down — the seed moved.** `seed_hash` is verified against the phrase's seed, never assumed, and the refusal prints `received` and `expected` so the run can re-derive. This gate is what makes the two phrases discrete: editing `stories.idea` moves Phrase ①'s hashes and leaves this one untouched.
4. **`T-11` — the chapter set and its seeds disagree, or the count is not a positive whole number.** A `chapters` entry above `chapter_count` is refused (the set and its notes cannot describe different books); a ragged entry *inside* the count is DROPPED, because materialisation counts the brief's `chapter_count` and never reads the list.

Then, in the same commit, every previously `accepted` brief for **this phrase** is moved back to `draft` — two accepted rows would make "the chapter set" ambiguous for every gate that reads it — and the new brief is created and accepted (§4.20's three writes). A book that restarted Phrase ② keeps the retired briefs of the phrase it replaced as audit history, scoped by `phrase_id`.

**The two refusals that keep the classic tools off a live lane.** Both key on `phrase/plotpoints.ts:plotlineLaneIsLive` — the dial AND a started phrase row — and the "AND" is load-bearing, measured rather than reasoned: every Story the engine creates is born `plotlines_mode: 'groups'`, so a guard that read the dial alone would refuse §8.1's contract tools on books that have no Phrase ② at all.

| tool | refusal | the rule |
| -- | -- | -- |
| `createPlotline`, `editPlotline` | `R6` (`tools/writer.ts:guardPlotlinePhraseOwnership`) | while the lane is live, the chapter's contract IS its `chapter_<order>` entry and the §4.3 row is its mirror: a write naming an `order` an entry covers is refused with the entry's id and key, and the `fix` names that entry's own revise route (`POST /stories/:id/phrases/groups/<entry>/revise`) or the grouped `draft_groups` job. An `order` NO entry covers — the gap left when the Author shrinks `chapter_count` — stays classic-writable rather than being refused into a dead end. |
| `raiseFinding` | `R3` (`tools/reader.ts`, `phrase/findings.ts:findingGroupNamespace`) | a `plotlines` finding on a live lane is filed AGAINST the chapter entry that covers the finding's order, so the fix routes to one entry instead of N (R3's cost argument, unchanged, in a new namespace); when no entry covers that order the tool refuses rather than filing a claim nothing can answer. |

---

## 9. Run lifecycle

```
sweep → Story is `ready`? → WorkItem claimed (lease)
      → spawn: open one fresh thread on the employee kind the role names;
               storyId bound, brief assembled
      → run:  the employee acts as an agent through tools, no chat
              (an Editor may delegate to fresh sub-contexts inside this run, D-17e;
               a `toolMode: 'none'` run makes one provider call instead — §16.5)
      → report: submitVerdict / raiseFinding / createTask / requestAuthor / proposeIdea
                selectRevision / requestMerge / rejectAll / verifyIdea
                (a `toolMode: 'none'` run's report is its validated structured
                 answer, which the engine persists — §16.5)
      → commit: server applies, stamps, deletes non-selected revisions (D-22),
                invalidates dependents, rebuilds digest
      → teardown: thread destroyed, run row closed, the employee returns to idle
      → findings: confirmed by an Editor (D-33) → rewritten for the Author (D-34)
                → approved by the Author, or by the switch if the cost is covered (D-35)
                → task → dispatched to a Writer on a later sweep
      → recompute the phase's level and trend
            → phase Goal passed?  → `pending` · `phase_signoff`: STOP, wait for the lock (L-29)
            → Ideas or confirmed findings awaiting the Author, and nothing else to dispatch?
              → `pending` · `idea_selection` / `finding_approval` (D-29, D-34, L-36)
            → drained? (no task, no unconfirmed finding, one clean round at
              or above the dial threshold) → `pending` · `drained` (L-37)
            → else trend flat or regressing? → `pending` · `stalled` (§6.8, L-37a)
      → next sweep
```

**S-1** One Work Item = one Run = one fresh thread. A thread is never handed a second job, and no thread is ever reused. The employee is the standing identity; the thread is the agent execution it causes; the phase's phrase session outlives both.
**S-2** The brief is assembled by the server from the store: `role prompt + phase + Goal criteria for that phase + the Seed + Digest + the artifact under review + the specific job + what changed since the last pass + the open tasks that job must clear + the selected Ideas this run must absorb (D-29)`. A Critic's brief adds the research question and the sources already cited, so two Critics do not browse to the same page. It MUST be self-contained; a thread has no memory of any prior thread. A `toolMode: 'none'` run is the named exception: it is given the **compiled prompt** instead of the assembled brief — the Seed Brief, the `critical`/`high` Knowledge, and (from round 2) the base revision and the open fix tasks (§16.5) — because its whole job is one structured answer, not an action on the book.
**S-3** Teardown is unconditional — including on failure, timeout, and Author stop. No orphan threads. The run row records why it ended.
**S-4** A run that ends without calling `submitVerdict`, `raiseFinding`, `createTask`, `requestAuthor`, `proposeIdea`, `verifyIdea`, `selectRevision`, or `requestMerge` is a **no-op turn**: it MUST count toward loop detection (§6.7 / L-15). A Writer run that ends without writing anything and without escalating is a bug in its brief, not a cost.
**S-4a** There is no timeout on a run and no retry cap. A run that takes four hours is fine. A run that hangs is detected by **lease expiry** (L-2), not by a clock: the lease lapses, the Work Item returns to the queue, and the run is torn down.
**S-5** Concurrency: `max_concurrent` per template, per Story, and a global cap. The sweep fills capacity across all Stories, so the crew stays busy whenever any Story has work (the Author's "constantly busy" requirement).
**S-6** A run's wall-clock and token usage are recorded and surfaced per Story, per chapter, per template — **informationally only**. Nothing may read them as a limit (E-0). They answer "why is this taking an hour?", not "can we afford this?"
**S-7** A delegated sub-context (D-17e) is not a Work Item and gets no row of its own in the crew panel; it is shown nested under the run that spawned it, because the Author's unit of attention and of control is the dispatched run.
**S-8** The grouped Summary's two no-tool job kinds (§17). `draft_groups` (Writer) answers `GroupDraft` — one `{group_ref, content}` per group in its batch — and `review_groups` (Critic) answers `GroupReview`, every issue naming the groups it crosses. Both run with `toolMode: 'none'` (§16.4), so their report is the validated structured answer and the engine does the write. What the engine does with a `draft_groups` answer is the redesign's whole swap: **install, archive, recompose, mirror — in the commit that closes the run** (§4.19, R2/R5/R6). There is no selection step, no `selectRevision`, no `requestMerge` and no "reject all and re-brief" for a grouped Summary (S-4's report verbs simply never appear on such a run), and an answer that fails the schema is refused for the WHOLE batch (`phrase/revisions.ts:parseGroupDraftAnswer`): half an Overview is not an Overview.
**S-9** The grouped Summary's bounds, and why they are not caps in E-0's sense (R4, R5). A Writer batch is `max(1, stories.summary_sampling)` groups; a group whose text is current gets **two** rewrite attempts before the loop stops paying for it (`GROUP_WRITE_ATTEMPT_CAP`, counted over closed HOLDING runs against that group's `latest` revision — never over a live run, never over a reading seat); a group an unresolvable defect names stays open in the review rail with its attempts visible, which is the escalation. None of these bounds a phase transition or halts a loop: they stop ONE group from being rewritten forever while the rest of the Summary keeps working, and the Author's own knobs (`summary_sampling`, `summary_critics`, the dial) still set how much is asked for.

---

## 10. The Dashboard

### 10.1 Layout

Four regions. The right region is the **employee roster and the crew**, not an agent list — the Author controls the harnesses and watches the threads they are running on the selected Story. A tile names a standing employee; a live row names a thread that employee is currently executing as an agent.

```
┌────────────────┬────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────┐
│ STORIES        │ THE STORY                  │ EMPLOYEES / CREW    │
│ phase-first,   │ ① Summary ② Plotlines      │ standing roster +   │
│ then status:   │ ③ Chapters · Cast · Ledger │ live threads for    │
│ awaiting →     │ Revisions · IDEAS tray     │ this story          │
│ ready → done   │ [ LOCK PHASE ] [BACKTRACK] │ ──────────────────  │
│                │                            │ TEMPLATES           │
│                │                            │ new · duplicate ·   │
│                │                            │ prompts · rules     │
├────────────────┴────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────┤
│ DIRECTIVE BAR — "kill the mentor in ch4" · "make it colder"       │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

The directive row is the **content region's footer**, not a window-wide strip: the Author's one
channel belongs to the Story (or the desk), and it scrolls with neither. On a phone it is the
minimal composer — one field and a send control docked to the viewport, with no label row or hint.

**Update 2026-11-24 (the Summary Phrase, §4.19/§17).** The centre column's phrase list is now ONE
phrase plus its groups, not three phrases: the rail is `Summary`, and under it the five group rows —
**Premise · Promise · Theme · Tone · Ending** — each badged `to write` (no revision yet), with a
Writer mark while a live pass is replacing its text, and with the count of open blocking findings.
Cast, Ledger, Revisions, Ideas and the rest are supporting folders and leaves under the one phrase,
not sibling phrases; Characters, Plotlines and Chapters remain panes reachable by direct route
(`ALL_STORY_PHRASES`), which is why the diagram still names them. The phase strip (U-12a) is
unchanged — it is the pipeline position, not the rail — and the rail's badges come from the same
`GET …/phrases` read as the panes, so a badge can never contradict the pane it points at.

**Update 2026-11-26 (the per-phrase Seed, U-25 / §18.13).** The rail numbers the pipeline's phrases — **Summary ① · Plotlines ②** are its rows (`STORY_PHRASES`), and **Chapters ③** carries that number in `ALL_STORY_PHRASES` and in its pane title rather than on the rail — with Characters listed outside the numbering as a supplement (U-20, §18.8) — and **every phrase header carries its own `Seed` child, as its first row**: the single story-level Seed leaf the rail used to offer is gone, and it folds with its header (U-21 as amended). Nothing about the TEXT moved: Phrase ①'s Seed is still `stories.idea` and a started Phrase ②'s Seed is still its own `phrases.seed` copy, previewed as `seed_head`/`seed_chars` (§4.20, §18.8, D-72).

### 10.2 Requirements

**U-1 Stories panel.** Every Story shows its **phase** (① Summary · ② Plotlines · ③ Chapters, with a lock icon on each phase it has signed), its **`status` badge** (`ready` / `pending` / `completed` / `stopped`), its consistency levels for the phases it has reached (`idea→summary 2/2 · summary→plotlines 6/6 · plotlines→chapters n/a`), its `trend` arrow, and `status_reason` when pending. **Stories waiting on the Author sort to the top** — they are the ones only the Author can move.
**U-1b** A `pending` Story is **two different colours**: awaiting the Author — signoff, idea pick, finding approval, or **drained** — is calm (the loop finished something, ran out of authorised work, or is waiting to be told), stalled is urgent (the loop hit a wall). L-34, L-36, L-37a. Awaiting-signoff is the state the whole product aims at, and it is the one the Author should be able to clear in one click.
**U-1c** **Drained reads as a finished thing, not an idle one**: the row says what the crew stopped finding ("no critical or major left · 12 minor and 4 taste awaiting you") and offers the two moves L-37 allows — **pick up the leftovers** or **complete**. The word "drained" should not appear on screen; it is the internal name for "we are done with what we were told to do."
**U-1a** The **stalled** screen is the Author's review surface, not a log: it shows the stuck criteria, the oldest open findings, the chapters involved, what the crews tried across rounds, and the four exits from L-23 as buttons.
**U-2** A Story with nothing blocked still shows what the crew is doing to it right now.
**U-3** Per-Story Stop / Resume, and one global Stop. Global Stop is the biggest button on the screen.
**U-4 The Story view.** **Amended 2026-11-26 (U-25, §10.2 / §18.13) — the Seed is no longer a story-level surface, and the card this sentence pinned was never mounted.** The sentence read: the **Seed** is "a pinned, read-only card at the top of every Story for every viewer — it never scrolls away". That card exists only as unmounted dead code — `components/SeedCard.tsx`, whose only importer is its own test — and the surface the Author actually used was the rail's Seed leaf plus the read-only Seed pane. The Seed now sits where the Author put it: **under its phrase, as that phrase's first child row** (U-25), ①'s row being the Author's idea and ②'s row being that phrase's own copy (D-72, §4.20). **What does NOT move is the reason the sentence existed:** the Seed stays read-only for every viewer — and for every employee, therefore every agent, forever (P5) — and it stays the thing the book is measured against (C-14). The Summary pane is editable by the Author until the lock; on a grouped Story the Author's hand goes on a **group**, one at a time (U-18) — phase ①'s prose is never typed into the pane, because the engine installs what it accepts (§4.19 on a grouped book, §16.6 on a legacy one) — and once locked it is read-only with an "Ask assistant" affordance and a **Backtrack** button that says exactly what it will delete (L-32). Plotlines and chapters are shown side by side — the contract next to the prose — because the whole product is the distance between them.
**U-5** Chapter prose shows inline accept/reject per proposal, with the `why` and the run that made it, and undo. A diff, not a replaced paragraph.
**U-17 The prose is editable, and the pause is the price.** The chapter pane is a text editor, greyed out while the Story runs, with one button that makes it live: **Pause & edit** (D-37). It states what pausing costs — runs killed, dispatch halted — and **Resume** states what the edit will trigger: digest re-derived, N dependents marked for recheck (D-37a). While paused, the pane says so at the top ("paused by you · 3 runs stopped"), because a Story that looks idle and is actually being edited by hand is a different thing from one the crew has drained (U-1c).
