**U-6** Goal panel: the criteria list with live pass/fail per criterion, the failing evidence, the `fix_hint`, and the Author's controls (toggle hard, delete, add, threshold, per-chapter bar override).
**U-7 Crew panel.** Live threads: the employee kind (Writer / Editor / Critic / Reader / Researcher), template, what the thread is doing, which task it holds, elapsed, and a kill button per thread. A row is one thread, not the employee itself; the employee is idle when it has no row.
**U-8 Template panel.** The catalogue. Each card: roles naming their employee `kind` (Writer / Editor / Critic / Reader / Researcher) and whether a Critic role has `web`, phase it serves, done-definition, `attempts`, escalation rule. Actions: **New**, **Duplicate**, **Edit roles and prompts**, **Set agents per role** (D-38), **Set attempts**, **Assign to phase**, **Assign as a Writer of this Story** — repeatable, since the count is the Author's call (D-17b, D-38a). Built-ins duplicate-only (D-17). The employee roster above the catalogue is global; it lists every employee once, alphabetically (Critic · Editor · Reader · Researcher · Writer), with Writer given no priority — every Story's threads run as agents under it.
**U-8a** The Writer employee (and any template role of that kind) is visually distinct from the readers — it is the only one that produces prose, and the Author should never confuse the crew that writes with the crew that judges.
**U-8b** An Editor template's card shows its **`selection_rules` in the Author's own words**, editable in place, because that text _is_ the Author's automated taste (P12). Editing it and watching the next `selection_note` change is the core tuning loop of the product.
**U-8c** Each template card shows `attempts` — how many competing drafts it spawns — since that is the dial that turns divergence on and off.
**U-8d** Each role row shows `agents: N` and its `aggregate` mode, since "this Editor is three agents of that employee kind, arguing and returning one answer" (D-38) is invisible unless the card says so. When a run's agents disagreed, the crew panel marks that run: the dissent belongs in the `selection_note`, and the Author needs to know which authoritative answers were unanimous.

**U-9 Directive bar.** A single line of Author intent addressed to the Story's **Editor**, which becomes a `directives` record and propagates (D-19). Not a chat box: it does not accept conversation, it accepts decisions. It sits at the bottom of the content region; on a phone it is the harness's minimal composer — one field and a send control, docked to the viewport.
**U-10 The Author's queue.** Confirmed findings awaiting approval, Ideas awaiting a pick, and escalations, aggregated across all Stories, are the home screen. "3 stories need you." This is the loop-engineering aimed at the human (D-34).
**U-10a** Each finding in the queue reads as the **Editor wrote it**, not as the reviewer wrote it: the claim, the evidence line, the cost, and two buttons — approve, discard (D-34). The Story view shows its **auto-approve dial** beside Stop, with the costs it currently skips (D-35), because the Author should never be surprised later by work the machine started on their behalf.
**U-10b** Approving is progressive and never batched away silently: the Author may approve one finding, approve a whole chapter's worth, or turn on auto-approve for a cost and stop touching that class of problem. The queue drains at whatever pace the Author sets, and the loop keeps finding more underneath them (L-25b).
**U-11 Nothing on screen is agent activity for its own sake.** No "loop running ✓" spinner. Progress reads as story: chapters drafted, hole in act 2, setups unpaid, arcs unresolved.
**U-12** The Dashboard is usable with zero harnesses running: the Author can write the Seed, a Summary, plotlines, or chapter prose by hand at any time, and the crew simply picks it up on the next sweep. With harnesses running, chapter prose still needs the pause first (U-17, D-37) — the rule is about racing, not about permission.
**U-12a** The **phase strip** — ① Summary · ② Plotlines · ③ Chapters, each with a lock icon when signed — is the first thing on the Story view and the second thing on each Stories-panel row. The Author should be able to tell where any Story is in the pipeline from across the room.
**U-13 Revision comparison.** When a unit has competing revisions, the Story view shows them **side by side** with the readers' verdicts and the Editor's `selection_note`. The Author can select, merge, or restore any of them before the Editor commits (D-24), and can watch the losers disappear on selection (D-22). **This requirement is about plotlines, chapters, and a legacy Summary.** A grouped Summary has no competing set to lay side by side: its comparison surface is per group — the current text beside the COUNT of its superseded revisions (§4.19) — and a group's history is read, never deleted (R2). The Select/Merge controls therefore do not appear on a grouped Summary, and the routes refuse those verbs there rather than pretending (R5).
**U-15 The Ideas tray.** Every `proposed` Idea for the current phase, in one place, **above a collapsed list of what has already been decided** — selected, discarded, deferred, each with its `selection_note` (D-32). The tray is **phase-scoped and empties at the lock** (D-36); it is a workspace, not an archive. The decided list is not decoration: it is the only reason the next fresh Critic does not propose the same thing again, and the Author should see the crew re-raising a settled point as the repeat badge it is. who filed it, what it claims, the sources it cites, and the Editor's verdict with evidence (`verified` · `refuted` · `unverifiable`). The Author **selects** or **discards** each one; a selected Idea becomes the next Writer's brief (D-29). Unverified Ideas are visibly dimmed and cannot be selected without an explicit "I know" (D-27). The tray shows a count, and the phase cannot be locked while it is non-empty (L-7a).
**U-16 Lock and backtrack are the two biggest buttons on the Story view.** **Lock phase N** appears only when that phase's Goal passes and the Ideas tray is empty; it states in one line what becomes read-only. **Backtrack to phase N** states in one line what gets deleted, and asks once (L-31, L-32). There is no other way to change a locked artifact, and the UI should say so on every locked pane. **Phase ①'s button is named for the act, not for the flag: `Accept final Summary → Phrase 2`**, and since 2026-11-26 (D-78) its one line states what the CLICK does, not only what becomes read-only — the tray goes (D-36), the Summary phrase's own claims are voided (`disputed` included — `phrase/locks.ts:VOIDABLE_FINDING_STATES`, mirrored by `story-contract.ts:ACCEPT_VOIDED_FINDING_STATES`, so this figure equals `voided_findings` rather than the review band's number) and the queue's `open`/`claimed` tasks closed as `wontfix` — `loop/types.ts:OPEN_TASK_STATES`, exactly the count the receipt reports as `wontfixed_tasks` — each counted, with the `blocked` rows the sweep does not touch named in their own `leaves N blocked task(s) for you` clause, and Phrase ② opens from the compiled Summary. Every count clause in that line appears only when the read actually carries it (`story-contract.ts:lockCheck` → `:summaryAcceptLeftovers` → `:summaryAcceptStatement`), because a line that names a leftover this book does not have is the same lie as a blocker that is not one. The enable rule adds exactly one wait to the two above, and it is the desk's, not the route's: a clean Summary has to EXIST to be accepted (U-19).
**U-14 Completion is a proposal.** A `completed` Story shows what made it complete — the passing levels, the ledger state — **and exactly what accepting will discard**: "47 findings and 12 Ideas will be closed without action, the oldest from chapter 2" (L-25b). The Author accepts with their eyes open, and the count is kept as a record afterwards. A green tick the machine gave itself is not information; neither is a to-do list quietly emptied.

**U-18 The Summary workspace is the five groups, not one text (2026-11-24, R2/R4/R6).** The Summary pane is the composed **Overview** plus one pane per group. Each group pane states, from the `GET …/phrases` read alone: the group's own **revision number** (the number the rail stopped printing — a group's number belongs in its own pane, never in the rail), its text, what it still owes (the open findings that name it and have no revision of ITS resolving them, R3), and its superseded count. The pane's one write is **Revise this group**: `POST …/phrases/groups/:groupId/revise` with the new text, which installs a revision and archives the one it replaces exactly as a Writer install does (R2). While a Writer pass holds the group the control is **disabled**, and the pane says why in the same sentence the route's 409 uses — the button and the refusal read the same `writer_busy` fact, so neither can be stale about the other (R4/F8). No Select, no Merge, no whole-Summary "ask for another round", and no sampling dial on this surface: those belong to a legacy book's pane, and their routes answer 409 here naming the verb that works. The pane shows the latest text as a **word diff against the revision it replaced** — each changed run has its removed words struck through in red together and its added words green together, while unchanged words stay plain, and only the immediately previous revision is on the wire for it — plus the **count** of superseded revisions; the wire carries `chars`, not the older archived prose, and a row the reader cannot open is decoration (U3).
**U-19 The sign-off gate tells the truth about a grouped Summary (R1/R3/R4).** The desk MUST NOT present a Summary as ready while the phrase read says otherwise: while the phrase read has not landed (in flight or failed — an absent read is not an empty valid Phrase), while any group is unwritten, while **there is no clean Summary to accept**, or while a live Writer is rewriting a group. Each blocker is phrased as the wait it is, with the group's title, and the list is derived from the same rows the engine acts on (`story-contract.ts:groupSignOffBlockers`). **THE TWO WAITERS THAT ARE GONE (2026-11-26, T12/T13): an open blocking-cost finding on any group, and an open task in the queue (the sentence that used to name it called it the Story's task, because the read's task rows carry no group attribution).** Neither was ever the route's blocker, and the accept now clears both itself — the phrase's open findings are voided and its open tasks closed as `wontfix` in the lock commit (D-78) — so holding the button shut over them asked the Author to decide work the click decides (L-36: "a pending _decision_, not a blocked _Story_"). They are COUNTED, not gated: `story-contract.ts:summaryAcceptLeftovers` feeds the confirm line, which is L-25b's "the count is recorded and shown", and the old sentence's scope claim — leftovers "on this Summary or anywhere in the Story" — is gone with them, because those rows support no scope in either direction. **THE WAITER THAT REPLACED THEM,** verbatim from `:cleanSummaryGateReason`: `no clean Summary to accept yet — <Title>: <reviewedGapReason>; …`, one clause per group the reviewed Overview is missing, and the state that satisfies it is the server's own token — the reviewed selection is `ready` (a complete clean selection) or `chosen` (`:cleanSummaryExists`) — never a re-derivation from the group rows the desk happens to be holding. What the desk may NOT do is invent a gate the server ignores: the HTTP lock stays exactly §6.3a/§10.2/U-16's gate (the Ideas tray, then the phase Goal), because a blocker the UI claims and the route denies is a button that lies — and removing the two blockers moved this desk CLOSER to that route, not further from it, because the route never refused on either one. The one group truth the desk does NOT print as a sentence is the owed reading: the Critic seats and deep passes the CURRENT vector still owes are the engine's hold, not a UI blocker — the sweep withholds the `phase_signoff` offer over them (`status.ts:decideStory` → `phrase/exclusion.ts:groupPipelineOwed`, D-62), so the Story simply stays `ready` and the drain line names the passes. A rail blocker for that would be a second, looser copy of a rule the server already enforces. **AND THE RAIL TICK IS THE GATE, NOT A SECOND OPINION OF IT (2026-11-26, T13):** for a grouped Summary `story-nav.ts:summaryReadyForReview` calls `story-contract.ts:lockCheck` and decides for itself only the two facts that belong to a rail — no tick while the phrase read has not landed, because the rail must not verdict rows it never received, and the tick STANDS once the Summary is signed (L-30: the gate's group clauses are switched off for a locked book precisely because the accept has happened). One call is the agreement in both directions; two hand-written clause lists over the same rows is how the tick and the button drifted apart the first time the clause set changed. The `'sampling'` branch keeps its own clause set, and its defect check still goes through `summaryBlockingFindings`, the same read `lockCheck` uses.

**U-20 The rail is per-phrase, and Phrase ② has a door the Author opens (2026-11-26, R11, §18).** A Story with two enabled phrases cannot show one phrase's truth on the other's rail: the desk MUST read a phrase through `GET /stories/:id/phrases/:key` (one phrase's entries, one phrase's scores, that phrase's brief) and must never infer a phrase's state from the Summary's rows or from the presence of `plotlines` rows (§4.1's ABSENT rule, R6 applied one phrase down). The Start panel's two options are the compiled Phrase ① text and the Author's own words, and the desk's disabled control and the route's refusal have to come from the same read, in the same words the API answers — an unstarted phrase is not a broken phrase, and the sentence that explains why belongs to the route that refuses. **Honest state at the time of writing:** the server verbs exist and are pinned (`api/phrase-start-routes.test.ts`), and so does the desk's data layer — `api.ts` has `getPhraseDetail` / `startPhrase`, `state.ts` fetches Phrase ②'s read LAZILY (a Story whose `plotlines_mode` is `'classic'` never pays for a surface nobody is looking at) and caches its failure in its own slot so the Plotlines pane cannot print the Summary read's sentence. What is NOT there yet is the PROOF: the rail is re-numbered (`STORY_PHRASES` carries its two rows, Summary ① → Plotlines ②; Chapters ③ carries that number in `ALL_STORY_PHRASES` and in its pane title, not on the rail, and Characters is off the numbering), the entry panes are ONE phrase-generic pair (`components/PhraseEntries.tsx`, driven by `SUMMARY_SURFACE` for Phrase ① and `PLOTLINE_SURFACE` for Phrase ②), Phrase ② has its own pane and its own Start card (`components/PlotlinesPhrasePane.tsx` and `components/PlotlinesHandoff.tsx`, reached through `StoryView`'s `isPlotlinesGroups` route), and `story-contract.ts` answers every question the rail asks — and the desk half IS pinned now: `StoryOutline.test.tsx`, `StoryView.plotlines.test.tsx`, `PlotlinesPhrasePane.test.tsx`, `PlotlinesHandoff.test.tsx`, `SummaryGroupPane.test.tsx`, `SummaryGroupPane.locks.test.tsx`, `StorylineApp.plotlines.test.tsx`, `StorylineApp.test.tsx`, `state.plotlines.test.ts` and `api.plotlines.test.ts` cover the numbering, the Start card, the lazy read and every refusal sentence — the shared entry pair has no suite of its own, so `PhraseEntries.tsx` is pinned through the two SummaryGroupPane files and through `PlotlinesPhrasePane.test.tsx`. What §18.8 still owes is the LIVE-browser probe, which no vitest run can substitute for. One collision to keep out: `src/dashboard/components/story-nav.ts`'s four `StoryPhraseKey`s are the UI's PANEL vocabulary, not the backend catalog — the desk's "characters" and "chapters" panels have no `phrases` row behind them, and its `phraseStarted` means "the artifact behind the panel exists" where the API's `phrase_started` means "the `phrases` row exists". A client that conflates them will show an unstarted phrase as a broken one.

**U-21 A phrase header is also its disclosure control (2026-11-26, T-W6; amended 2026-11-26 by U-25).** The rail's `Phrase 1` / `Phrase 2` rows are click targets: one click folds THAT phrase's rows — **its own `Seed` row first (U-25)**, its sections or plotlines, and the group rows nested inside a section — and clicks again to open them. **THE AMENDMENT IS ONE CLAUSE ADDED AND ONE ROW REMOVED:** a phrase's `Seed` child is inside the fold like every other child of that header, and the rail's single story-level Seed leaf — the row that stayed on screen while its phrase was folded — is gone, because a Seed that outlives its phrase's fold is the story-level leaf U-25 removes, and U-4's amendment records the story-level card that used to name it. Folding is per phrase, never global: on a book with six phrases a fold-all control is a way of losing the outline, and the Author folding Summary must not close Characters. The chevron is an `aria-expanded` cue on the header BUTTON, which is the same control that drops the `ref` and opens the Overview, so folding and navigating cannot disagree.

**U-22 Employee threads: Running by default, Archived on purpose (2026-11-26, T-W6).** The Crew panel shows `running` threads, because that is the question the desk is open to answer ("what is it doing to this Story right now"). `archived` threads are a per-Story opt-in behind an explicit control, and switching to them swaps the FETCH rather than filtering client-side: a long-running Story can hold thousands of archived threads, so the whole set is never fetched to display ten of them. The two lists are never merged — a finished thread silently appearing next to a live one reads as new activity.

**U-23 Phrase prose is rendered as Markdown, and the surfaces that show the source are on purpose (2026-11-26, T4).** The compiled Overview is Markdown **by contract**: `shared/phrase.ts:composePhraseContent` joins one `## <title>` heading per non-empty group (§4.19), and the contract says so in words (`src/storyline-server.swagger.yaml`, `PhraseView.overview`) — which is exactly why the client must render it rather than print it, because a printed Overview hands the Author the `##` instead of the section, and the text the loop judged is the composition, not its source. The READING surfaces therefore render through one component, `components/MarkdownText.tsx`: the Summary Overview card (the grouped pane and the legacy `SummaryPane`), each group/entry pane's whole text (`components/PhraseEntries.tsx`), Phrase ②'s entry-pane prose (the same `components/PhraseEntries.tsx` `group-content` node — the Overview pane there, `components/PlotlinesPhrasePane.tsx:PlotlinesOverviewPane`, prints no prose of its own, pinned both ways in `PlotlinesPhrasePane.test.tsx:108-124`), the Handoff card's copied-Seed prefix (`components/PlotlinesHandoff.tsx` — the `seed_head` window of the compiled Summary, which normally opens on `## Premise`), the revision-body comparison (U-13), and the §4.2 prose fields of a revision — the revision card and the opened revision share `components/SummaryPane.tsx`'s `RevisionFields`, whose `<dd>` carries no `white-space` rule at all, so a draft body there showed the literal `## Premise` AND ran that field's own lines together; its `not written yet` marker stays plain, because a field's VALUE is a value and not a document. That component is `react-markdown` + `remark-gfm` + `remark-breaks` with **no `rehype-raw`**, so raw HTML in model-authored text stays ESCAPED: agent prose is data and never markup, and no run's answer gets to place a `<div>` on the Author's desk. **And a line break the author wrote survives rendering** (the one exception: prose that echoes a raw HTML block line — `before\n<div class="x">kept</div>\nafter` — is parsed as html-block content, so the markup stays escaped and visible but that newline folds into a space): `remark-breaks` turns every newline in agent prose into a real break (`<br>`), because the producers write LINE-ORIENTED text — `src/server/phrase/plotpoints.ts:chapterEntryContent` joins one `Label: value` line per contract field with a single `\n` and no blank lines, and `components/story-contract.ts:revisionBodyText` flattens an object-shaped revision body the same way, onto a node that was a literal `<pre>` before this rule — and markdown alone folds a lone newline into a space, which runs six labelled fields into one sentence. That is the other half of what `pre-wrap` used to do for these surfaces, and taking `pre-wrap` away from rendered markdown (`src/dashboard/styles/story.css`) is only SAFE because of it; a blank line still means a paragraph, and a fence, a table, a list and a heading are untouched by it. Four surfaces keep the source, and every one of them is a decision the next agent must not "fix" — and a plain field value sitting INSIDE a rendered pane is not one of them: §4.2's prose fields render, and the only thing that stays plain among them is the pane's own `not written yet`: the group/entry pane's **U3 word diff** (U-18 — `components/RevisionDiff.tsx` prints the words with each changed run in `<del>/<ins>`, because the diff is prose, not a patch viewer, and rendering one side of a comparison invents a second document to compare); the **Digest** (`sv-digest-body`, D-12a — the engine derives it, so it reads as a derived index and not as prose); the **Seed** (`sv-seed-body` — the Author's own words, shown and copied exactly as typed, which is D-25a's "the Seed is plain text that is trivial to copy"); and the chapter **`ProseEditor`** (U-17), which edits the source and therefore has to show it. Nothing here moves the wire or the store: every text stays the Markdown its author installed, and a rendering choice is never a claim about what the engine accepts.

**U-24 The Summary card's headings carry the clean revision and the Reader's number (2026-11-26, T14).** The Author asked, verbatim: "The heading should have format: `Premise (v2) 100%` where v2 is the clean revision version, and the percentage is how rated by reader." The Overview card therefore DISPLAYS `## Premise` as `## Premise (v7) 100%` — and DISPLAYS is the entire requirement: `components/summary-heading.ts:decorateSummaryOverview` is a rule about how the text U-23 already renders is displayed, applied to the WIRE text at its one call site in `components/SummaryOverviewPane.tsx`. **Nothing about the document moves:** §4.19's composition (`shared/phrase.ts:composePhraseContent` — one `## <title>` per non-empty group, the text under it), the stored Overview, the wire's `overview`, and Phrase ②'s copied-then-hashed Seed all keep the bytes the producer wrote. Two rules make writing the numbers INTO the text impossible rather than merely untidy, and they are the reason the decoration is a render step: D-72's Start copies the compiled Overview VERBATIM onto Phrase ②'s Seed and every downstream gate keys on its `seed_hash`, so a number inside the document would move that Seed — and the phrase's whole independence rule — every time the Reader re-scored it; and P6's single exception (D-65, §17.14) keeps a Reader's number OUT of every artifact — a reader writes nothing, and its score is "never recomputed, averaged or re-scaled anywhere", so it is a display fact about the book, not a word in it. **The two numbers are the card's own rows, passed through and not re-derived:** the clean `selected_revision`, and the score that judged THAT vector (`reviewed.groups[].scores`, joined on `reviewed.vector_hash` and nothing else, D-67), put through the one rounding/clamping rule the reviewed rows already print (`story-contract.ts:reviewedGroupRows` → `:phraseScorePercent`). **Absence prints as absence,** exactly as the provenance strip under the card already does: `(v7)` alone, `100%` alone, or the plain title with neither part — and a real `0` prints in both slots, because revision `0` is a book that has never installed a revision and `0%` is a judgement the Reader actually made — a zero is a judgement, an absence is not one, and that is D-65's distinction rather than a formatting choice. The match is by TITLE, never by position, because a retitled or re-ordered Overview must not get one section's numbers glued onto another section's heading; only the FIRST occurrence per group is decorated, a `##` inside a code fence stays literal, an unclosed fence swallows the rest of the document the way markdown does, the output is idempotent, and no body byte, blank line, line ending or absent trailing newline is touched — which is precisely what makes the value this returns still the document Phrase ② would copy. **Reconciled with U-23 so nobody confuses the stored text with the displayed one:** U-23's sentence about the Handoff card "normally open[ing] on `## Premise`" describes the STORED Seed, which is what that card is showing (`seed_head`, and it stays plain and undecorated on purpose); this requirement changes the grouped Overview card's rendering only, and the legacy `SummaryPane` renders undecorated because a `'sampling'` book has no reviewed rows to decorate with. Pinned by `summary-heading.test.ts` — both numbers, either one alone, a real `0`, the fence and blockquote cases, idempotence, and the byte-for-byte promise — and by `SummaryOverviewPane.test.tsx`, which reads the decorated heading out of the rendered card.

**U-25 Every Phrase carries its own Seed row, and only Phrase ①'s is the Author's idea (2026-11-26, §18.13, D-71/D-72/D-76/D-77).** The Author asked, verbatim: "The Seed (The Author's Idea) belongs to the Phrase 1, so it should be under Phrase 1 as seed. Phrase 2 have different seed (which is really the compiled version of Phrase 1 used as seed, but Author can paste his own seed in). Each Phrase are discrete, seperate from each other." The rail therefore has **no top-level Seed row**: `Seed` is a child row of EVERY phrase header the rail numbers — `components/StoryOutline.tsx` **prepends** one `seedChild()` row to the children of every entry in `components/story-nav.ts:STORY_PHRASES`, so the row is one generic rule rather than a clause per phrase, and it is the **first** child, so it is the first thing the Author sees when a phrase opens — and it folds and opens with its phrase under U-21, because a Seed that stayed visible while its phrase was folded was a story-level leaf wearing a phrase's name. **Mechanically, the row is a child REF of its phrase (`components/story-nav.ts:phraseSeedRef()`), and `StoryPanelKey` has no `'seed'` member any more** — the desk has no third Seed surface, and the pane's heading names the phrase it belongs to: `components/story-folders.tsx:SeedPane` prints `Phrase 1 — Summary · Seed — the Author's idea` from the optional `phrase` prop this wave gave it, on the same `Phrase 1 — Summary · Seed` composition — **one helper now**, `components/story-nav.ts:phraseSeedTitle`, printed by `SeedPane`, by `selectionTitle` and by the folder's own title, so the rail's row and the content area's heading cannot drift apart (pinned verbatim in `components/StoryView.test.tsx`; the strings are byte-for-byte what they were before the helper existed, and `components/story-nav.seed.test.ts` pins those). What that row opens is the phrase's OWN seed, and the two answers are different texts. **(a) Phrase ①'s Seed is the Author's idea** — `stories.idea`, shown by the read-only Seed pane the desk already had (`components/story-folders.tsx:SeedPane`, fed by `view.story.idea` off the Story row), moved one level down the tree rather than rebuilt. P5 is untouched and permanent, and the pane still says so in its own badge — `components/story-folders.tsx` prints "Author-written · read-only for every agent, every phrase, forever (P5)" above ①'s text (the §4 Seed row's rule, in the desk's own words). **The bytes did not move:** nothing was copied into the Summary phrase's `seed` column, and Phrase ①'s gates keep hashing `seedHashOf(story.idea)` (`loop/records.ts:currentSeedBrief`, 1384) — because Phrase ①'s seed IS the Story's idea, which is precisely what `phrase/catalog.ts:phraseSeedOf` answers for a phrase whose own `seed` was never written (the fallback §4.20 records). A rail row is a location and a column is a fact: only the first one moved. **(b) Phrase ②'s Seed is that phrase's OWN copy** — `phrases.seed`, read only through `phraseSeedOf` (D-72), never `stories.idea` — the compiled Phrase ① Overview by default, or the Author's own pasted words. **The desk shows the window the wire carries, not the text:** `seed_head` (the first `SEED_HEAD_CHARS` = 400, `api/projections.ts:1489`) and `seed_chars` from `GET /stories/:id/phrases/:key` (D-76, §18.8), because the full copied seed stays on the row and the Author recognises their own words from a head plus a length they can check — and a pane that printed ①'s idea under ② would be the same lie U-20 already refuses (the desk's own answer for that row is `components/PhraseSeedPane.tsx`, which never reads `story.idea` at all). **And on a Story whose dial is still `'classic'` this pane does not ask for that window either**, and says so rather than sounding like a request that stalled: the projection ride that carries `seed_head`/`seed_chars` belongs to the grouped surface (`state.ts:phraseDetailWanted`), and the pane asks for the read on the same rule — so a started ② on a classic book is told plainly, in the pane's own words, that its copied Seed stays on the server and nothing here is waiting, while its started mark stays what it is: the Story row's, not a read's (U-20's rule one row over; pinned in `components/StoryView.plotlines.test.tsx`, whose classic-dial case asserts that pane's own no-read sentence and that ①'s idea is NOT printed under ② — that file never renders ①'s Seed row, so the fact that ①'s row prints ①'s OWN text is pinned instead by `components/StoryView.test.tsx`, whose `{panel: 'summary', ref: phraseSeedRef()}` render asserts `.sv-seed-body` equals `view.story.idea` (`story-folders.tsx:61`), and on the real rail, on the GROUPED book, by `src/dashboard/real-server-seed.test.tsx`). **(c) Which of the two ② runs on is chosen ONCE, at Start, and never again:** the Start card's two arms (`components/PlotlinesHandoff.tsx`, with the same choice restated on an unstarted phrase's own Seed pane — the same verb and the same refusal sentences, no new route and no second rule) send `POST /stories/:id/phrases/{key}/start` with `{source: 'compiled' | 'custom', text?}` (D-76), and after the phrase has started no verb replaces its seed — a new seed means a new phrase, which is D-71's one-time Start, D-72's copy, and D-77's rule that a Story-Seed edit DELETES Phrase ② instead of re-seeding it; the started pane is therefore read-only and says why instead of showing a button the Author cannot press (U-16). The desk's shut control and the route's one-time `409` are one fact in the route's own words (U-20's rule, applied to this row). **(d) Phrases stay discrete, and this row is where the Author SEES the discreteness** rather than inferring it from prose: two phrases, two Seeds, and the loop already enforces the same split one level down — Phrase ①'s brief keys on `story.idea` (`loop/records.ts:currentSeedBrief`) while `structurePlotlines` refuses an Editor's `seed_hash` that is not `phraseSeedOf`'s current value (§8.8, §4.20).

**WHAT DID NOT MOVE, and the requirement says so because the ask sounded like a redesign:** **no tool was added, renamed or re-scoped.** The ask named `summaryTool` and `plotlineTool`; the catalogue's names are `readSeed` (§8.4) → `structureSeed` (§8.7) → `editSummary` (§8.1, refused `R6` on a grouped book) on Phrase ①, and `structurePlotlines` (§8.8) with `readPlotline` (§8.4) and `createPlotline`/`editPlotline` (§8.1, refused `R6` on a live lane, §8.8) on Phrase ② — and none of them needed renaming, because a crew tool is how an AGENT reaches a phrase while the desk is a client of the per-phrase HTTP verbs (U-20). No route, no wire field, no OpenAPI key, no `phrases.seed` read, no hash and no D-71/D-72/D-76/D-77 rule changed; this is a rail requirement, not a server one, and `SNAPSHOT_VERSION` is still 4.

**Two facts recorded honestly, because the requirement this one amends promised more than the desk had.** (1) The "pinned, read-only card at the top of every Story" U-4 named **was never mounted**: `components/SeedCard.tsx` is unmounted dead code — its only importer is `SeedCard.test.tsx` — and the surface the Author used was the rail's Seed row plus the read-only pane. The dead component stays (deleting it is another wave's business, and this file records facts rather than tidying them). (2) **This is the one place the Seed's missing edit control is recorded.** The Author has **no edit affordance** for the Seed's text: `components/NewStoryForm.tsx` captures it at create (`{title, idea}` → `createStory`), and `loop/engine.ts:editSeed` is reachable only as `PATCH /stories/:id`'s `idea` field (the `PATCH /stories/:id` handler in `api/routes.ts`, which grants `confirm: true` itself precisely because the desk has no confirm to ask for). That gap predates this requirement and survives it — U-25 moves a row, it does not add an editor, and promising one here would advertise a write path D-25a's confirmation flow does not have in the desk. **And it is the gap U-12 sits on, without amending U-12:** its "can write the Seed … by hand at any time" rules about WHO may write the text — a permission, never a promise of a control — so the missing control is a hole in the desk, recorded here, and not a reason to weaken the requirement. §18.13 points at this paragraph rather than restating it.

**The pins are the rail's and the content area's own suites:** `components/StoryOutline.test.tsx` (every phrase header carries exactly one `Seed` child, first in the fold, folded with the header per U-21, and nothing else on the rail is labelled Seed), `components/StoryView.test.tsx` (the phrase-child selection opens that phrase's Seed pane, and the heading names the phrase, and ①'s pane prints that P5 badge VERBATIM — so the promise (a) makes is a string a suite owns, not a sentence in this file), `components/PhraseSeedPane.test.tsx` (a started phrase shows its OWN read's copied head and length — never the Author's idea — read-only per D-72; an unstarted one offers both Start arms and answers with the route's own refusal sentences) and `components/story-nav.seed.test.ts` (the one fixed token, the retired `seed` panel key, the per-phrase title, the absence of an attention mark, and the directive bar's focus) — **and the routing is now pinned over a real server rather than a stub:** `src/dashboard/real-server-seed.test.tsx` boots the server, renders the real `StorylineApp`, clicks ①'s `Seed` row and ②'s `Seed` row on the real rail, and asserts the content area each click opens (the grouped Story's own `phrases/groups/<uuid>/revise` verb composes the Overview and `phrases/plotlines/start` copies it, and the frame the file pins before it renders anything is `phrase_started: {summary: true, plotlines: true}` — so ②'s pane prints `phrase-seed-state` = `started` over the copied 46-character Overview, while ①'s pane prints `stories.idea` verbatim; **the spy is `getPhraseDetail`, which is Phrase ②'s read**, and on that grouped book it proves the desk asked for it exactly ONCE — ①'s pane needs no spy, because it is fed by the Story row (`view.story.idea`, `story-folders.tsx:61`) and not by a phrase read at all), and the same file pins this requirement's **classic-dial state, which is the honest one**: on a `'classic'` Story that spy is never called, ②'s Seed pane says NO read is coming and nothing is waiting, and ①'s pane is simply not in that test's frame — it never opens ①'s row there, so ①'s own-text rendering stays the GROUPED book's pin and `components/StoryView.test.tsx`'s; `components/DirectiveBar.test.tsx` now runs the bar on a GROUPED dial too, pinning the focus Phrase ②'s real ref gets there. §18.13 records the desk suite's state as observed against the finished tree.

---

## 11. Reading economy — cost is out of scope

**E-0** Neither tokens nor wall-clock are design constraints. Unlimited both, by decision. **No budget, cap, timeout, or round limit may gate a phase transition, halt a loop, or be used to argue against a design.** A Story may be worked on for days; that is expected, not a problem to solve.

**E-0a** The only scarcity the design honours is **attention and correctness**: the Author's time, and the risk that a fresh context reads too much and loses the thread. Four things replace every budget as the thing that stops work: the **phase sign-off** (L-29), the **approval wait** (D-34), and **drained** (L-37) — all three designed — and **loop detection** (§6.8), the unplanned one. Note what is _not_ on that list: an unfinished to-do list. The loop keeps finding work until the Author says the book is done (L-25b) — the throttle is the Author's attention, spent through the auto-approve dial (D-35), never a cap on how much may be noticed.
**E-0b** Web reads are free in the same sense (D-27 aside): a Critic MAY fetch twenty pages to support one Idea, and any other role may look something up before it acts on it (T-6b). The constraint on research is **verification, not volume** — an unverified claim is worthless however well-sourced, and a verified one costs nothing extra to have.
**E-0c** **Sources are not archived.** A citation only has to hold for the duration of the Story being written (D-27): a book is produced in days, published or posted, and then the Author starts the next one. Link rot after publication is not this product's problem, and neither is a citation library. `accessed_at` is recorded and that is all.

What still matters is **reading economy**, because a fresh context that re-reads the whole book every turn produces _worse judgments_, not merely slower ones.

**E-1** A run's required reading is `Digest + the artifact under review + the criteria`. Roughly constant regardless of book length.
**E-2** A run that reads more than its brief asked for is a `digest_gap` signal, so the Digest improves rather than the reading silently growing.
**E-3** A run's brief MUST fit the job it is given: one judgment, or one bounded edit set. An oversized brief is what reintroduces context rot _inside_ a single run, which P2 exists to prevent.

**C-B** All chapters are in flight together by default (L-8), which is safe only because every one of them is a separate session (S-1). Since cost is out of scope, expense is never a reason to sequence; the dependency set exists to say **where a fix must propagate** (L-9), not what may run when.
**C-C** There is no per-Work-Item, per-chapter, or per-Story budget. The stops are the **phase sign-off** (L-29, the designed one) and **loop detection** at Story level (§6.8, the unplanned one), and both pause rather than fail.
**E-4** Divergence is cheap and valuable: `attempts` defaults above 1 wherever an Editor template is assigned, because comparing drafts is the mechanism the whole architecture exists to provide (P13).

---

## 13. Non-goals

- **Not a chat client.** There is no conversation surface for the loop. The composer is a directive bar addressed to the Editor; its answer is the queue changing, never a reply.
- **Not an agent list.** The right panel names the global employees and the crew templates. An agent is an employee only while its thread is executing; it is never a roster entry.
- **No reviewer prose.** A reader never writes a chapter, a plotline, or a line of dialogue. It files a finding and a task.
- **No Editor prose.** An Editor selects and merges structure; it never composes a sentence (L-19).
- **No best-story ambition.** The target is the Author's own story, automated (P12, G-8). A criterion that chases objective quality is a build defect.
- **No surviving rejected drafts.** Non-selected revisions are deleted (P13, D-22).
- **No agent chooses the chapter count.** The Author declares it, and it is read-only for every agent at every phase (D-3b).
- **No token budget, no time budget, no round cap.** Cost is out of scope by decision, not by oversight.
- **No silent spinning.** A loop that stops converging says so and waits (P14, §6.8).
- **No long-lived agent.** An employee becomes an agent only for the thread its Work Item opens; no thread outlives its Run.
- **No agent edits a _locked_ Summary**, and **no agent edits the Seed** — those are the Author's, and the only way in is a backtrack.
- **No Critic writes prose.** A Critic researches, rates, and proposes; it never edits — and it never decides that its own Idea is good enough (D-26, T-9).
- **No unconfirmed suspicion costs a Writer a pass.** A finding from a Critic or reader is a claim until an Editor verifies it (D-33).
- **No completion requires an empty findings list.** The loop always finds something; completion needs no work left _at the dial threshold_, not zero findings, and the Author's acceptance closes the remainder with a count on it (L-25b, L-37).
- **No appeal above the Editor's triage.** It sets the cost, disputes what it doubts, and that is the end of it; the Author edits the template, not the finding (D-34a).
- **No reporting a finished book as stuck.** Drained outranks stalled (L-37a): running out of problems is not going in circles.
- **No discarded Idea is deleted inside the phase that decided it.** A decision is memory, not an alternative (D-28, D-32) — and it is memory for that phase only (D-36).
- **No Idea outlives its phase.** Locking deletes the tray; the artifact is what remains (D-36).
- **No finding reaches the Writer unapproved.** Critic raises, Editor verifies and rewrites, Author approves — unless the Story's switch approved it for them (D-34, D-35).
- **No tool is hidden from a role.** Every session sees the whole catalogue; the server says no (T-6a, T-1a).
- **Nothing is a prompt-only rule.** Every capability §8 names is a registered, schema-validated tool. If the surface cannot do something a brief asks for, that is a build gap to be reported and filled (T-12), not something to exhort a fresh context into (T-13).
- **No phase runs past its sign-off.** A Goal that passes stops the loop (L-29); there is no "keep polishing until the Author notices".
- **No carrying work across a backtrack.** Scrapped phases leave prose behind; only their records survive (D-25).
- **No numeric quality score.** Criteria only.
- **No autonomous sign-off.** The Author locks each of the three phases and marks work done. A phase that passes its Goal waits; it does not proceed.
- **No multi-story run.** A run exists for exactly one Story.
- **Nothing survives a finished book.** `completed` means completed: no taste record, no carry-over of what the Author rejected, no export, no lessons. A book that is done leaves records behind for audit and nothing else (D-25a, D-39).
- **No sequel awareness in the first build.** A sequel is a new Story whose phase ① crews are given **read access to the prequel** — that is the shape of it, and it is deferred, not designed (D-39). It is the one place the single-Story scoping rule (T-3) will need an explicit exception, which is why it stays out of v1 rather than being half-built.
- **No agent-authored Digest.** The Writer writes everything in the book; the Digest is not in the book — it is derived from commits by the engine (D-12a).
- **No Author prose while the crew is running.** The chapter editor is live only from a pause (D-37); the product does not attempt live collaborative editing.
- **No recipes in the first build.** Templates carry it. A recipe is what gets named once several Stories have been built the same way by hand (P15).
- **No engine rewrite needed to change the design.** Not a non-goal so much as a build constraint (P15): a phase model, a role set, or a criterion must be replaceable without rewriting the store.

---

## 14. Open decisions

Record the answer here rather than inventing one silently.

**Answered by the Author (normative — these are decisions now, not questions):**

- **Seed changes → start from scratch.** No partial reopen: a new idea is a new book. (D-25a)
- **All chapters are written together, and cross-chapter consistency is the employees' job.** A contradiction between chapter 1 and chapter 12 marks both and the loop fixes the side the plotline says is wrong. (L-8, L-8a, L-11a)
- **Phase ① gets full divergence — as sampling, not as an Editor choice.** N tool-less one-turn samples per round, M screening passes per sample, and a deterministic engine selection; the Editor's phase-① selection was removed by the research pipeline. (§16.1, §16.6, D-3d)
- **Every role gets the whole tool catalogue**; a Critic is defined by its instructions — read the story, rate it, propose ideas, use web search to form and confirm them. (T-6a, D-30)
- **Discarded Ideas are never deleted _within their phase_**, because the Editor must know what was already rejected or the loop re-argues it forever — and the tray is emptied when the phase locks. (D-28, D-32, D-36)
- **An approved finding is what starts a Writer.** Critic raises with a cost → Editor verifies and rewrites → Author approves (or the switch does) → task → Writer. Nothing skips the last step unless the dial says so. (D-34, D-35)
- **Completion is the Author's call, not an empty queue.** The loop proposes; the Author accepts; the rest is discarded with a count on it. (L-25b, U-14)
- **A Seed edit is a full clear.** A second book from the same idea is a hand-copied Seed in a new Story. (D-25a)
- **Critics may propose anything**; the Editor is what keeps ideas inside the Author's guidelines. No pre-filtering of what a Critic is allowed to think. (D-30, D-31)
- **Sources are not archived.** A citation only has to hold while the Story is being written. (E-0c)
- **Tools are built and registered on the harness for every role, and the catalogue is discoverable from inside the run.** Writer, Critic and Editor each hold the whole surface, can list it and read any signature without leaving the session, and report a gap when a brief asks for something no tool reaches — so nobody is ever left needing a capability they cannot find. (T-6a, T-6c, T-12, T-13)
- **Phase ③ is the automated one.** The Critic judges effect (diverges from the plotline, references something that doesn't hold up, spends a mystery early), the Editor verifies whether it is true, the Writer updates. The Author watches, stops, and steers at their own discretion — none of it is required. (Loop B, L-35, D-33)
- **Critics triage by cost; the tool enforces the schema.** `cost` is a required enum, so no finding arrives untriaged. (T-11)
- **The Editor verifies and rewrites the finding for the Author's eye.** (D-33, D-34)
- **The Author approves progressively, and nothing reaches the Writer without that approval.** The loop keeps finding more underneath them until the Author accepts completion, which discards the rest. (D-34, L-25b)
- **There is a switch to auto-approve `critical` and `major`**, because the Author would approve them anyway. (D-35)
- **Ideas persist only within their phase.** Locking a phase keeps its final artifact and deletes everything else, the tray included. Phase ③ is the only place that could pile up, and the Author is watching it, so it does not. (D-36)
- **A Seed edit clears every later phase.** To spin a second book from the same idea, copy the Seed into a new Story by hand — the product does not fork Stories. (D-25a)
- **The Author writes chapter prose in the UI, from a paused Story.** Pause & edit, type, resume; the pause is what prevents races and corruption, and the resume re-derives the Digest and re-checks dependents. (P9a, D-37, D-37a, D-37b, U-17)
- **Writer, Editor and Critic are employee kinds; templates are crew definitions that assign them, and the Author decides how many roles exist.** A single role may run N agents of its employee kind inside one run and aggregate to one answer, so "one Editor or a panel" is a template setting rather than an architecture decision, and the panel's disagreement never reaches the loop as a vote. (D-38, D-38a, T-0, U-8d)
- **Nothing survives a finished book.** Finished means finished. A sequel is a later feature: a new Story whose phase ① crews can read the prequel. (D-39)
- **The Digest is built by the loop engine from committed changes**, so the history is right and no authoring agent can contaminate the thing every reviewer trusts. (D-12a)
- **The design is expected to change.** Build it out, test it in building, revise the engine; deferred things stay named holes. (P15)
- **The Editor's triage is final.** Its cost call on a finding is not appealable item by item; the Author tunes the template when it is wrong, the same way they tune prose taste. (D-34a, G-7)
- **The loop runs until the Story is drained, and then the Author decides whether the book is done.** No budget, no empty-queue test, no automatic completion: the crew stops when it has stopped finding anything at or above the dial threshold, says so, and waits. (L-37, L-25b, D-35b)

**Closed by the agent's recommendation** — the Author delegated these ("ask me anything critical"; they are the nitty ones). Each is reversible on its own and none changes the architecture:

- **1a** losing revisions: hard delete in the selection commit, no grace period (D-22 stands).
- **1d** a divergence unit is **a chapter, a plotline, or the Summary** — not acts, scenes, or endings; a wider unit is expressed as more attempts on its chapters. In phase ① the unit is the whole Summary sample, and the divergence is the sampling round (§16.6).
- **2** the Goal is **per-Story with a per-chapter `bar_override`**, so the ending can be held higher than chapter 3 (U-6).
- **5a** "all agents agreed" = hard criteria pass under independent fresh review (L-28); no N-reviewers-in-one-round requirement, since fresh review already happens across rounds and the dial controls volume.
- **5b** a contradiction in act two does **not** halt the book: the uninvolved chapters keep being polished and `status_reason` says so (L-36 extended to the stuck case).
- **6** escalation is dialled per **Story** (`auto_approve` already lives there, D-35), not per template — the crew's taste is per template, the interruption budget is per book.
- **8** vocabulary: the UI shows **Seed, Summary, plotline, chapter, finding, Idea, Goal**; beats/promise/payoff/arc stay internal except inside a criterion's own text.
- **11** the Author **can unapprove one chapter** — keep its prose, reopen its review — and it is a different verb from backtrack (L-31). It is the one case where a chapter may legitimately sit at a different vintage than its neighbours.
- **13a** a failed web search is just a thinner Idea, not a `digest_gap`; provider is config, not spec.
- **14** **no partial phase locks** (L-33). A lock means the phase is signed.
- **15** same-criterion `taste` findings are **folded by the Editor into one entry per chapter** as part of its rewrite (D-34), since fifty "the prose felt flat here" lines are unreadable. Under D-33b every `taste` row IS a `proposal` (an `issue` at that cost is refused at raise time), so the fold merges claims of one kind and never blurs a defect into a preference.
- **17** the Story view shows the **cost distribution of the last N rounds** — confirmed-at-each-cost and downgrades — so a systematically soft Editor is visible as a number without any per-item review. It is the only check on D-34a's no-appeal rule.
- **0 / 1d / 5 display:** the Story view groups chapters **by what the crews are doing to them** (drafting / contested / awaiting a fix / clean), not by order, because with all chapters in flight together order is no longer what is happening (L-8, U-4).

**The running log** — every question raised during the design, with its status as it was answered. Where an item still ends in "Recommended:", the recommendation block above supersedes it; the reasoning is kept because it is the record of _why_ the answer is what it is.

0. **Resolved by the three-phase model:** the Author's first-class writing is the **Seed** and the **lock** — and now also **chapter prose, from a paused Story** (P9a, D-37). Phase 1 means the Author's words are structurally in the book from the first record, and the pause-to-edit rule means they stay in it at every later phase without racing the crew.
   0a. **Does the Seed ever change?** — **Answered: yes, it clears every later phase, same Story row.** A second book from the same idea is a manual Seed copy into a new Story; no forking, no lineage, no "duplicate with history". (D-25a)
1. **Does the Author ever write chapter prose directly?** — **Answered: yes, in the UI, from a paused Story.** Pause & edit → type → resume; the pause is what prevents the race and the corruption, and resume re-derives the Digest and re-checks dependents. `proposals` therefore keeps its purpose in one direction only: it is how an _agent_ touches Author prose (P9, D-20a), never how the Author touches their own. (P9a, D-37, D-37a, D-37b)
   1a. **Who deletes the losing revisions, and when?** D-22 says the server, in the selection commit, hard. The alternative is "the Author does it, later" — which keeps the drafts around for comparison but reintroduces exactly the pile of alternatives P13 removes. Confirm hard deletion, and decide whether the Author wants a grace period (the run's output recoverable for N days) before it is truly gone.
   1b. **One Editor, or a panel?** — **Answered: it is a template setting, not an architecture decision.** Writer, Editor and Critic are employee kinds; a template role assigns one and says how many agents of that kind run inside a single run. The Author chooses how many roles exist, and a single Editor role may run N agents inside one run and aggregate to one answer (`agents`, `aggregate`). The loop still receives exactly one report per decision, so there is no vote anywhere in the engine and D-34a (the Editor's call is final) survives. The one obligation: internal dissent is named in the `selection_note` and shown on the run (D-38, U-8d).
   1c. **Can the Editor delegate a merge to a Writer?** — **Answered by the recommendation.** The Editor _requests_ a merge (`requestMerge`) and the sweep dispatches a Writer; `delegate` (D-17e) and a role's own `agents` (D-38) are for reasoning inside the run, never for writing. The write monopoly stays with the Writer (P10).
   1d. **What is a "unit" for divergence?** Chapters and plotlines are named. Are whole acts, the ending, or a single scene also units that get N attempts? Wider units give more value per comparison and much larger comparisons. **Settled for phase ① (2026-11-23):** the unit is one complete Summary sample, and N is `summary_sampling` (§16.6).
2. **Is the Goal per-Story, per-chapter, or both?** Recommended: per-Story with a per-chapter override (`bar_override`), so the ending can be held to a higher bar than chapter 3.
3. **One Writer per Story, or a Writer per phase?** — **Answered by D-38a: the Author's choice, no engine rule.** A structural Writer for ① and ② and a prose Writer for ③ is just two templates assigned to different phases (`serves_phase` already exists). Two voices on the same phase is also legal; style divergence between them surfaces as a `taste` finding rather than an error.
4. **Digest ownership** — **Answered: the loop engine builds it from committed changes, so the history is right.** The Writer monopoly is broken here on purpose: the agent that wrote chapter 9 must not be the one that summarises it, because every downstream run trusts the Digest without checking (D-12a). Crews may only file `digest_gap`; staleness still blocks a review and never a fix (D-14); and any past Digest is reconstructible, so the Author can see what a reviewer was actually told when it judged.
5. **Chapter ordering default** — **Answered: all chapters together** (L-8), with the dependency set repurposed as a propagation map (L-9, C-B). Still open: **how the Author sees that** — 8 chapters all `in_pass` at once is a wall of motion. Recommended: the Story view groups them by _what the crews are doing to them_ (drafting / contested / awaiting a fix / clean) rather than listing them in order, because order is no longer what's happening.
   5a. **What counts as "all agents agreed" for `completed`?** L-25 says hard criteria pass + no competing revisions + clean ledger + soft threshold. Is that enough, or must N independent fresh reviewers concur within the same round? The latter is far more robust and, with no cost constraint, affordable.
   5b. **Does `pending` stop the whole Story or only the stuck unit?** — **Largely answered by the approval gate** (L-36): an unapproved finding, and by extension any single stuck chapter, leaves the Story `ready` and the crew working; only a Story whose _every_ remaining blocker is the Author's to move goes `pending`. Still open for the genuinely stuck case: if chapters 6–9 are a contradiction the loop cannot resolve (L-21), should chapters 1–5 keep being polished, or is a Story with an unresolved contradiction in act two better left alone? Recommended: keep working the uninvolved chapters, and say so in `status_reason` — halting the whole book over one contradiction is how the crew goes idle while the Author is asleep.
6. **Escalation frequency dial:** per template, or per Story? Too chatty and it isn't autonomous; too silent and the Author loses the book they wanted.
7. **Recipe scope** — **Answered: out of the first build, and the engine is expected to change anyway.** "It is an evolving design. Even I'm not sure how this turns out yet — we build it out, test it in building, and then revise the engine." So templates carry v1, a recipe stays a named hole (§13), and the build constraint that follows is P15: a phase model, a role set, or a criterion has to be replaceable without rewriting the store.
8. **Vocabulary budget:** how much of "beats / promise / payoff / arc" is shown to the Author versus kept internal? Decides how much of §4 the UI exposes.
9. **Does Phase 1 get full divergence?** — **Answered: yes** (D-3d). **Superseded 2026-11-23:** the divergence is the sampling round, not a classic Editor choice. N tool-less one-turn samples (`summary_sampling`, standard 3), M screening passes per sample (`summary_critics`, standard 5), and a deterministic engine selection; the sampler's `attempts` is 1 because one run is one sample. The consequence the original answer priced still holds in a different place: the count the Author tunes for phase ① is `summary_sampling`, and the sampler's prompt is where tone guidance lives (the Seed Brief's `tone_hints`). (§16.1, §16.5, §16.6)
10. **How many Critics, and what angles?** — _How many_ is **answered: the Author's choice** (D-38a), and a single Critic role may itself run N agents of the Critic employee kind (D-38). Still open is _which angles_, and that is template content rather than a build decision. Closed by recommendation: ship four built-in Critic templates, one per angle — **genre convention**, **plausibility and fact**, **what this Author's readers complain about**, **mystery and pacing** — because "the Critic" is not one judgment; it is four, and one Critic asked to do all four does two of them badly. The Editor's verification cost is the same either way (E-0). **Settled for phase ① (2026-11-23):** `summary_critics` (standard 5) is both the number of screening passes per sample and the number of tool-using deep-review passes the selected revision owes, and the passes rotate over those four angles (§16.7). The screening pass is a mode of the same Critic employee, never a Reader.
11. **Backtrack granularity** — **Answered: granularity exists, but it rarely matters**, because all chapters are in flight together and a problem in chapter 12 already drags chapter 1 along with it (L-8a). So the practical distinction is not _which chapter_ but _which phase_. Still open: should the Author be able to **unapprove one chapter** (keep its prose, reopen its review) without a phase backtrack? Recommended: yes, and it is a different verb from backtrack — it is the one case where a single chapter may legitimately be at a different vintage than its neighbours.
12. **Are discarded Ideas deleted?** — **Answered: within the phase, never; across a phase boundary, always** (D-28, D-32, D-36). The unbounded-growth problem I raised is solved at the source — the tray is emptied at the lock — so no relevance filter is needed.
    12a. **The cross-Story taste record** — **Answered: no. Nothing survives a finished book.** "A book is finished means it finished." The recommendation of a per-Author `rejections` list is rejected: the next book is not smarter by machinery, and that is a choice, not an oversight. The cross-book channel that _is_ planned, later, is the prequel: a sequel's phase ① crews get read access to the previous book (D-39) — which carries the story, not the taste, and is deferred rather than half-built.
13. **Web access specifics** — Partly answered: every role may browse (T-6b), and archiving is **not** needed (E-0c). Still open: **(a)** which provider and whether a failed search is a `digest_gap`-style signal or just a thinner Idea; **(b)** whether Critics may browse **fiction itself** — the risk is the crew imitating a live author instead of the Author (C-13), and the Author's answer is that instructions decide (D-30), which means someone must actually _write_ that instruction into the built-in Critic templates or the risk is real by default.
14. **Is a phase lock allowed to be partial?** — Sharper now that all chapters run together: if phase ③ never waits for a per-chapter lock, "partial lock" only means anything in phases ① and ②. Recommended: no, still.
15. **How loud is a Critic in phase ③?** — **Answered by the cost enum plus the auto-approve switch** (T-11, D-35): `critical`, `major` and `minor` flow through to a Writer automatically, `taste` sits in the queue until the Author chooses, and completion discards whatever is left (L-25b). The dial exists. Still open: **whether `taste` findings should aggregate at all** — fifty separate "the prose felt flat here" entries are unreadable, while one line per chapter is not. Recommended: the Editor folds same-criterion `taste` findings into one entry per chapter as part of its rewrite (D-34), since it is already rewriting them for the Author's eye. (D-33b settled the first half of this question on the way: `taste` is a `proposal`'s cost, never an `issue`'s, so what folds is a set of preferences and the defects stay counted on their own.)
16. **Does the Editor's rewrite of a finding need the Author's trust?** — **Answered: yes, and that is what the Editor is for.** Its severity call is final and there is no per-item appeal (D-34a). The Author tunes the template when the triage is wrong, exactly as with prose taste (G-7).
17. **What if the Editor is systematically wrong about severity?** With no appeal step, the only symptom of an Editor that files every `critical` as a `minor` is a book that drains too early and reads thin. Recommended: the Story view shows the **cost distribution of the last N rounds** — how many findings the Editor confirmed at each cost, and how many it downgraded — so a pattern is visible without any per-item review. That is a number, not a queue, and it costs the Author one glance.

---

## 15. Vertical slice — the thing to build first

Prove the loop before building twelve panels. One Story, all three phases, in this order:

0. Author writes a **Seed** — three sentences — and `chapter_count` = 8. Every employee can read it; nobody can write it. **Steps 1 and 1a are the LEGACY walk** (§16): they describe a `summary_mode: 'sampling'` book. A Story created now is grouped, and its step 1 instead runs §17: the same two research jobs, then one tool-less `draft_groups` pass writes all five groups and INSTALLS on acceptance (§17.4), `summary_critics` reading seats review the group vector (§17.5), the four-angle deep review files findings that must name their groups (R3), and the approved `fix` task routes the NEXT batch to exactly the groups it names — with no select, no merge, no truncation, and no round counter anywhere.
1. **Phase ①.** The sweep runs the research pipeline in order (§16.1). An **Editor** job structures the Seed (`structureSeed` → one `accepted` Seed Brief, `seed_hash` matching the Seed, and the `research_questions` list that states — explicitly, T-11 if omitted — whether the book needs research at all: `[]` waives the leg, D-51). A **Researcher** job researches that brief and records two sourced `knowledge` entries — one `critical`, one `high` — with `writeKnowledge`; a pass that finds nothing to record closes itself with `reportNoResearch` (zero writes, and a report the loop counts — D-51). Three tool-less one-turn **Writer** samples then expand the brief (no tools, one `SummarySample` JSON object each) and five tool-less **Critic** passes screen each sample with an `IssueList`. The **engine** selects the least-issue sample, installs its six prose fields on the Summary — never `chapter_count` — appends the round to `sampling_history`, hard-deletes the losing samples and their reviews, and advances `sampling_round`, all in one commit (§16.5, §16.6). The `idea→summary` levels read from the installed Summary.
   1a. The selected revision gets the real review: five tool-using `wi_review_*` **Critic** passes (full catalogue, web included). Their findings go to one **Editor** job per finding (`verifyFinding`), and the Story's dial covers `major`, so an approved `critical`/`major` one becomes a `fix` task (D-34, D-35). **Lock Summary** is disabled while that task is open, and the open task arms round 2 — three new samples compiled from the selected draft plus the fix task (§16.7). When round 2's selection consumes the task, the fresh deep review finds nothing blocking, and the phase Goal (C-14, C-15) passes, the Story stops at `pending`/`phase_signoff`. The lock sets `summary_locked` = true, `phase` → `plotlines`, a Writer's next `editSummary` is refused naming P5, **and the tray is emptied in the lock commit** (D-36). The tray gate (L-7a) itself is unchanged, but phase ①'s shipped crew files no Ideas: the screening pass has no tools, and the deep-review pass is told to report defects rather than proposals.
2. **Phase ②.** **Steps 2 and 2a are the LEGACY walk** (§6, `plotlines_mode: 'classic'`); a Story created now runs the lane, and its step 2 is **2b**. Sweep spawns a **Writer** → reads the locked Summary and the Goal, creates exactly 8 plotlines via `createPlotline`. C-0 passes at 8 and fails at 7 or 9. A **Consistency reader** files findings (it cannot edit); the next sweep hands each finding to a fresh Writer, which edits. Goal reads `4/6 · failing: C-4, C-5`, then `6/6`.
   2a. Critics propose plotline changes; the Author selects; the loop stops at `phase_signoff`; the Author **locks the plotlines** → `phase` → `chapters`.
   2b. **The lane (R11, §18) is what a Story created now walks.** The Author **starts Phrase ②** — the route copies the compiled Overview, or the Author's own words, onto a `phrases` row as that phrase's Seed, and from that moment the phrase reads its own copy and never `stories.idea` (D-72). An **Editor** `structurePlotlines` pass answers with a chapter count and a one-line seed per chapter, and the Author's accept is the one irrevocable act: `chapter_count` becomes 8 and `chapter_1 … chapter_8` exist in the same commit (D-72). Tool-less **Writer** batches then answer `ChapterDraft` — plotpoints per entry, every entry the batch carried or nothing at all (D-73) — and each install writes the entry's revision line AND that chapter's §4.3 contract, so C-0 and every downstream criterion read the rows they always read (D-75). The **Critic**'s deep cross-chapter seats read the whole chapter vector and file findings naming the chapters they cross (R3); the approved `fix` task routes the NEXT batch to exactly those entries, whole (D-66). The **Reader**'s score seat arms on THIS phrase's clean vector (D-67's rule one phrase down, display-only), and the **Editor**'s lock seat offers to freeze one chapter at a time (R-lock) — an offer, never a debt. When the phase Goal passes, the Author **locks phase ②** and the 8 chapters materialize against the contracts the entries hold — the same rows step 3 opens, from a pipeline with no select and no merge. A **Seed edit or a backtrack to phase ① takes the phrase with it** — row, entries, entry history and brief — because the Start verb is one-way and a phrase nobody can re-seed is a dead book (D-77).
3. **Phase ③.** All 8 chapters enter `in_pass` together (L-8). A **Writer** on chapter 1 → `createChapter` + `editChapter`; a **Review crew** (fresh) → verdict against C-7…C-9 + `raiseFinding`; a **Critic** reads the chapter as the Author would and files "this spends the mentor's mystery two chapters early" — which lands `unconfirmed` (D-33). The next sweep gives that finding to an **Editor**, who `verifyFinding`s it true, which spawns the task; a fresh **Writer** fixes it. Chapter `done` on Author accept — or with no Author action at all (L-35).
   3a. **The false positive.** A second Critic claims chapter 3 contradicts chapter 7. The Editor checks and **disputes** it: no Writer pass is spent, and the disagreement is visible to the Author as a disputed finding rather than as work.
   3b. **The cross-chapter fix.** A confirmed finding on chapter 12 says the locket was already sold in chapter 1. Both chapters go `needs_recheck` (L-8a, L-11a); the loop fixes chapter 1, because the plotline says the sale happens there. No Author action.
   3c. **The gate.** The `major` finding auto-approves (D-35) and the Writer is on it within a sweep. A `minor` one the same Critic files sits `confirmed` in the queue, **spawning no work at all**, until the Author approves it or discards it (D-34). Flip the switch to include `minor` and the next one goes through by itself — that single toggle is the difference between steering phase ③ and watching it.
   3d. **The schema.** A Critic files a finding with no `cost`; the tool refuses naming the field, and the run re-sends it triaged (T-11). It calls `describeTool` on `raiseFinding` first and gets the enum values back — the correction is a lookup, not a guess (T-6c).
   3e. **The gap.** Give a Critic a brief that asks for something no tool reaches ("judge whether this chapter's length matches the genre norm" — there is no word-count surface). It files its finding anyway and calls `reportGap` naming what it needed and what it did instead. The gap appears on the Author's queue as a tool request, and the Story's `trend` is untouched (T-12).
4. Author hits **Stop** mid-chapter-2. Dispatch halts, the run dies, state is intact, restart resumes at chapter 2.
   4b. **The Author writes.** Mid-phase-③, the Author decides chapter 6's opening is wrong and hits **Pause & edit**: dispatch halts, in-flight runs die, and the prose pane becomes editable (D-37). Attempting the same edit without pausing is refused naming D-37. They rewrite two paragraphs, hit **Resume**, and the pane tells them what that costs: the Digest re-derived from the edit (D-12a), the chapters that depend on those paragraphs marked `needs_recheck` (D-37a, L-11a). The span is now Author-written, so the next Writer may only `proposeChange` against it (P9, D-20) — and the crew does not try to undo what the Author typed (D-37b).
   4a. **Backtrack.** On chapter 5 the Author decides act two is wrong and **backtracks to phase ②**. Chapters 1–5 and their revisions are gone; the plotlines are unlocked and editable; the verdicts and Ideas of the scrapped pass are still readable as history. Nothing in the store still references a chapter that no longer exists.

Then prove the two things that make this the product rather than a pipeline:

8. **Divergence.** Set `attempts: 3` on chapter 2 (after the backtrack, chapters are being written again). Three fresh Writers produce three drafts, invisible to each other. Readers verdict each. An **Editor** applies the Author's `selection_rules`, picks draft 2, writes a `selection_note` naming why — and drafts 1 and 3 are **gone in the same commit**. A Writer dispatched to chapter 3 cannot find them.
   8a. **A crew definition, not an agent.** Set that Editor role to `agents: 3`. Three editor agents (executions of the one Editor employee kind) read the same three drafts inside one run and the role returns **one** selection, one verdict set, one `selection_note` — the loop sees a single report, and the crew panel shows the three nested under the run (D-38, S-7). When two of them prefer draft 1, the note says so ("2 of 3 preferred draft 1; selected 2 on the `must_happen` rule") rather than hiding the split. No Work Item is created for any of them, and no vote appears anywhere in the engine.
9. **Going in circles.** Make C-4 deliberately unfixable: two plotlines that contradict, and the crews keep working around the contradiction instead of through it. After `flat_rounds`, the Story moves to **`pending` with `pending_kind: stalled`** — a reason naming chapters 6–9 and the finding that will not close. The sweep stops touching it. The Author unlocks the plotlines, settles the contradiction, re-locks, and the Story returns to `ready` by itself.
10. **The kinds of waiting.** Put three Stories in the same list: one awaiting a lock, one **drained**, one stalled. They must be distinguishable without reading text (U-1b, U-1c, L-34) — this is the screen the Author lives in, and the first two are both "the loop is finished with something" while the third is "the loop is stuck".
11. **Drained comes first.** With the dial on `critical` + `major`, keep running phase ③. Eventually a full pass finds no task, no `unconfirmed` finding, no `competing` revision, and a fresh round of Critics produces nothing at or above `major` — while 12 `minor` and 4 `taste` findings sit open in the queue. The Story goes **`pending` with `pending_kind: drained`** and dispatches nothing (L-37). The row says what stopped ("no critical or major left · 16 findings awaiting you") and offers exactly two moves (U-1c). Approving one `minor` finding returns it to `ready` and the crew moves within a sweep; leaving it alone keeps it drained.
    11a. **Drained is not stuck.** Set `flat_rounds` low enough that this Story's flat signature would have tripped loop detection, and confirm it reads `drained` and not `stalled` (L-37a). Then raise the dial to `critical` only and watch the same Story drain sooner — the threshold that decides what counts as work is the same switch that decides when the crew stops (D-35b).
    11b. **Completion is a decision, not an empty list.** Every hard criterion passes, the Story is drained, and 16 findings plus 9 Ideas are still open. The loop proposes `completed`, and the prompt states what accepting will discard (L-25, L-25b, U-14). The Author accepts; the book is done, the leftovers close with a count against them, and the sweep stops touching it. **If the loop refuses to propose completion because the list is not empty, the build is wrong** — "nothing left to criticise" is a bar no book clears. **If it proposes completion before the Story is drained, the build is wrong in the other direction**: the crew stopped while it still had authorised work.

If step 8 feels like the Author's taste being extended rather than replaced, and step 9 feels like a helpful pause rather than a failure, the topology is right and the rest is more stages. If the Editor's pick is wrong more than half the time, fix `selection_rules` before building anything else — **that text is the product**.

---

## 16. Phase ① research pipeline (2026-11-23 — the LEGACY `'sampling'` mode since 2026-11-24; a new Story runs §17)

This section is normative and replaces the classic phase-① loop wherever the two disagree (§16.8 names the passages). It describes the pipeline the package ships: no flag selects it, and no classic path remains — a phase-`summary` Story always runs this order.

**D-40** **Phase ① is one pipeline: structure → research → sample → screen → select → deep review → refine.** The Seed is Author truth and is never edited (P5); the Editor writes a DERIVED `SeedBrief`; the Researcher gathers sourced `Knowledge`; N tool-less one-turn Writer samples expand the brief; M tool-less Critics screen each sample; the **engine** selects the least-issue sample and truncates the rest; the selected revision gets the tool-using deep review, the Editor's verification and the Story's auto-approve dial; the approved fix tasks arm the next sampling round, whose prompt is compiled from the selected draft plus those tasks. The pipeline's ladder is fixed: **no sample is taken before an `accepted` Seed Brief for the current Seed**, and **no sample is taken before that brief's research pass has closed — unless the Editor waived the pass** by accepting the brief with an empty `research_questions` list (D-51: the waiver is a stated editorial decision on the brief, never a heuristic about the title or the genre, and never an omitted field).

### 16.1 Order, job kinds and claim keys

| # | job kind         | claim key                              | employee   | priority | template                     | offered tools | gate before it may be offered |
| - | ---------------- | -------------------------------------- | ---------- | -------- | ---------------------------- | ------------- | ----------------------------- |
| 1 | `structure_seed` | `wi_structure_seed_<seed_hash>`        | Editor     | 0        | `editor-seed-structure` (bound explicitly) | full | no accepted `seed_briefs` row whose `seed_hash` is the current Seed's |
| 2 | `research`       | `wi_research_<brief.rev>`              | Researcher | 0        | `researcher-story`           | full | an accepted brief for the current Seed exists, its `research_questions` list is NOT empty (an empty list WAIVES the pass — D-51, §16.9), and no CLOSED run names the key |
| 3 | `sample_unit`    | `wi_sample_<summary>_<round>_<attempt>` (wave 0), `wi_sample_<summary>_<round>_w<wave>_<attempt>` (wave ≥ 1) | Writer     | 2        | `writer-summary-sampler`     | none          | gates 1-2 closed — gate 2 counts as closed when the brief WAIVES research (D-51) — the round is ARMED (§16.7), fewer than `summary_sampling` drafts exist, the CURRENT WAVE's attempt count is under the cap, no live sampler |
| 4 | `screen_samples` | `wi_screen_<revision>_<attempt>`       | Critic     | 2        | `critic-screening`           | none          | the sample is `drafted` AND is not a draft of a round the loop has already chosen past (D-52 — that screen could never be answered), fewer than `summary_critics` reviews exist, the attempt count is under the cap, no live screen on that sample |
| 5 | `check_unit`     | `wi_review_<selected>_a<pass>`         | Critic     | 2        | `critic-genre` …, rotated     | full          | a `selected` Summary revision exists whose `round` is the last COMPLETED round (`stories.sampling_round`), and that pass has never run |

`<seed_hash>` is the FNV-1a hash of `stories.idea` (`src/shared/seed-hash.ts`), so an Author edit changes it and re-arms job 1 while every later stage waits for the new brief; `<brief.rev>` is the brief row's revision; `<round>` is 1-based; `<attempt>` is the 1-based ATTEMPT, not the revision count, so a failed answer (which persists no row) is retried under the next key.
