- **Selection is not a Work Item.** There is no `choose_sample` job and no `select` job in phase ①: the engine's `settleJob` and every sweep call the deterministic selector directly (§16.6). `seed_review` is not in `WORK_ITEM_KINDS` either — it was replaced by `structure_seed`.
- **No Reader runs a phase-① review.** The review crew is the screening Critic, the tool-using deep-review Critic, and the Editor. Readers keep phases ② and ③. (A `groups`-mode Story — every Story created since 2026-11-24, so not this section — additionally runs one Reader pass over the installed Overview, D-65 §17.14. It is not a review: it files no finding, opens no task, submits no verdict, and no gate ever waits on a score (only an in-flight run counts, as an open thread; D-65 §17.14 states the mechanics).)
- **`confirm_finding` is dormant.** The kind stays in `WORK_ITEM_KINDS` and `confirmFinding` stays in the catalogue and in §8, but no job generates either now, and the old phase-① `critic_confirmed` gates are gone: the Editor's verification of the deep review's findings is the acceptance that arms the next round.
- **The Goal is unchanged.** Phase ①'s level is still C-14 and C-15 and no new criterion was added; the Seed Brief is an input, never the contract C-14 measures against (the raw Seed is).

### 16.2 Records

New collections are APPENDED to `COLLECTION_NAMES`; an older snapshot loads with all three empty and no §4 name is renamed or dropped.

#### `knowledge` — the Researcher's artifact

| field        | type            | notes |
| ------------ | --------------- | ----- |
| `story_id`   | text, required  | |
| `phase`      | enum, optional  | absent = applies to the whole book |
| `kind`       | enum            | `fact`, `world_rule`, `tech`, `period`, `place`, `person`, `other` |
| `topic`      | text            | normalised subject key; part of the dedupe fingerprint |
| `claim`      | text            | one checkable sentence |
| `detail`     | text, optional  | what a writer needs to know, including uncertainty |
| `importance` | enum            | `critical`, `high`, `normal`, `low` — only the first two reach a sampling prompt (§16.5) |
| `sources`    | list            | `[{url_or_title, claim, accessed_at}]`; at least one, T-10 |
| `fingerprint`| text            | stable hash of (story, normalised topic, normalised claim) — D-16's dedupe, reused |
| `seen_count` | integer         | a repeat bumps it and refreshes the sources instead of opening a row |
| `author`     | text            | the Researcher role name/run that last wrote it (P11) |

#### `seed_briefs` — the Editor's derived Seed

| field            | type           | notes |
| ---------------- | -------------- | ----- |
| `story_id`       | text, required | |
| `seed_hash`      | text           | the hash of the Seed this brief was derived from; a mismatch counts as NO brief |
| `logline`, `protagonist`, `want`, `obstacle`, `stakes`, `setting`, `promise` | text | the structural reading of the Seed |
| `need`           | text, optional | the gap between want and need, when the Seed implies one |
| `world_rules`    | list           | restatements of the Seed, never inventions |
| `tone_hints`     | text, optional | voice, register, point of view the Seed implies |
| `constraints`    | list           | what the story may not do |
| `open_questions` | list           | where the Seed is silent; a legitimate answer, never a filled-in blank |
| `research_questions` | list, optional | **D-51.** The Editor's research decision, written by `structureSeed` (which REQUIRES the arg): the world facts a draft cannot get from the Seed alone. `[]` WAIVES phase ①'s research leg — no `wi_research_*` Work Item is generated and sampling is not gated on one; a non-empty list scopes the dispatched Researcher's pass to exactly those questions. An ABSENT field is not a waiver: it is a row written before the field existed, and `loop/records.ts:researchGate` reads it as `needed`. Only this field decides — never the title, the genre or the prose |
| `status`         | enum           | `draft`, `accepted` — one accepted brief per Story; a new hash supersedes the old in the same commit |
| `notes`          | text, optional | |

#### `sample_reviews` — one screening answer

| field         | type           | notes |
| ------------- | -------------- | ----- |
| `story_id`    | text, required | |
| `revision_ref`| text           | the ONE sample this review judges (D-21 keeps its siblings out of the run) |
| `critic`      | text           | the Critic role name that produced it |
| `issues`      | list           | one object per defect: `severity` (`critical` or `high` — the schema closes it), `criterion`, `location`, `quote`, `detail`, optional `fix_hint` |
| `valid`       | boolean        | `false` would mean the repair retry still failed; a failed screen currently persists NO row, so every stored review is `valid: true` |

These are **not** findings. They never enter the D-33/D-34 ladder, cannot block a Writer, and exist to rank the round's samples and to seed the deep review.

#### Additions to existing records

`revisions` gains `origin` (`sampled` in round 1, `refined` from round 2), `round` (1-based), `prompt_hash` (the compiled prompt's hash) and `issue_counts` (`{critical, high}`, written by the selection). `stories` gains `summary_sampling`, `summary_critics`, `sampling_history` (one line per completed round: each sample's `revision_id`, `critical`, `high`, `valid`, plus `selected`) and `sampling_wave` (the retry generation of the round in flight — absent = wave 0; bumped by the sweep when the Author clears a sampling-failure escalation, D-50).

**D-41** The three collections and the two record additions are additive; no `pipeline` flag was shipped and no existing Story needs a migration. §4's preamble permits added fields; nothing here renames or drops one.

### 16.3 The Researcher — the fifth employee

**D-42** `researcher` is an **employee kind**, Rank A like the other four (`ROLE_KINDS` / `STORY_AGENT_RANKS`). Its template is `researcher-story` (`serves_phase: ALL_PHASES`, `web: true`, `attempts: 1`, `max_concurrent: 1`): it researches the period, place, technology and facts the Seed Brief depends on and writes one `writeKnowledge` per claim — or, when the questions the job named need no fact from outside the book, closes the pass with `reportNoResearch` (D-51). It never writes a story artifact, and a write that reaches for one is refused naming **P10**.

Its §7 row (T-1c): read the Seed and every story artifact; **write `knowledge`**; write `tasks` (it may file the fix it cannot do, D-11); READ `proposals`, `verdicts`, `findings` and `ideas` (a proposal or a judgement is an opinion about a story artifact, which the Researcher does not make; T12/F2); read `seed_briefs` and `sample_reviews`; `recommend` on `sub_agent_runs`. `GET /agents` projects a fifth row, and the roster is answered ALPHABETICALLY (Critic · Editor · Reader · Researcher · Writer) — the route sorts the harness projection, whose catalogue order appends the Researcher last (T12/F1).

**D-43** The knowledge surface is its own group, registered on **every** session like the rest of the catalogue (T-6a): `structureSeed` (editor, writes `seed_briefs`; requires `research_questions` — D-51), `writeKnowledge` (researcher, writes `knowledge`), `readKnowledge` (every kind, bounded to 40 rows per T-7), `retireKnowledge` (researcher, marks an entry `low` and appends the `(retired)` marker rather than deleting it — P11) and `reportNoResearch` (researcher only, D-51: closes a dispatched pass that found nothing to record — see §16.9). §8.7 has the argument table; T-1c and §7's rows decide who may call what.

### 16.4 The tool-less execution mode — a named exception to T-6a

**D-44** **The catalogue stays uniform; the run's offered list does not.** T-6a's principle is unchanged — every session is built with §8.1–§8.5 and §8.7, and `kind` selects the brief and the permission row — but a run may be dispatched with `toolMode: 'none'`, which is an **execution mode**: the run is offered an EMPTY tool list (the transport sends no `tools` at all), receives no tool context, and answers one assistant turn under a `response_format` contract. No permission is widened or narrowed by the mode; it is a subtraction at dispatch, and only phase ①'s `sample_unit` and `screen_samples` use it.

The transport, precisely:

- The schemas are **plain JSON Schema object literals** in `src/shared/schemas/` (`SummarySample`: premise, promise, theme, tone, ending, world_rules, knowledge_used; `IssueList`: `issues[]` whose `severity` is the enum `critical`/`high`). There is no TypeBox dependency and no schema builder.
- The request asks for `response_format: {type: 'json_schema', json_schema: {name, strict, schema}}`. If the provider rejects the field with a 4xx naming `response_format`/`json_schema`, the client retries ONCE with `{type: 'json_object'}`; the prompt still states the contract's shape (the Writer's role prompt names every field), so the downgrade is not blind. A second failure fails the run with its `end_reason`.
- The answer is parsed and validated **in process** against the same literal. There is no streaming repair (the client is non-streaming): a bad answer costs exactly ONE repair retry — the bad text is echoed back with the validator's message and path, and the model re-emits the complete object. Two provider calls are the whole budget; a second failure fails the run and persists nothing.
- The run row is created and closed exactly like any other (`spawning` → `working` → `torn_down`/`failed`), and the request/answer pair is retained in the thread log. It opens NO live engine thread, because none exists; the employee tile still counts it as one working thread, because `GET /agents` merges the harness's live engine threads with the live run rows (`src/server/run/story-engine.ts:StoryHarnessManager.mergeJournaledRuns`, keyed `run_row:<runId>`), and it also appears in the run rows and in the retained thread history.

### 16.5 Sampling and screening mechanics

**D-45** **The prompt is compiled, deterministic and byte-stable.** `src/server/run/phase1-prompt.ts` is the one compiler. Its system turn is the template role prompt plus the output contract (the schema name), identical for every sample of every round; its user turn is, in order, the **Seed Brief**, the **`critical`/`high` Knowledge** (sorted by importance rank then `fingerprint`; `normal`/`low` never enter), and — from round 2 — the **BASE REVISION** (the previous round's selected draft) and the **FIX TASKS** (open `fix` rows, sorted priority descending then id). Nothing in the text reads a clock, a run id or a revision id; the round number lives in headings only. The hash is `sha256(system + "\n\n--phase1--\n\n" + user)`, first 16 hex characters, and lands on the run row's `brief_hash` and the revision's `prompt_hash`, which is how the Author can prove two samples shared a prefix (P11). Diversity is the provider `seed`, taken from the sample's 1-based `sampleIndex`; no `temperature` is sent. The screening prompt shares the same Seed Brief and Knowledge sections and names ONE draft's fields (never its revision id); each sample's screening batch shares one system turn.

- **One structured turn per run.** `summary_sampling` (standard 3) is the number of samples a round owes; each is its own one-turn run, and each answer becomes a `drafted` revision in the commit that closes it (`origin: sampled` in round 1, `refined` later; `round` set). Each sample's `summary_critics` (standard 5) screening runs each become one `sample_reviews` row in the commit that closes them.
- **They run one at a time per family.** The sweep offers ONE sample of the in-flight round and refuses a second while a live sample run of the CURRENT WAVE exists (`wi_sample_<summary>_<round>_…`, or `…_w<wave>_…` after a re-arm), so a round's N samples are N sequential one-turn runs; screening offers one owed pass per drafted sample and holds only that sample's family. `max_concurrent` on the two templates is a ceiling, never the count (D-38a).
- **The attempt caps are implicit and load-bearing.** A round may dispatch at most `summary_sampling + 3` sampling runs PER WAVE and each sample at most `summary_critics + 3` screening runs (`records.ts:samplingCapOf`/`screeningCapOf`). A failed answer persists nothing, so the counter — not the row count — is what allows a retry; the cap is what stops a broken provider buying a run per sweep forever.
- **Total sampling failure escalates (P7), and the escalation has a real recovery (D-50).** When the in-flight round still has NO revision after its cap, the sweep opens one `escalation` task owned by the Author (title prefix "Phase ① sampling failed after", reused rather than duplicated across sweeps) saying the provider is not returning valid `SummarySample` JSON; its `detail` records the wave it was opened at (`wave: <n>`). The spent attempts stay in the run rows, so "clear this row and retry" cannot work by itself — the recovery is a WAVE: once the row is no longer `open` (the Author's clear-pending makes it `wontfix`, an answered directive makes it `done`) and the `wave: <n>` in its detail still equals `stories.sampling_wave`, the sweep bumps `sampling_wave` by one in a commit. The sample claim prefix then becomes `wi_sample_<summary>_<round>_w<wave>_`, the attempt counter starts at 1 again, and the round samples afresh; the recorded-wave guard makes the bump happen once per cleared escalation. Changing `summary_sampling` upward also re-arms without a wave (the cap moved), which is the second half of the row's own advice.
- **Screening exhaustion degrades to a partial-review selection and escalates once (D-50).** A sample that cannot collect its `summary_critics` valid reviews inside the cap counts as SCREENED (`records.ts:isFullyScreened`: full set, or attempts spent). The round therefore selects on the reviews it has — possibly none — instead of stalling forever, and the selection commit opens ONE escalation titled `Phase ① screening incomplete in round <n> — selection proceeded with partial screening` (`owner_role: 'author'`), whose detail names the sample(s), the cap, and states that selection proceeded. Screens have no wave: the degrade is their recovery.

**D-46** **The engine is what writes the sample.** No tool call persists a sample or a screening answer: the engine reads the run's validated structured answer and writes the `revisions` / `sample_reviews` row itself, on the same named-exception principle as D-12a's Digest (P10's second exception). The run row, the template and the assignment are what attribute the prose to the Writer.

### 16.6 Selection and truncation

**D-47** **Selection is a deterministic engine settlement, not a model call and not a Work Item.** It runs after every `sample_unit`/`screen_samples` settlement and once per sweep (so a restart that died between the last screen and the selection still finishes the round). It fires only when: the in-flight round `R = (stories.sampling_round ?? 0) + 1` has at least one revision; no revision of `R` is already `selected`; at least one revision is `drafted`; the round OWES NO MORE SAMPLES — `roundRevisions(R).length >= summary_sampling`, OR the CURRENT WAVE's sample attempts reached `summary_sampling + 3` (degrade to the survivors only once sampling is genuinely exhausted, never while more samples are owed), OR the round's sampling LEG has closed (D-52: the cap is spent, or the round STARTED and the arming rule turned it off mid-flight — `loop/records.ts:samplingLegClosed`); every `drafted` revision is SCREENED — its full `summary_critics` set, OR its screening attempts reached `summary_critics + 3` (the partial-selection degrade of §16.5); and no live sample/screen run remains.

The ordering is fixed and there is no score (P6): **fewest `critical` → fewest `high` → fewest total → the earliest sample** (creation order; ties in that order fall back to id).

In ONE commit it then (opening the Author's `Phase ① screening incomplete in round R …` escalation first when any drafted revision was screened only partially, per §16.5):

1. installs the winner's **six prose fields** — `premise`, `promise`, `theme`, `tone`, `ending`, `world_rules` — on the Summary, and **never `chapter_count`** (Author truth, D-3b);
2. marks the winner `selected` and records its `issue_counts`;
3. appends the round's line to `stories.sampling_history` BEFORE any delete, so the audit can name every competitor;
4. **hard-deletes** every other revision of the round and every `sample_reviews` row pointing at one (P13/D-22's truncation, as the request asked) — and, since D-52, every `drafted`/`competing` Summary draft of an EARLIER round too: a draft that was still being screened when an earlier selection ran escaped that commit's truncation, and nothing else ever removes it. The retirement takes the finding that named ONLY a retired draft with it (`discarded`) and the open task that finding queued (`wontfix`), so a defect about a text the Summary no longer holds cannot hold the sign-off; a finding naming no draft, or a draft that survives, is left alone (D-53);
5. marks every open `fix` task `done` — the round's prompt carried it, so the resample IS its answer — and closes each finding it named, still `confirmed`/`approved`, as `fixed` (the rule a classic fix pass applies). A resample that did not fix the defect re-files the same fingerprint on the new draft, which L-16a reads as a regression rather than trusting this write;
6. advances `stories.sampling_round` to `R`.

The losing rows are gone from the store. `sampling_history` is the stored audit line; the Story view can still RENDER a deleted loser because the journal kept the delete payload and the projection marks it `discarded` — a reading surface, not a surviving row (P11).

**D-48** **The round counter and its resets.** `stories.sampling_round` means "rounds completed"; the in-flight round is that number plus one. A backtrack to `summary` and an Author `editSeed` both scrap every revision and reset `sampling_round` to 0, `sampling_history` to empty and `sampling_wave` to 0, because all three describe samples the same commit deleted. Without the reset the reopened phase would sample "round N+1" against a base revision that no longer exists.

### 16.7 The refinement gate, the deep review, and the stops

**D-49** **Round 2+ is armed by approved work, not by a calendar.** Round 1 samples as soon as gates 1-2 are closed. A refinement round is offered only when BOTH hold: at least one `fix` task is `open`, and no `critical`/`major` finding is still `unconfirmed` (an unverified complaint is not an instruction — D-33). Its prompt carries the previous round's selected draft and those tasks (§16.5). If the Story's dial does not cover the confirmed costs, no task is created; the Story waits at `pending`/`finding_approval` until the Author approves one (D-34).

**D-52** **The arming rule gates NEW samples; it never strands a round that already sampled.** A refinement round's leg can close under itself — a review files an unconfirmed `critical`/`major` while the round's first draft is still being screened, and D-49's arming rule then refuses the next sample. Two facts are read separately, from the ledger, and deliberately at DIFFERENT scopes: the cap arm counts the CURRENT WAVE's sample attempts (`records.ts:sampleAttempts` at `stories.sampling_wave`, the re-arm contract of T12/E), while the started arm asks the ROUND question wave-agnostically (`records.ts:samplingRoundStarted` counts any `wi_sample_<summary>_<round>_*` key via `sampleRoundPrefixOf`, current wave or not — a wave bump is a retry of the same round, so the drafts made before it are still this round's and a started round that cannot close would strand them). the round had NOT started (D-49's release stands and the round is not selected), and the round HAD started (its sampling leg is closed, its drafts are screened and reviewed, and the selection proceeds on the drafts that exist). Refusing the second case is what stranded a round: §16.6's sample-set arm was never satisfied, the sign-off was gated on a selection that could not happen, and the loop waited on work it had itself stopped buying. Screening keeps running for the active round's drafts regardless of arming — what the leg closing cancels is a NEW sample, not the passes an existing draft owes — and the sign-off is still gated on D-33 (the unconfirmed finding holds the lock) and the next round is still gated on D-49, so this is a shorter round, not a weaker gate.

**D-53** **A finding belongs to ONE draft, and the loop may be sent at it only so many times.** Phase ① reads a finding's owner from `related_refs` (`loop/records.ts:findingRevisionScope`), and the answer is computed once for the sign-off's blocking set, the verification queue, the drain probe and the selection's retirement (D-52 step 4), so no two gates can disagree about which draft a complaint describes:

- **`selected`** — it names the draft the Summary row carries. Normal work: it blocks, and it is verified.
- **`superseded`** — every ref names a draft this Story has already had (the revision row, or the round's `sampling_history` line, still names it) and none of them is the carried draft. The defect was written about a text the Summary no longer holds: it stays `unconfirmed` on the record for the Author, and it neither blocks a gate nor buys another verification pass, because the pass re-reads the KEPT draft and can never confirm or dispute it.
- **`artifact`** — it names no draft, or names something that is not a Summary draft (the Summary row itself, a criterion, a chapter). L-11a's whole-Story shape keeps the blocking weight it always had; it is never silently dropped, and an unknown id counts as `artifact`, never as `superseded`.

Because that reading is the only attribution a finding carries, phase ① ASKS FOR IT AT FILING: `raiseFinding` on a Summary target, in phase `summary`, once a selection has installed a draft, is refused naming **D-33** (`field: 'related_refs'`) unless `related_refs` names the carried revision — with the id it wants in `expected`, and a distinct sentence when the refs name only set-aside drafts. The three shapes the rule is NOT about stay free: a `stories`-targeted whole-Story finding, a chapter/plotline finding, and a filing before the round's first selection.

And the queue is BOUNDED. Phase ① mints `wi_verify_finding_<id>_<n>` — one anchored family per finding, the trailing `_` so `finding_1`'s budget is never spent by `finding_10` — and stops offering passes once the family reached **`FINDING_VERIFY_CAP`** (one pass plus `SAMPLING_RETRY_ALLOWANCE` retries, 4 today). A verification run that closes without deciding anything leaves the finding `unconfirmed`, and an unconfirmed finding is a D-33 hold, so an uncapped queue bought the same question on every sweep forever: never drained, never stalled, never explained. On the sweep after the budget is spent the loop opens ONE Author escalation — `Phase ① verification undecided for finding <id> …`, `owner_role: 'author'`, `finding_ref` set, and state **`blocked`** rather than `open`, because an `open` row counts as dispatchable work and would leave the Story `ready` with nothing to run, while a `blocked` row is the shape the decision tree renders as "the crew asked you" (P7, L-36). The sweep closes the row (`done`) as soon as the finding is not stalled any more — the Editor answered it, the Author decided it, or the round retired the draft that carried it. The closing is NOT phase-scoped even though the opening is: `findingsVerifyStalled` returns nothing outside phase ①, so a row whose finding gets decided after the Story moves on can only be retired by a close pass that still runs. And the stalled list excludes two findings beyond the superseded-draft case: one whose last bought verification pass is still live (`records.ts:verifyFindingLive` — the budget counts dispatched runs, so opening the Author's question over the head of an Editor run that has not answered would buy a decision one commit late) and one scoped to a set-aside draft. `records.ts:findingsVerifyStalled` is the one list both halves read, so generation and escalation cannot disagree about which findings stalled. The reuse lookup that keeps the row at ONE per finding matches its `finding_ref`, and the title prefix it matches on is anchored with the ` —` separator: `finding_1` is a text prefix of `finding_10`, and a bare id prefix would hand one finding's question to another finding's sweep.

**Deep review.** The selected revision of the round that just completed gets `summary_critics` tool-using Critic passes, one per `wi_review_<selected>_a<pass>` key, rotating over the four built-in angles (`critic-genre`, `critic-plausibility`, `critic-audience`, `critic-mystery`). These runs hold the whole catalogue and may use the web; their findings — at any cost — go to one `verify_findings` Editor job PER finding (`wi_verify_finding_<id>_<n>`), which binds `editor-general` because it is the FIRST Editor template in `DEFAULT_ASSIGNMENT` — a job that names no template takes the first assigned template of its role kind. The dial then auto-approves what it covers, and the approved finding's `fix` task is what arms the next round.

**Sign-off.** Phase ①'s `phase_signoff` is offered only when the Goal passes AND all of: no competing revision (D-52: a Summary draft of a round the loop has already chosen past is not competition — it is a leftover, and it must not hold the lock forever), no `pending` draft, no sourced Idea awaiting verification, no unconfirmed `critical`/`major` finding (D-53: a finding about a set-aside draft is not this round's work), no phase-① `fix`/`rewrite`/`merge` task queued, no `critical`/`major` finding open on the selected revision, every deep-review pass the selected revision OWES closed (the debt exists only for the revision that IS the current selection — `records.ts:isDeepReviewSelection` reads the same condition the `wi_review_*` queue does, so an older-round draft picked from the Summary pane cannot owe passes the loop will never dispatch and hold the lock on a debt nobody can pay), the phase not yet locked, and the Ideas tray empty (L-7a). The OFFER list above is still exactly what the sweep requires, and 2026-11-26 changed the ACT and not the offer: signing sets `summary_locked`, moves the phase, mirrors `summaries.locked`, and empties the tray (D-36) — and now ALSO voids this Summary's open findings and closes the queue's `open`/`claimed` tasks as `wontfix` in that same commit (`loop/types.ts:OPEN_TASK_STATES`; a `blocked` row is an Author question rather than a dispatchable job, so it is left where it is), on a `'sampling'` book exactly as on a grouped one (a book with no Summary Phrase row sweeps only its unscoped claims, which are the legacy whole-Overview meaning anyway), and returns the counts on `lock_receipt` (§6.3a). Whether that act also opens Phrase ② turns on the OTHER dial, not on this section's Summary mode: the second commit runs wherever `plotlines_mode` is `'groups'`, and is skipped — reported on the receipt, never refused — where it is not (D-78).

**Drain.** The phase-① drain preconditions add the pipeline's own stages to L-37's four: an accepted Seed Brief for the CURRENT Seed; a closed research pass for that brief; no live sample/screen; the in-flight round either complete (selected, so no `drafted` sample is left) or deliberately not owed (the round is only "owed" while it is armed, and per D-52 a round whose leg has CLOSED is owed its selection, not further samples); and every deep-review pass the selected revision owes closed (`records.ts:isDeepReviewSelection` — a selection from an earlier round owes none, and the drain must not name work the queue cannot offer). A round that degraded (spent sampling cap, spent screening cap) counts as complete once its selection committed. Drain is still tested before stalled and wins (L-37a).

**Removed from the loop, kept in the vocabulary.** `confirm_finding` (kind) and `confirmFinding` (tool) are no longer dispatched at all — phase ① or otherwise — and `critic_confirmed` no longer gates a fix, a selection, a sign-off or the drain. The tool still exists and still behaves as §8 documents for any template that reaches for it.

### 16.8 Superseded passages

This section replaces, as phase-① rules:

- **§4.2 D-3d** — its "N Writer expansions, readers, an Editor applying `selection_rules`" describes phases ②/③ now; phase ①'s divergence is §16.5/§16.6.
- **§6.2 Loop S** — the classic crew order (Writer expands, Critics file Ideas, readers judge, an Editor chooses) is replaced by §16.1.
- **§6.2a and L-16/L-20 as applied to phase ①** — no `write_unit` and no Editor selection exist in phase ①.
- **§8.1 T-3c's "`editSummary` is the Writer's only write during phase ①"** — the shipped phase-① Writer runs hold no tools; the engine writes the sample (§16.5).
- **§14 items 9 and 10** — phase-① divergence is `summary_sampling`, and the Critic count and angles are settled for phase ① by §16.7.
- **§15 steps 1 and 1a** — the two-Writer/Editor-select walk is replaced by §16.1's order.
- **The 2026-11-22 classic phase-① build** — `wi_seed_review_*`, `writer-summary` with `attempts > 1`, per-draft `wi_read_*` passes, an Editor `select`, `wi_confirm_finding_*` and the `critic_confirmed` fix gate. None of them is dispatched for phase ① now; the seed review became `structure_seed`, the Editor's choice became the engine's selection, and the per-finding confirmation thread became the deep review + Editor verification.
- **§17 (2026-11-24) supersedes this whole section for a `summary_mode: 'groups'` book** — which is every Story created since that date. Superseded there: §16.1's sample/screen/select order, §16.5's sampling and screening mechanics, §16.6's deterministic selection and truncation, §16.7's owed-round passes and the plateau's Author escalation, and §16.2's `sampling_round`/`sampling_wave`/`sampling_history` counters. **Still in force under BOTH models**: §16.3 (the Researcher), §16.4 (`toolMode: 'none'` and its structured transport), the Seed Brief and the research gates (D-40/D-51 — the group pipeline reads the same `researchGate`), and the citation/evidence rules. Nothing in §16 is deleted: a legacy `'sampling'` book runs exactly these rules, and `GET /stories/:id`'s `sampling` projection is their audit.

### 16.9 New requirement ids

- **D-40** — phase ① is the research pipeline; its gates (brief → research → sampling) and its no-Reader review crew.
- **D-41** — the three collections and the `revisions`/`stories` additions are additive; no `pipeline` flag and no migration.
- **D-42** — the Researcher as the fifth Rank A employee kind, its template and its P10 refusal.
- **D-43** — the knowledge tool group, registered on every session.
- **D-44** — `toolMode: 'none'`: the named execution-mode exception to T-6a, the plain-JSON-Schema transport, the `json_object` fallback and the one repair retry.
- **D-45** — the compiled, byte-stable sampling prompt, the `seed`-only diversity, the sequential per-family dispatch, the attempt caps and the escalation.
- **D-46** — the engine persists each validated sample and screening answer (P10's D-12a-style exception).
- **D-47** — deterministic selection and the selection commit's six effects, including the fix-task consumption that closes findings.
- **D-48** — `sampling_round`/`sampling_history` and their reset on `editSeed` and a backtrack to `summary`.
- **D-49** — the refinement gate, the deep review's owed passes, and phase ①'s sign-off/drain preconditions.
- **D-50** — the round's STOP conditions and its two recovery paths (T12/C, D, E): selection waits for the full sample set unless the round's sampling attempts reached `summary_sampling + 3`; a draft counts as screened when its review set is full OR its screening attempts reached `summary_critics + 3` (partial selection, ONE `Phase ① screening incomplete in round <n>` escalation); a cleared sampling-failure escalation bumps `stories.sampling_wave`, which re-arms the round under `wi_sample_<summary>_<round>_w<wave>_<attempt>` (the recorded `wave: <n>` guard makes one bump per cleared row).
- **D-51** — phase ①'s research leg is gated on the Editor's explicit decision, not on a guess: `structure_seed` carries a REQUIRED `research_questions` array and `[]` waives the leg (no `wi_research_n` Work Item, no sampling gate); an absent field is a legacy row and keeps D-40's gate. A dispatched Researcher that finds nothing to record closes the pass with `reportNoResearch` — researcher-only, one required `rationale`, zero writes, and a REPORT (S-4 `no_op === false`) rather than a silent no-op. §16.9.1 states the rule.
- **D-52** — the arming rule gates NEW samples only. A round that started and was then disarmed (or spent its sample cap) has a CLOSED sampling leg (`records.ts:samplingLegClosed`), and its existing drafts are screened, reviewed and SELECTED rather than left stranded with the sign-off gated on a selection that could not run; a round that never started keeps refusing (D-49). The selection commit also retires the earlier rounds' leftover drafts, with the findings that named only them (`discarded`) and the open tasks those findings queued (`wontfix`); a set-aside draft is not competition and is not owed screening, so it holds neither the sign-off nor the drain.
- **D-53** — a finding's `related_refs` decide which draft it describes (`records.ts:findingRevisionScope`: `selected` / `superseded` / `artifact`), and one answer feeds the sign-off, the drain, the verification queue and the selection's retirement. A superseded finding stays on the record but blocks nothing and is never re-verified; the whole-Summary shape keeps its weight. `raiseFinding` asks for the carried revision at filing time (refused naming D-33 with the id in `expected`). The verify family is anchored per finding and capped at `FINDING_VERIFY_CAP` (1 + `SAMPLING_RETRY_ALLOWANCE`); past it the loop opens ONE `blocked` Author escalation ("the crew asked you", `finding_ref` set) and closes it when the finding leaves `unconfirmed`.

#### 16.9.1 D-51 — the research waiver and the "nothing to research" report (normative)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Waiver** | The Editor's accepted Seed Brief owns the research decision. `research_questions: []` waives the phase ① research leg: gate 2 of §16.1 is closed by definition, no `wi_research_n` is queued, and the phase ① drain clause counts the brief as satisfied on its own. A non-empty array keeps D-40's research-before-sampling ordering exactly as written and scopes the dispatched pass to exactly those questions (the Work candidate's `note` carries them to the Researcher). Only the field decides — never the title, the genre or the prose. |
| **No silent waiver** | `structure_seed` REQUIRES the arg: omitting it is a T-11 refusal. An Editor that has not stated the decision cannot leave the run in the old always-research shape by accident, and "I don't know" has no encoding. |
| **Legacy** | A stored brief with no `research_questions` field predates this rule and reads as `needed` — D-40's original behaviour. Absence is never a waiver (D-5), so a brief written before the field existed cannot silently stop being researched. |
| **One predicate** | `loop/records.ts:researchGate()` returns `'waived'` / `'needed'` / `'closed'` and is the AUTHORITY on the rule: Work synthesis (§16.1 gates 2-3), the phase ① drain clause and the status decision tree all read it, and nothing may decide the question differently from it. The tool layer does not import the loop, so `tools/knowledge.ts:waivesResearch` is the one mirror — it must stay byte-equal to `records.ts:researchWaived` (`Array.isArray(...)` and empty, so a malformed stored value is NOT a waiver), because the `research_waived` field and the `next` line the Researcher reads back would otherwise tell the agent a decision the gate disagrees with. Pinned by the D-51 rows in `src/server/tools/runtime.test.ts`, which assert the payload and the gate against the SAME row. |
| **Report** | A Researcher that was dispatched and concludes the questions need no outside fact calls `reportNoResearch(rationale)` — researcher-only, ZERO writes (no Knowledge row, no task, no invented fact: P7), one required line naming what makes the premise self-contained. The run ends `torn_down` with `no_op === false` (S-4) and is closed for the brief's key, so the pass is not re-dispatched. Ending the pass silently instead is a counted no-op turn and is a defect. |
| **Panel** | The tool is BOTH a read tool (`READ_TOOLS`: it touches no record, so a D-38 panel agent answers it rather than proposing a write that does not exist) and a report tool (`REPORT_TOOLS`: it is how the run reports). The two sets answer different questions — "did this touch the store" vs "did this run report". |
| **Provenance** | D-41 still requires provenance for every fact that DOES reach sampling: waiving research removes the Work Item, never the requirement. |

---

## 17. The Summary Phrase — grouped authoring (2026-11-24, the model every new Story runs)

**Status: normative, and it supersedes §16 for a Story whose `stories.summary_mode` is `'groups'` — which is every Story created since this date (`loop/engine.ts:createStory`, §4.1).** §16 is not deleted and is not decorative: it stays the contract for a legacy `'sampling'` book (mode ABSENT means `'sampling'`, R6) and for the later-phase backend this redesign does not touch. Where the two sections disagree about phase ①, the Story's mode decides which one runs, and the ONE predicate that answers that question is `phrase/catalog.ts:isGroupManagedStory` — every guard in the loop, the tool layer and the routes reads it from there, and nothing may re-derive the answer from the presence of rows.

**Why the redesign happened, in one paragraph.** §16's whole-Summary round asks N Writers to rewrite all six prose fields end to end, then deletes N−1 of them (D-22). One sentence of criticism therefore costs a rewrite of everything; the deleted drafts survive only as a line in `sampling_history`; a "Revision 1 … N" rail cannot say *Tone is fixed and Premise still isn't*; and a Writer budget spent re-drafting a whole book cannot be spent on the part that is actually wrong. The grouped model keeps §16's gates, its evidence rules and its reading economy, and changes the **unit of the work**: the Summary is five named parts, each with its own revision line, installed on acceptance, reviewed as one vector, and answered by findings that name the parts they are about.

### 17.1 The requirement ids (R1–R7)

| id | the contract, in one line | where it lives |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **R1** | The Phrase catalog is **data**: the five groups, their titles, what each owes and the order they compose in live in `shared/phrase.ts`; only the Summary phrase is enabled — **superseded in part on 2026-11-26 (§18): the catalog now ENABLES `plotlines` as well, created by the Author's START instead of at `POST /stories`; the "catalog is data, nothing branches on a phrase or group name" half of this rule is what made that a catalog edit rather than a loop rewrite, and it stands unchanged**; `world_rules` is NOT a group. No store, loop or route branches on a group name — and nothing addresses a group BY name: a group's persisted `phrase_groups.id` (a UUID) is its address on the wire, in a finding's `group_refs`, in a run's `group_refs` and in a revision's `group_id`; the catalog key is display text for humans and prompts. | `shared/phrase.ts`, `phrase/catalog.ts:ensurePhraseCatalog` |
| **R2** | Every group has **its own revision line**. An install writes one new `latest` per group it wrote, archives what it replaces, and gives an untouched group neither a number nor a row. History is retained, never truncated. | `phrase/revisions.ts:installGroupRevisions` |
| **R3** | A finding **names its groups** (`group_refs`, §4.10) and closes only when every group it names has a revision claiming it. A Summary finding with no groups is refused on a grouped book, and the refusal's `fix` never offers "omit the list" there (D-61); closure is re-checked on every sweep for `approved` rows, on group books only (D-64). | `phrase/findings.ts`, `tools/reader.ts:raiseFinding`, the reconcile step at `loop/engine.ts:604-639` |
| **R4** | A live Writer pass **holds exactly the groups it is rewriting**: an overlapping batch is SHRUNK not cancelled, disjoint batches run together, and the hold is derived from live run rows and fails CLOSED. | `phrase/exclusion.ts`; `run/executor.ts` copies `group_refs` Work Item → Run row (§4.13) |
| **R5** | **No whole-Summary sampling and no selection.** An accepted answer installs immediately; `sample_unit`/`screen_samples`, the deterministic selector, the plateau's Author question and the Summary `select`/`merge`/`revise` routes do not run for a grouped book — and since the 2026-11-24 hardening neither does the crew's `selectRevision` aimed at the Summary, which answers `R5` with a `fix` naming `raiseFinding` + `group_refs` (D-61). Review is keyed by the **group vector**, so it re-arms by itself, and the drain and the sign-off read that vector rather than the sampling rows (D-62). | `loop/work.ts:phase1GroupCandidates`; the install at `loop/engine.ts:1445-1528`; the guards at `engine.ts:1606`, `1994`, `2073`; the tool refusal at `tools/editor.ts:selectRevision`; the owed reads at `phrase/exclusion.ts:groupPipelineOwed` + `loop/status.ts` |
| **R6** | Compatibility with no migration: `summary_mode` absent means `'sampling'`; §4.2's five prose fields are a **generated mirror** written in the install's commit — and written ONLY there: on a group book the crew's `editSummary` answers `R6` instead of writing the mirror by hand (D-61), while a `'sampling'` book keeps the tool exactly as it was; the legacy `sampling` projection stays readable; a legacy book becomes grouped only through the Author's explicit import. | `phrase/catalog.ts:importLegacySummary`, `api/projections.ts:phraseView`, `tools/writer.ts:editSummary` |
| **R7** | The final **role-instruction prose** is a separate, later task. The catalog's group `description` fields are GROUP DEFINITIONS the assembler quotes; the binding "rewrite / hold / rate" sentences belong to the prompt compiler so one place owns them for every batch, and the template prose stays generic. | `run/phase1-prompt.ts:compileGroupDraftPrompt` / `compileGroupReviewPrompt` |

### 17.2 Order, job kinds and claim keys

```
the Editor structures the Seed    (structure_seed)  → seed_briefs: one accepted Seed Brief     [§16, unchanged]
the Researcher gathers Knowledge  (research)        → knowledge: sourced, importance-triaged   [§16, unchanged]
one tool-less Writer pass         (draft_groups)    → group_revisions: a `latest` + an `archived` row PER GROUP it wrote
                                                      + phrases.content (the Overview) + §4.2's mirror  (R2/R5/R6)
one tool-less Reader score       (score_sections)  → summary_scores: FIVE rows, one per section — display-only (D-65)
N tool-less reading seats         (review_groups)   → sample_reviews rows carrying a GroupReview, every issue with group_refs
the deep review with tools        (check_unit)      → findings that MUST name their groups (R3)
one tool-less Editor lock seat    (group_lock)      → ONE group row's four lock fields, plus the claims whose whole scope just stopped being writable → `discarded`
                                                      optional, and ON only while `stories.editor_group_locking` is set (§17.18)
the Editor verifies               (verify_findings) → confirm / dispute                        [§16, unchanged]
the dial auto-approves            (D-35)            → a `fix` task, and the task's groups are what the NEXT batch is built from
```

| job | claim key | how many, and when |
| -- | -- | -- |
| `draft_groups` (Writer, `toolMode: 'none'`) | `wi_draft_groups_<summaryRef>_<group-set hash>_<attempt>` — the FAMILY is the group set, so a failed pass retries under the next attempt number and a restart reproduces the family exactly (L-3) | one per sweep while at least one group is owed, unheld, and inside its allowance; the batch is `max(1, stories.summary_sampling)` groups **cut BETWEEN finding-sets** — R4's exclusion defers a whole set that touches a held group, and D-66 forbids the dial from splitting one finding's set, so a defect naming three groups on a dial of 1 is still ONE pass (§17.6, §17.15) |
| `score_sections` (Reader, `toolMode: 'none'`) | `wi_summary_score_<vectorHash>` — the SAME vector the reading seats are keyed by, so an install re-arms the score by itself and no counter exists to drift (D-65) | **one per group vector, and only once the Overview is COMPLETE** (`isScorableSummary`: a group nobody has written yet means no score at all — the number judges how the sections fit together). It is offered at `priority: 2` with `rank: -1`, so under a concurrency ceiling of 1 it takes the first slot of the vector, ahead of the Critic seats. Its closed run spends the vector whether the answer landed or was refused — the same `runsForItem` rule the seats follow |
| `review_groups` (Critic, `toolMode: 'none'`) | `wi_review_groups_<vectorHash>_<pass>` | `stories.summary_critics` seats per **group vector**; one run per seat per vector, and a seat that already reported on this state is not bought again |
| `check_unit` (Critic, tools ON) | `wi_group_check_<vectorHash>_<pass>` | the same count, rotating the four deep-review Critic angles (`critic-genre`, `critic-plausibility`, `critic-audience`, `critic-mystery`); opens only once every structured seat for that vector has reported |
| `group_lock` (Editor, `toolMode: 'none'`) | `wi_group_lock_<cleanVectorHash>_<groupId>` — one seat per **GROUP** per CLEAN vector, keyed by the clean selection's hash exactly like the score seat, so a newer clean text re-arms it by itself and one section's answer can never spend another's (§17.18) | at most FIVE per clean vector, and ONLY while `stories.editor_group_locking` is ON (absent = OFF = this seat does not exist at all): every gate is in `loop/editor-lock.ts:editorLockEvidenceOf` — the Setting, a COMPLETE clean selection whose entry for this group IS the group's current text, a Reader score row for THIS group at THIS vector with a non-blank `rationale`, the group written and unlocked, no live Writer hold (`phrase/exclusion.ts:liveGroupWriters`, fail-closed), and no closed run already under the key. Offered at `priority: 2` with **`rank: 1`**, LAST in the class behind the score seat (-1) and the Critic seats (0): an unlocked section keeps being rewritten, which is the loop's healthy state, so its absence costs the least. **Optional work by construction** — `groupPipelineOwed` has no lock field and `GROUP_PIPELINE_RUN_PREFIXES` does not know the family, so no drain sentence, sign-off gate, hill-climb or goal ever waits on this seat |

The `draft_groups` job answers `GroupDraft` = `{groups: [{group_ref, content}], knowledge_used: []}`; a reading seat answers `GroupReview` = `{issues: [{severity: 'critical' | 'high', criterion, location, quote, detail, fix_hint?, group_refs: […]}], fix_verifications?: […]}`. `severity` keeps §16's two legacy words because every "blocking" predicate in the loop reads exactly that pair; `origin` is GONE from the review contract, because introduced-vs-inherited graded a CANDIDATE against a base, and a grouped answer is installed rather than compared. `group_refs` is required and non-empty **in process** as well as in schema: `parseGroupReviewAnswer` refuses an empty list and refuses a ref that is not one of the Story's groups, so an invented id is a refusal, not a dangling attribution.

The `score_sections` job answers `SectionScores` = `{sections: [{group_ref, score, rationale, evidence}]}` (`shared/schemas/section-scores.ts`) — the third no-tool answer contract of this pipeline, and the only one whose product is a number. `score` is a whole number 0–100; `rationale` is the one-line reason and `evidence` the quoted line the judgement rests on, both required (D-15 applies to a score exactly as it applies to a finding: a judgement with nothing behind it is not a judgement). The in-process validator ignores `minItems` and the range, so `phrase/section-scores.ts:parseSectionScoreAnswer` is where those rules actually live: it refuses, WHOLE, an entry naming a group the Story does not have, a group scored twice, a score that is not an integer in range, an entry with an empty `rationale` or `evidence`, and an answer that does not name every group EXACTLY once. A refused answer writes NO rows — three of five stored sections would put a number on text that was never judged. The entries come back in CATALOG order, taken from the Story's own group rows and not from the answer's order, so two identical answers journal identical rows.

The `group_lock` seat (R-lock, §17.18) answers `GroupLockDecision` = `{group_ref, lock, rationale}` (`shared/schemas/group-lock.ts`) — the pipeline's fourth no-tool answer contract, and the only one whose product is a FLAG on a row rather than a text, a review or a number. It carries **no score and no threshold field on purpose**: the schema is strict at its one level (`additionalProperties: false`, all three fields required, `minLength` on `rationale` for the provider's benefit), and the rule that `lock: true` needs two quotable halves lives in engine-owned prompt text (`run/phase1-prompt.ts:GROUP_LOCK_INSTRUCTION`) because JSON Schema cannot say it. `group_ref` is ONE id rather than a list — the seat's whole identity — and an answer naming a different group is REFUSED rather than re-attributed, because the judgement was made about the text that was on screen. The in-process validator ignores `minLength`, so `loop/editor-lock.ts:parseGroupLockAnswer` is where the real rules are: the answer must name the seat's own group, must be a boolean, and must carry a non-blank reason whichever way it went (D-15 applies to a `false` exactly as to a `true` — that sentence is what the rail shows when it asks why the loop kept rewriting). A refused answer, a stale seat and a domain refusal all write NOTHING, and the run row is the record either way.

**Which groups owe work** (`loop/work.ts:groupWorkTargets`, read from records so a restart reproduces it — L-3): a group with `revision: 0` (never written), or a group an open finding targets that no revision of THAT group resolves (`findings.ts:findingGroupsStillOwed`). A finding with no `group_refs` — possible only on a legacy row, since R3 refuses a new one — targets all five. That is the whole routing rule; §17.6 is what trims it.

### 17.3 What a grouped Story writes, and what it never writes

| written by the group path | never written by the group path |
| -- | -- |
| `group_revisions` (the new `latest`, plus the rows it archived), `phrase_groups.content` / `.revision` / `.updated_by`, `phrases.content` (the composition) and `phrases.revision` (bumped **once per install, not once per group**), §4.2's five mirror fields, `sample_reviews` rows carrying `GroupReview` issues, `summary_scores` rows written BY THE ENGINE from a `SectionScores` answer (D-65 — the crew has no score tool; the row is persisted in the settle commit, exactly as a screening answer becomes a `sample_reviews` row), `findings` (with `group_refs`), `tasks`, `runs` (with `group_refs`), and the ordinary Goal/verdict/ledger machinery | `revisions` rows for the Summary unit, candidate `sample_reviews` about a Summary draft, `stories.sampling_round`, `stories.sampling_wave`, `stories.sampling_history`, the accepted-revision plateau entries for the Summary, §4.2's `world_rules`, and `chapter_count` — D-3b stands: no Writer, grouped or otherwise, carries Author truth |

`GET /stories/:id` keeps projecting `sampling` as the legacy audit, and a grouped Story reads there with round 0 and no history. That projection is a reading surface for rows only a legacy book has — it is not a claim that a grouped Story has rounds.

### 17.4 The install: one commit, and the six things it does

1. one new `group_revisions` row **per group the batch actually wrote**, `number` = that group's own next number (R2);
2. the row each of those replaced flipped to `archived` — under a CAS on the group's `latest`, so exactly one current text per group survives an attempted concurrent install;
3. `phrase_groups.content` / `.revision` / `.updated_by` rewritten from the same text, in the same commit, so "what is the current premise" is one row read and cannot drift;
4. `phrases.content` recomposed from the group rows **as they now are** (`composePhraseContent`, §4.19) and `phrases.revision` bumped once;
5. §4.2's five prose fields written from the same texts through the identity mirror (R6) — a reader can never see a new group beside an old mirror;
6. **never** `chapter_count`, **never** `world_rules`; and if the Summary row is `locked`, step 5 is SKIPPED rather than failed — the install stands, the mirror waits for the Author to lift the lock, and `review.mirror: {summary_id, in_sync, locked, fields}` reports the difference (L-7a/P5).

**The independence example, normative (R2).** A Critic files a finding about the Promise's stakes. The batch rewrites Promise alone. Promise goes **revision 1 → 2**, the Overview recomposes, the mirror's `promise` moves — and **Premise, Theme, Tone and Ending are still at revision 1**, with their text, their number and their `updated_by` untouched, no new rows, no allowance spent, and their `writer_busy` flags never flipped. "One group's work never revises another's" is a store-level fact, not an instruction to a model.

### 17.5 The group vector, and why review re-arms by itself

The accepted state of a grouped Summary is `groupVectorHash`: sha256-16 over the sorted `<group id>:<number>` pairs, so the same set of texts in any order hashes the same, and **any install moves the hash**. Consequences that are normative:

- the reading seats and the deep review are keyed by the vector, not by a round number, so a new text re-arms exactly the review of THAT text and a restart reproduces the keys from the rows (L-3);
- a seat's job is the Overview as installed — its `group_refs` carry **all five** groups, because a defect that crosses groups must be able to name every group it crosses;
- the **scoring seat is keyed the same way** (D-65): `wi_summary_score_<vectorHash>` is one pass per vector, and the five rows it writes carry the hash read back off the CLAIM KEY (`exclusion.ts:sectionScoreVectorHashOf`), never one re-derived at settlement — a Writer install mid-pass must not re-stamp an old judgement onto a new book. The rows ACCUMULATE rather than overwrite, so the rail can show a section's history as well as its current number;
- the rail reports `review.vector_hash`, `reviews_on_current_vector` and `deep_reviews_on_current_vector` so "has anyone read what is on the record?" is a fact on the wire, not a guess.

### 17.6 Dispatch: exclusion, parallelism, and the two attempts (R4)

- **The hold is a predicate over rows, not a semaphore and not a lease.** `phrase/exclusion.ts:liveGroupSets` reads the live `runs` rows of the holding family (`GROUP_HOLDING_KINDS = ['draft_groups']`); the S-5 ceiling, the lease and L-2's expiry are untouched. Being clock-free, two engines asking in the same instant agree (L-5).
- **Fail closed.** A live holding run with no `group_refs` holds EVERY group: one deferred sweep costs less than two writers installing over each other and an archived revision nobody read.
- **Defers a whole set, never shrinks one (R4 + D-66).** `partitionGroupRefs` still splits a candidate batch into `{free, deferred}` — it is the read every caller shares — but since D-66 the Writer batch is cut by `phrase/exclusion.ts:planGroupDraftBatch`, which applies the rules in a stated ORDER: (1) a set that touches a held group is deferred WHOLE, which replaces the old shrink (offering `{tone}` when `{premise, tone}` was the owed set installed a text that answered half a defect, and D-66 forbids that state); (2) a set with a group outside its allowance (parked by the two-attempt cap) is withheld whole, so the queue does not pay for a partial pass over a defect it cannot finish; (3) `max(1, summary_sampling)` then caps BETWEEN sets — the first set of the sweep is always taken, however big it is, and the dial ration only the sets after it. Overlapping sets merge: two findings sharing one group become one pass over the UNION of their groups (a union-find over the shared finding ids, `findingGroupSets`), because two passes over the same group would spend the group's second attempt before the first text was read. A wholly held batch offers nothing, and no claim key is spent on an empty answer. **Disjoint sets run concurrently.**
- **Two attempts per TEXT — not a lifetime ceiling.** `GROUP_WRITE_ATTEMPT_CAP = 2`, counted over CLOSED holding runs whose JOURNAL POSITION is after the write that produced the group's current text: `writeBaselineOf` takes that text's `latest` revision row when one exists, and otherwise the LAST create-or-update event on the `phrase_groups` row. So a successful install MOVES the baseline and RESETS that group's count (and only that group — R2), which means a group that was rewritten cleanly and is criticised again has a fresh pair of attempts against the NEW text; a live run is not counted; a `review_groups` or deep-review run is never counted, because **reading the Overview does not spend the budget for rewriting it**; the pass that installed the current text predates its own revision row and so is not an attempt AGAINST it, which is why a freshly written group starts with its full pair in front of it; and a Seed reset (`engine.ts:editSeed`) re-baselines through the reset's own update event, so the scrapped Seed's passes are never charged to the new one. The baseline being a JOURNAL POSITION rather than a row id is load-bearing: `createdAfter` resolves ids through a row's CREATE event, which for a group row predates every write the allowance counts, and counting from that create after a reset would park every group and let an EMPTY Overview read as drained.
- **The escalation is the finding, not a new question — and a parked group is not owed.** When a group's allowance is spent the loop stops paying for that group and the finding stays open in the review rail with its attempts visible; the Author's moves are to decide the finding or rewrite the group by hand. The loop parks the group rather than asking the Author a second question about the same defect, and it never installs a least-bad text. `groupPipelineOwed` deliberately does NOT count a parked group: a cap that also held the drain would mean a provider that cannot answer the `GroupDraft` schema keeps the Story from ever reading `drained`, and the stall would look like a hang.

### 17.7 A finding's groups, and when one may close (R3)

- **attribution is checked against the book** before any row moves: an unknown group id is refused (never dropped), and a phase-① Summary finding with no `group_refs` on a grouped book is refused with the `key=id` list in the message — a finding that names none means "the whole Overview", and that is five groups rewritten to answer one sentence. The refusal's `fix` BRANCHES by book: a legacy whole-Summary Story is told it may omit the list (there, an empty list really does mean the whole Overview), while a grouped Story is told to name every id, because **a grouped Summary has no unscoped path to omit into** — closure is a join on the ids (`findings.ts:findingGroupScope`). The legacy D-33 rule that a phase-① Summary finding must name the draft it describes is skipped on a group book: there is no competing draft there, so the id that rule demands does not exist (R5);
- **a resolving revision is an explicit claim**: `finding_refs` on the `group_revisions` row, frozen at dispatch so a late task cannot claim an older text. Newest such row wins;
- **all-or-nothing closure**: `findingResolution` → `{targeted, resolved, missing, resolved_all}`, and `closeFindingWhenGroupsResolved` flips the finding to `fixed` only when `resolved_all` AND the row is `approved` (D-34: an unapproved row must not close over the Author's gate; auto-approve writes `approved` with `approved_by: 'auto'`, so covered costs close without a tap). It is idempotent, so a replayed install after a restart changes nothing;
- **a repeat sighting widens the scope** instead of filing a second claim (D-16's dedupe merges `group_refs` the way it merges `related_refs`);
- **the sweep reconciles what the installs owe, on group books only.** The planner cuts a batch off an OPEN finding (`groupWorkTargets` accepts D-33's `unconfirmed`/`confirmed` rows on purpose), so a pass can install the answer BEFORE any of the three approval funnels writes `approved` — the Editor's `approveFinding`, the engine's approval commit, the dial's auto-approve. The install-time close then correctly declines, and because no group still owes the finding the planner never dispatches again, so the row used to sit `approved` and unanswered forever. The sweep re-runs the same read every round (`engine.ts:604-639`, the `loop_reconcile_findings` step → `findings.ts:closeFindingAnsweredByInstalls`, which RE-READS the row rather than trusting the caller's pre-update copy) and closes the fix tasks that named the now-`fixed` finding, so a defect the book can already show as answered never holds L-37's drain hostage. The step is gated on `isGroupManagedStory` for an honest reason: with no phrase rows `findingResolution` answers `resolved_all: true` VACUOUSLY — nothing was ever in scope — and acting on that would delete a sampling round's fix work. A reconcile step rather than a write at each approval, because a crash between any funnel and the close has to self-heal anyway (L-3). **The rows that step cannot reach** (2026-11-25): a finding already `fixed` on an earlier settle, one the Author `discarded`, and one the Editor `disputed` each left their `fix` rows open on every book mode — the gate above only closes rows beside a close THIS pass made, and it never runs at all on a single-Summary book — while `records.ts:dispatchableTasks` admits any `kind: 'fix'` row, so the orphan held the drain ("N tasks still queued") with nothing dispatchable and no run live, and `generateWork` read an unserviceable row (`work.ts:openFixTaskOwnerEscalation`). The sweep's `loop_close_moot_fix_rows` leg (`engine.ts:641-716`) closes any open row whose `finding_ref` names a finding in `types.ts:MOOT_FIX_TASK_FINDING_STATES` (`fixed`, `discarded`, `disputed`), on every story mode, kind-agnostically and before work is generated (D2 fix; `loop/orphan-fix-rows.test.ts`).

**The multi-group example, normative.** A Reader files "the Ending resolves a confrontation the Premise never set up" with `group_refs: [premise, ending]`. It buys **one** `draft_groups` pass over both groups — on a dial of 1 as much as on a dial of 5, because D-66 caps batches and not defects (§17.15) — the install stamps `finding_refs: [that finding]` on **both** new revisions, listed ONCE per revision however many of its groups the batch rewrote (`loop/work.ts` dedupes before the install copies them), and the closure join is unchanged: `closeFindingWhenGroupsResolved` flips the row only when `missing` is empty. Since D-66 the loop never SCHEDULES half of a defect, so the partial state below is reachable only when an answer is refused or a group is parked — which is exactly when the join earns its keep. `findingGroupsStillOwed` still routes the missing groups into the next batch (`phrase/revisions.ts:parseGroupDraftAnswer` refuses an answer that skipped a group the job carried, so a Writer cannot install one of two; `loop/engine.ts` installs only when the parse says `ok`). (`integration/summary-groups.test.ts` is that example end to end, on a Story whose dial is 1.)

**The wider example: three groups, a batch of one, and a late approval.** On a book whose `summary_sampling` is 1, a finding naming THREE groups is still fixed by ONE pass — the three groups go out together under the claim key for the set `{premise, tone, ending}`, one settle writes three revisions, and the finding is `fixed` on that sweep. Before D-66 the dial cut that defect into three sweeps, and §4.10 has no "partly fixed" state to describe the middle of it: the Author's book spent two rounds showing a defect with one or two of its groups rewritten. Because the pass is planned off an OPEN finding (`groupWorkTargets` accepts D-33's `unconfirmed`/`confirmed` rows), the install can still land BEFORE an approval funnel writes `approved`, and then the install-time close declines (the state gate is exactly the field the funnel has not written yet), the planner has nothing left to dispatch, and the sweep's reconcile step closes the finding and its tasks one round later. Pinned by `loop/group-findings-batch-cap.test.ts` — the one-pass dispatch, the deduped claims, the refused partial answer, the late approval, and the vacuous legacy read that must not close.

### 17.8 Gates, sign-off, the drain, and the drain's honest limit

- **The HTTP lock gate is unchanged**: the Ideas tray first (L-7a/D-29), then the phase Goal (U-16). The redesign added no server-side blocker to that route and no new `pending_kind` — and it is still the WHOLE gate on 2026-11-26, when D-78 changed what the act DOES once those two gates open (§6.3a), not what it refuses on.
- **The desk adds the group truth to what it SHOWS** (U-19), from `story-contract.ts:groupSignOffBlockers`. Since 2026-11-26 (T12/T13, D-78) that is FOUR sentences and not five: the phrase read has not landed or failed; a group is unwritten (named by title); there is no clean Summary to accept, named per missing group with the wire's own gap token (`:cleanSummaryGateReason`, and the state it checks is the server's — `:cleanSummaryExists` reads `reviewed.state`); a live Writer is rewriting a group, so the text on screen is about to move. The two that left the list — an open blocking-cost finding on a group, and an open task in the queue — left because the ACCEPT voids and closes them in its own commit (L-36's "a pending decision, not a blocked Story"), and they are counted into the confirm line instead (`:summaryAcceptLeftovers` → `:summaryAcceptStatement`); the old task sentence's "on this Summary or anywhere in the Story" went with them, because the read's task rows carry neither a phrase nor a group id and supported no scope in either direction. It may not show a blocker the lock route ignores, and it may not wave through a Summary the reviewed selection says is not clean — and the rail's ready tick is the same call, not a parallel list (`story-nav.ts:summaryReadyForReview` → `lockCheck`).
- **The drain reads the group pipeline, and both gates read ONE predicate.** `loop/status.ts:phase1Unmet` has two branches and never runs both: a grouped book answers from `phrase/exclusion.ts:groupPipelineOwed` — the same gates `work.ts:phase1GroupCandidates` builds candidates from — and a `'sampling'` book answers from the sampling families (§16.7). The grouped sentences, in §6.4's order, are exactly: `"…group-pipeline run(s) still in flight (R5, §6.4 steps 3-6)"` (live runs matched by `isGroupPipelineRun`), `"…group(s) still owe the Overview a draft or a fix pass (R2, §6.4 step 3)"`, `"…Critic pass(es) still owed on the installed group Overview (R3, R5, §6.4 step 4)"`, and `"…deep-review pass(es) still owed for the installed group Overview (§6.4 step 6, R5)"`. The seat keys the probe counts are the GENERATOR's keys (`exclusion.ts:groupReviewSeatKey` / `groupCheckSeatKey`), so the queue and the probe cannot spell one seat two ways. **The scoring pass (D-65) joins the in-flight family and no owed family**: `wi_summary_score_` is in `GROUP_PIPELINE_RUN_PREFIXES`, so a live scoring run holds the drain like any other open thread and the line names it — `"…group-pipeline run(s) still in flight (1 scoring the installed Overview) (R5, §6.4 steps 3-6)"` — while `groupPipelineOwed` has NO scoring field, so an unclaimed pass, a refused pass and a book with zero score rows all still read `drained`. A run's existence may hold the drain; a NUMBER never does. Pinned by `loop/section-scores.test.ts` and stage-by-stage by `loop/phase1-groups-drain.test.ts`. Pinned stage-by-stage by `loop/phase1-groups-drain.test.ts`.
- **Sign-off is held by the same read, and the gate got TIGHTER, never looser.** `status.ts:decideStory` withholds the `pending / phase_signoff` OFFER while the CURRENT vector's structured seats or deep passes are owed (`groupOwedForSignoff.structuredReviews + deepReviews > 0`). The legacy clause it replaces — "deep passes owed on the SELECTED Summary revision" — answers zero unconditionally on a group book, because a group book has no selected Summary revision; leaving it meant the sweep offered the lock over an Overview nobody had finished reading (L-29 signed a verdict that was not complete). Because `groupPipelineOwed` counts the seats of the CURRENT vector first and only then the deep passes, **an accepted install re-arms a whole reading cycle**: `summary_critics` `review_groups` passes, then that many `check_unit` passes, before the Story may read `drained` or be offered a sign-off. That is the honest reading of "the crew still has work": the text moved, so the reading is owed again.
- **The engine skips the sampling machinery** for a grouped book: no selection (`engine.ts:1606`), no wave re-arm (`1994`), no plateau audit (`2073`), and the planner's mode switch never reaches the sampler (`work.ts:1261-1262`). The legacy plateau STOP still gates a legacy book, minus the Author escalation and the wave bump R5 removed.
- **Deleting a Story deletes its Phrase rows** (the deviation this section recorded on 2026-11-24 is closed): `engine.ts:2673-2675` purges `phrases`, `phrase_groups` and `group_revisions` in the same `delete_story` commit as the Story row, after the live runs are killed with `'delete'` and settled, with no lock exemption (P1: the whole book goes). Phrase findings, tasks and group-review verdicts ride the pre-existing `findings`/`tasks`/`verdicts` deletes. The measurement that found it survives as provenance — `Temp/create-storyline-orchestrator/T4/phrase-delete-probe.ts`, run through `yarn vite-node`, reported `phrases=1 phrase_groups=5` before the fix. Pinned by `api/story-delete.test.ts`.
- **Scrap, the Seed edit and the backtrack are group-aware now** (D-63, §17.12):
  - `loop/invalidation.ts:scrapPhasesAfter` deletes the story's `group_revisions` rows whenever the scrapped rank is below `chapters` and the book is group-managed, and REPORTS the number as `counts.groupRevisions` beside `revisions`/`ideas`/`digests`/`verdicts`. The reasons are all three of the joins a stale `latest` row keeps: the write allowance counts "runs after THIS text" from it (`exclusion.ts:writeBaselineOf`), the review seats are keyed to the vector its number hashes into, and R3's closure joins on claims stamped onto exactly these rows.
  - `loop/engine.ts:editSeed` (3111-3135) resets the five `phrase_groups` rows IN PLACE — `content: ''`, `revision: 0`, `updated_by: null`, **same UUIDs** — and the `phrases` row to `content: ''`, `revision: 0`, with the mirror cleared beside them, so the next Writer batch is the five unwritten groups again and `ensurePhraseCatalog` re-pressed is a no-op (R1's addresses outlive the text).
  - `loop/engine.ts:backtrack('summary')` keeps L-31's convention rather than inventing a new one: the reopened phase's OWN artifact survives (group texts and their mirror stay in sync as the base for another round) while the revision HISTORY goes with the scrapped work and the sampling counters restart. "Reset the current text" is the Seed edit's call; "keep the text, drop the history" is the backtrack's — the same split the legacy prose flow already made, now applied to the group rows.
  - **A GROUP LOCK IS CLEARED BY THE SCRAP THAT DELETES THE ROWS IT NAMES (D-69).** The same branch (394-413) writes `locked: false` and three `null`s — `phrase/locks.ts:unlockPhraseGroup`'s exact shape, under a CAS on the group row — onto every LOCKED group of a book whose `group_revisions` rows it just deleted, and touches no other row (an unlocked row leaves the four keys OUT, which is the shape the projection and the rail read as open). `content` and `revision` are untouched, so the text still survives the backtrack, and the findings the lock voided stay `discarded`. A lock is a fact about a REVISION row: leaving one on a group with no `latest` row meant the reviewed Overview could never compose (`reviewed.ts:cleanSelectionOf` resolves a locked group's entry through that row) while `revisions.ts:installGroupRevisions` refused every batch naming the group — WHOLE, so all five sections were stranded until an Author unlocked a lock they never set. A scrap that deletes no revision backing — a backtrack to `chapters` — clears nothing.
  Pinned by `loop/group-seed-reset.test.ts` (next batch = the five unwritten groups; the catalog re-press is a no-op; a sibling Story's catalog is untouched; a locked section keeps its words and loses the dead flag, then installs, stamps and composes again; and `scrapPhasesAfter(…, 'chapters')` leaves every lock standing).
- **The drain's honest limit, restated.** It never advances while the group pipeline owes — live runs, owed drafts, unreported seats, unrun deep passes — and the two things it does NOT claim are the honest edges: a group whose write allowance is spent is PARKED and not owed (a provider that cannot answer the schema must not make the Story undrainable), and the unmet list names groups and seats, never a per-criterion verdict, which stays the Goal panel's job.

### 17.9 Routes (the Author's grouped verbs)

| verb | meaning | refusals |
| -- | -- | -- |
| `GET /stories/:id/phrases` | one pure read for the whole grouped surface (§4.19's `PhraseView`) | a legacy book answers `mode: 'sampling'` with empty groups — a truth about the book, not an error |
| `POST /stories/:id/phrases/import` | the Author's explicit, **idempotent** move of a legacy Summary onto groups: the five prose fields become first revisions (`run_ref: null`) and `summary_mode` becomes `'groups'` | nothing implicit: no read, no sweep and no migration ever sets the flag |
| `POST /stories/:id/phrases/groups/:groupId/revise` `{content, note?}` | the Author rewriting ONE group — an install, exactly like a Writer's (R2) | `400` blank content · `404` a `groupId` the book does not have (the id is the `phrase_groups.id` UUID, not the catalog key) · `409` on a `'sampling'` book, naming `/phrases/import` · `409` while a live Writer pass holds the group, naming the holding run ids (R4/F8) · `409` while the group is **locked** — the install refuses the whole batch before its first write, so a hand edit cannot overrule a lock either (§17.18) |
| `POST /stories/:id/phrases/reviewed/choose` `{note?}` | **D-68**: the Author takes the Reviewed Summary — five installs, the passed-over texts `discarded`, the choice recorded on the Phrase row, one commit (§17.17). It does NOT lock the phase and claims to resolve nothing | `404` a Story the book does not have · `409` on a `'sampling'` book, naming `/phrases/import` · `409` while no complete clean selection exists, naming each group and whether it is `unwritten`, has `no_clean_revision`, or is held by an unresolved issue (D-67) · `409` while a live Writer holds ANY group, naming the holding run ids (R4: the choice installs five texts at once) |
| `POST /stories/:id/phrases/groups/:groupId/lock` | **R-lock (§17.18)**: the Author declares THIS group's current text finished — four fields on the group row, the claims whose whole scope just locked moved to `discarded`, and NOTHING else: no revision, no `content`, no Overview recomposition, no mirror write. Answers with the lock report (`locked_revision`, `locked_by`, `locked_at`, `voided_findings`) plus the fresh `view` | `404` a `groupId` the book does not have · `409` on a `'sampling'` book, naming `/phrases/import` · `409` while a live Writer holds the group, naming the holding run ids (R4 — a lock fixes a revision, and freezing text a pass is about to replace fixes words nobody read) · `409 group_unwritten` (a group at revision `0` has no revision to fix, and locking it would freeze an Overview with a hole) · `409 already_locked` (a second lock is a refusal, not a re-dating of a decision the book already carries) |
| `POST /stories/:id/phrases/groups/:groupId/unlock` | **R-lock (§17.18)**: the Author's alone, whoever set the flag. The flag comes off, the three metadata fields go to `null`, and the group is offered to the generator again on the next sweep. It resurrects NOTHING: `discarded` is terminal (§4.10), so a defect the crew still believes in is filed again by the next Critic pass against the now-writable text | `404` a `groupId` the book does not have · `409` on a `'sampling'` book, naming `/phrases/import` · `409` while a live Writer holds the group, naming the holding run ids · `409 not_locked` (there is nothing to lift, and the sentence says so rather than answering an idempotent no-op) — and there is no editor actor here at all: `editor_unlock` is the domain's refusal of a crew unlock, which this surface can never ask for |
| `POST /stories/:id/lock` | **D-78 (2026-11-26) — the phase sign-off, and on phase ① the ACCEPT of the final Summary:** the flags, the tray deleted (D-36), the Summary phrase's open findings voided to `discarded` and the queue's `open`/`claimed` tasks closed as `wontfix` in the SAME commit with the counts recorded and shown (L-25b), and — on the summary leg — a SECOND commit at this route that starts Phrase ② from `api/projections.ts:compiledSummaryOf` with `source: compiled` (§6.3a). The answer is the Story row plus the additive `lock_receipt` (`phase`, `deleted_ideas`, `next_phase`; `voided_findings`, `wontfixed_tasks`, `phrase_started`, `phrase_start_reason` on the summary leg only) | the two guards it has always had and only those: `404` a Story the book does not have · `409` while the phase's Ideas tray holds an undecided row (L-7a) · `409` while the phase's Goal does not pass, naming the failing criteria (U-16, G-2). **An open finding and an open task refuse nothing** — they are what the act stops, not what it waits for (L-36) — and a Phrase ② start that cannot happen (already started · nothing compiled to copy · `plotlines_mode` not `'groups'` · Phrase ② not in this build's catalog) is a `200` with the reason in `phrase_start_reason`, NEVER a `409` over a lock that committed (§6.3a) |
| `POST /stories/:id/summary/revise` | **refused on a grouped book** | `409`: there is no whole-Summary round to ask for; the verb that exists is the per-group revise (R5) |
| `POST /stories/:id/revisions/:revisionId/select` | **refused for the Summary unit on a grouped book** | `409`: nothing competes, so there is nothing to select (R5) |
| `POST /stories/:id/revisions/merge` | **refused for the Summary unit on a grouped book** | `409`: the text IS the group vector (R5) |

Every refusal names the verb to use instead, because "try again" with no direction is indistinguishable from a hang.
