The OpenAPI contract is `src/storyline-server.swagger.yaml` (`/v1/storyline/stories/{id}/phrases`, `/phrases/import`, `/phrases/groups/{groupId}/revise`, `/phrases/groups/{groupId}/lock`, `/phrases/groups/{groupId}/unlock`, `/phrases/reviewed/choose`, and `/lock` — whose `200` is the Story row plus the additive `PhaseLockReceipt`, §6.3a) — and `api/contract.test.ts` fails when the mounted route set and the documented operation set differ by one entry, which is what makes that file a check rather than a habit.

**The crew's tools are closed on the same book, and each refusal points at grouped work (`tools/`, 2026-11-24 hardening).**

| tool | on a group book | the `fix` it hands back |
| -- | -- | -- |
| `editSummary` (`tools/writer.ts`) | `ToolRefusal('R6')` — the five prose fields are the install's MIRROR, and a hand write would put the Overview out of step with the groups it composes. The check sits after the lock/phase guard and BEFORE argument parsing: the refusal is about the BOOK, so it never depends on the args being valid. | "run the grouped `draft_groups` job, or call the per-group revise route — the mirror follows the installed group texts" |
| `selectRevision` on the Summary unit (`tools/editor.ts`) | `ToolRefusal('R5')` — each group installs its own revision and the install recomposes the Overview, so a hand-picked draft would install text the group rows do not hold | "raiseFinding with `group_refs` naming every group the defect crosses — the grouped `draft_groups` job installs the fix" |
| `raiseFinding` on a grouped Summary (`tools/reader.ts`) | `ToolRefusal('R3')` for missing or unknown `group_refs`; the unknown-id `fix` branches by book, because only a legacy Story may omit the list (§17.7) | "call `raiseFinding` again with `group_refs` naming every group the defect crosses" |
| `raiseFinding` naming a LOCKED group (`tools/reader.ts`) | **R-lock (§17.18), two answers and one rule.** Every id named is locked → `ToolRefusal('R1')`: the claim has no text left to rewrite, and a row nobody may act on would hold the round open over prose the Author signed off. Some ids locked, some open → the LOCKED ids come OFF the stored scope and the claim survives for the groups that can still move — the same subtraction the domain already applies (`findings.ts:findingActionableScope`), so this is the reporter's own scope stated in the form the planner acts on, not a rewrite of the claim. An UNSCOPED claim is untouched: its scope is a read of the rows, and narrowing it here would invent an attribution the crew never made | wholly locked and the book has open groups: "call `raiseFinding` again with `group_refs` naming the groups the defect crosses that are NOT locked, or ask the Author to unlock the group first" · wholly locked and every group stands: "file nothing against the Summary — every group of this Overview is locked; only the Author lifts a lock" |
| `requestMerge` on the Summary in phase ① (`tools/editor.ts`) | still `D-23` on BOTH books — phase ① never merges — but the MESSAGE already branched by pipeline and now the STRUCTURED `fix` branches with it. Telling a grouped book "then `selectRevision`" sent it straight at the workflow the redesign removed. | grouped: "raiseFinding with `group_refs` naming every group the defect crosses — the grouped `draft_groups` job installs the fix" · legacy: "`listRevisions <unit>`, then `selectRevision`" |

Chapter and plotline selection, `selectRevision` on any other unit, and every tool on a `'sampling'` book keep the semantics they always had: the refusal test is `isGroupManagedStory`, never "is this phase ①". The copy itself is pinned by `tools/group-mode-writes.test.ts`, which pins both the grouped refusal data and the legacy book's unchanged advice — because a `fix` string that points at a retired tool is a defect the Author sees as a hang.

### 17.10 Prompts (R7, and what is already fixed)

The compiled prompts stay byte-stable per state and carry the prompt hash onto the revision row (D-44/D-45's cache rule is unchanged). A group draft prompt carries the Seed Brief (or the Seed, P5), the `critical`/`high` Knowledge triage, the group definitions quoted from the catalog, the CURRENT text of the groups in the batch (a revision is a revision OF a text — a Writer handed only tasks would rewrite a group it cannot see), and the FIX TASKS block **only when the batch carries any**: D-34's approved findings reach a batch as the tasks they became, and a first draft is handed none, because an empty heading reads as an unanswered demand. A reading seat's prompt carries the composed Overview — all five groups with their revision numbers, the combination under review, which is the key the job was claimed under — and the still-open fix tasks to grade. The binding rewrite/rate sentences live in the compiler (`GROUP_DRAFT_INSTRUCTION` / `GROUP_REVIEW_INSTRUCTION`), so the brief and the cache key come from one place; **the final role-level instruction wording is R7's later task** and is deliberately not claimed here. The draft instruction also carries D-66's promise to the model — "every group a fix names is already in THIS batch: answer the whole defect in every group it names, or claim nothing" — because a Writer that halves its own answer would hit the same all-or-nothing refusal as a halved dispatch, and the sentence is engine-owned for the same reason the exact-set rule is: an Author rewriting the sampler's role prompt must not be able to lose it. The scoring pass follows the same rule (D-65): `compileSectionScorePrompt` prints the composed Overview — every group with its revision number and current text, the set under judgement — and the binding contract is engine-owned text in the compiler (`SECTION_SCORE_INSTRUCTION`), not the template's, so an Author rewriting the `reader-consistency` role prompt cannot lose it. The lock seat follows it too (R-lock, §17.18): `compileGroupLockPrompt` prints the ONE candidate section at the clean revision the seat was armed for, the Reader's number with its rationale and quoted line, the open claims and Proposals that name it (capped, in id order, so one state compiles one byte sequence), the claims about it that already closed, and the locked peers; the two quotable halves of `lock: true`, the "the number is not the decision" sentence in both directions, and "`false` is normal" are engine-owned (`GROUP_LOCK_INSTRUCTION`). The template's copy supplies the VOICE, and the catalogue now carries it: the seat's system turn resolves from the slug `GROUP_LOCK_TEMPLATE_SLUG` (`editor-group-lock`), so until §17.18 added the built-in row it fell back to `fallbackRolePrompt`'s stated absence (§8) — a seat that ran on a placeholder rather than on a crew the Author could read and duplicate (D-17). The job's `groupRefs` are the authority on what was asked: the mock answers, the validator and the rail all read the WORK ITEM rather than parsing the prompt.

### 17.11 Superseded, retained, and untouched

| superseded **for a grouped Summary** | retained, and why |
| -- | -- |
| `sample_unit` waves and N competing Summary drafts; `screen_samples` screening of candidates; `loop/sample-select.ts`'s eligibility, origin-awareness, minimality and ranking; the `choose_sample` commit and the deletion of the losers (D-22 for the Summary); `sampling_round`/`sampling_wave`/`sampling_history`; the plateau's Author escalation and wave bump (`openRevisionPlateauTask`, `SAMPLING_PLATEAU_PREFIX` — gone for every book, R5); `POST summary/revise`, Summary `revisions/:id/select`, Summary `revisions/merge` | the Seed Brief and the research gates — `phase1GroupCandidates` reads the SAME `researchGate` predicate (§16.1, D-40/D-51), so the redesign changed what a Writer is asked to write, not what it must answer first; citation and evidence rules, Knowledge triage, T-10, D-16 dedupe; the deep review's four Critic angles; `verify_findings`, D-33/D-34, the dial (D-35/D-35b), the Ideas tray and the lock gates; `stories.summary_mode` as the Author's switch, absent = `'sampling'`; `GET /stories/:id`'s `sampling` projection; §4.2 as a read surface; `revisions`/`sample_reviews` rows of legacy books; the `writer-summary-sampler` template id, reused by `draft_groups` at `attempts: 1` |
| **the whole-Summary "installs nothing, buys another attempt" hill-climb** — a grouped install never rejects a text wholesale; the batch is refused only when the answer is not schema-valid, and then the WHOLE batch is refused | **the later-phase backend, untouched**: phases ②/③ still use `write_unit`, divergence, an Editor's `selection_rules`, D-22's deletion and D-23/D-24's gates. Only the Summary unit's phase-① machinery moved. |
| **Hardening addendum (2026-11-24) — three more things a grouped Summary no longer gets.** (1) A direct write to the mirror: `editSummary` answers `R6` (§17.9's table), so the five prose fields have exactly one writer — the install. (2) A Summary `selectRevision` from a run: the Editor's tool answers `R5` alongside the route's 409, so the retired selection is closed to the crew as well as to the Author. (3) A drain or sign-off that reads the sampling rows: `phase1Unmet` and `decideStory` answer from `groupPipelineOwed` (§17.8), so a grouped book's stalled state names groups and seats instead of a round that never ran. | **What the hardening deliberately did NOT touch.** The legacy `'sampling'` tools keep their semantics whole: `editSummary` writes, Summary `selectRevision` selects, an unscoped Summary finding still means the whole Overview, and `GET /stories/:id`'s `sampling` projection remains that book's audit. The lock route's gate (tray, then Goal), the dial, D-33/D-34, and phases ②/③ are unchanged. Every new refusal keys on `isGroupManagedStory` — never on the phase — so a grouped Story's chapter and plotline work sees none of it. |
| **D-69 addendum (2026-11-25) — a LOCKED group leaves the loop's reach, and one optional seat may put it there.** (1) `phrase/revisions.ts:installGroupRevisions` refuses a batch that names a locked group, WHOLE and before the first write, so "one group frozen, its siblings rewritten round it" is a state the store can reach and "a locked group rewritten" is not. (2) `phrase/exclusion.ts:groupWorkTargets` never offers a locked group, and a finding's ACTABLE scope is `findings.ts:findingActionableScope` — R3's attribution minus the locked groups — so a defect crossing one locked and two open groups routes to the two, and a defect whose every group stands is discarded rather than left to park. (3) A claim whose every named group just became locked is moved to `discarded` at lock time; an unlock never resurrects it (§4.10's terminal state). (4) The Author's lock and unlock are two routes; the Editor's is ONE optional seat, keyed `wi_group_lock_<cleanVectorHash>_<groupId>`, that `groupPipelineOwed` and `GROUP_PIPELINE_RUN_PREFIXES` deliberately do not know, so no drain sentence, sign-off gate, hill or goal waits on it. | **What D-69 did NOT touch.** §6.3a's phase locks and the sign-off gates (`summary_locked` / `plotlines_locked` are different records with a different meaning, and an install still WRITES through `summaries.locked` while it refuses a locked GROUP — the two locks point in opposite directions on purpose). R2's per-group revision lines, R3's closure join (the ATTRIBUTION is never rewritten — only what is actable is subtracted), R4's exclusion, the two-attempt allowance and D-66's whole-set rule, D-65's display-only scoring (its §17.14 sentence is amended to record the one rationale READ, not to move the boundary), D-67's Clean stamp and D-68's choose, and every `'sampling'` book: a legacy Story has no group rows to lock, so the flag, the seat and the four refusals never appear there. |
| **D-66 addendum (2026-11-25) — the batch dial no longer cuts a defect.** (1) `slice(0, max(1, summary_sampling))` over a FLAT owed-group list: a finding's set is now indivisible, and the dial caps only the sets after the first (§17.6, §17.15). (2) §17.6's **shrink**: `{premise, tone}` with `premise` live no longer offers `{tone}` — the set waits whole, because the shrunk batch installed half an answer. (3) A claim stamped once per GROUP: `finding_refs` is deduped per finding per revision. | **What D-66 did NOT touch.** R3's all-or-nothing closure and its `approved` state gate (§17.7), the claim-family and attempt-number rules (L-3), R2's per-group independence, R4's exclusion and write-attempt cap with its two exclusions, the drain's four sentences and the sign-off gate, D-64's reconcile, D-65's scoring seat, and every `'sampling'` book — `planGroupDraftBatch` is only ever called on a group-managed Story, and no legacy row changes meaning. |

### 17.12 New requirement ids introduced by this section

**D-54** (R1) the catalog is data, five groups, Summary-only, `world_rules` is not a group — "Summary-only" for two days: §18's D-71 adds the second ENABLED kind on the same data, which is what this rule was a promise about. **D-55** (R2) per-group revision lines, one `latest`, archived history, the batch as the unit of the install. **D-56** (R3) `group_refs` on findings and runs, all-or-nothing closure on `approved` findings, and the Reader's refusals. **D-57** (R4) the row-derived group hold: fail-closed, shrink-not-cancel, disjoint-concurrent, `GROUP_WRITE_ATTEMPT_CAP` and its two exclusions. **D-58** (R5) install-on-acceptance, the sampling skips, and the review keyed by the group vector. **D-59** (R6) the mirror in the install commit, the lock exception, the ABSENT-means-sampling rule, and the explicit idempotent import. **D-60** (S-8/S-9) the two no-tool job kinds and their answer contracts. Plus the §10.2 additions **U-18** (the group workspace) and **U-19** (the honest sign-off surface), and the vocabulary rule **V-3**.

Added by the same-day hardening, so the code and the spec cite the same thing:

- **D-61 — the grouped write path is closed at the TOOL layer, and every refusal carries a grouped `fix`.** `editSummary` → `R6`, Summary-unit `selectRevision` → `R5`, grouped Summary `raiseFinding` → `R3`, all keyed on `isGroupManagedStory`; the hint names the `draft_groups` job, the per-group revise route, or `raiseFinding` with the group ids, and never the retired selection (§17.9, §17.11).
- **D-62 — drain, sign-off and queue are ONE read.** `phrase/exclusion.ts:groupPipelineOwed` is the single source for live group runs, owed drafts/fix passes, the CURRENT vector's Critic seats and its deep passes; `phase1Unmet`, `decideStory` and the work generator all read it, the seat keys are the generator's keys, a parked group is not owed, and an install re-arms a full reading cycle (§17.8).
- **D-63 — the group book is purged and reset like every other artifact, and its identity survives the reset.** `deleteStory` purges the three Phrase collections in its one commit; `editSeed` resets the five rows in place with the same UUIDs (`''`/`0`/null) and clears the Overview and mirror; `scrapPhasesAfter` clears the revision HISTORY and reports `counts.groupRevisions`; a backtrack keeps the texts and drops the history, per L-31. The write-allowance baseline is a journal position, which is what makes a reset a reset (§17.6, §17.8).
- **D-64 — finding reconciliation is group-book-only, idempotent, and runs on the sweep.** `loop_reconcile_findings` re-runs `closeFindingAnsweredByInstalls` for `approved` rows and closes the tasks that named the now-`fixed` finding; it is gated on `isGroupManagedStory` because `findingResolution` is vacuously `resolved_all` with no phrase rows (§17.7).
- **D-65 — the Author may see a Reader's score of the installed Overview, and the loop may not act on it.** One tool-less `score_sections` pass per **group vector** (`wi_summary_score_<vectorHash>`, `rank: -1` so it takes the vector's first dispatch slot), offered only once every group has text, answering `SectionScores` with a whole number 0–100 plus a rationale and a quoted line per section. Its accepted answer commits FIVE `summary_scores` rows in one settle commit — nothing else moves — and the rail's `groups[].scores` reads them newest-first. It files no finding, opens no task, submits no verdict, and spends no group's write attempt; **no score value, and no scoring seat in any owed count, enters the criteria engine, drain threshold, hill-climb, sign-off gate or goal math** — the one thing that does count is the run's existence, since an **in-flight** scoring run still counts as in-flight group-pipeline work. That is the single bounded exception to P6 (§2), with §17.14 as its normative text.

Added on 2026-11-25 for R13 (the Reviewed Summary):

- **D-67 (R13) — a cross-group Critic pass leaves a CLEAN verdict on the text it read, and the Reader scores only what the crew stands behind.** A `check_unit` deep pass over the group vector is the one group-model review that FILES findings, so its close is the instant "this Critic read this combination of five texts and found no unresolved defect on this group" becomes a fact the book can show. The verdict is stamped on the revision row that group held in that vector (`group_revisions.clean`, `clean_run_ref`) and is never revoked by a later event; what a later event moves is the SELECTION, which is a read — per group, the NEWEST clean revision whose group carries no unresolved issue right now — so a group whose newest text failed review falls back to its earlier clean one, and the Reader's `score_sections` seat arms on that complete selection rather than on "every group has text" (§17.16 is the normative text; it amends §17.14's precondition).
- **D-68 (R13) — the Author chooses the Reviewed Summary, and the choice is an install, not an edit.** `POST /stories/:id/phrases/reviewed/choose` takes the complete clean selection and makes it the book in ONE commit: five new `latest` rows holding the chosen texts, every revision the choice passed over flipped to the terminal `discarded` state, the chosen vector recorded on the Phrase row (`reviewed_vector`, `reviewed_vector_hash`, `reviewed_at`), and the projection's `reviewed` block reporting the choice beside the score that judged it. Discarded rows PERSIST (R3's closure join has no state filter, so a `fixed` finding stays `fixed`); they are hidden from every current read. The choose does NOT lock the phase and claims to resolve nothing. Snapshot v4 marks the new state, one-directionally (§17.17 is the normative text).

Added on 2026-11-25 for **R-lock** (the group lock):

- **D-69 (R-lock) — a Summary group may be declared FINISHED, and a finished group is the fixed point the rest of the book is revised around.** Four fields on the group row (`locked`, `locked_revision`, `locked_by`, `locked_at`) are the whole mechanism: a lock writes no revision, touches no `content` and recomposes nothing, so the signed-off words are byte-for-byte what they were and the flag names the revision a person committed to. What follows from the flag, in the code and not in an instruction to a model: `installGroupRevisions` refuses any batch that names a locked group (whole batch, before the first write), `groupWorkTargets` never offers one, `findings.ts:findingActionableScope` subtracts the locked part of every claim so a cross-group defect routes to the groups that can still move, and a claim whose EVERY named group is locked becomes `discarded` at lock time — void, not owed, and never resurrected by an unlock. WHO MAY ACT: the Author owns both verbs (`POST …/lock`, `POST …/unlock`), and the unlock is the Author's alone whoever set the flag (`editor_unlock` is the domain's refusal of a crew un-signing its own book). The Editor may LOCK only on a Story that grants `stories.editor_group_locking`, and only through one OPTIONAL judgement seat per section per clean vector (`wi_group_lock_<cleanVectorHash>_<groupId>`), which reads that section's clean text, the Critic's open claims and Proposals, and the Reader's RATIONALE, and answers `GroupLockDecision` = `{group_ref, lock, rationale}`. **The judgement replaced a cutoff, and the cutoff is refused in both directions:** no number appears in the seat's eligibility rules, in its answer schema, or in the copy the catalogue row carries, and the seat's own copy is pinned digit-free for exactly that reason. Turning the Setting off arms nothing and lifts nothing. What it does NOT touch: §6.3a's phase locks, R2's revision lines, R3's attribution and closure join, R4's exclusion and allowance, D-66's whole-set rule, D-65's display-only boundary (amended to record the one rationale read), D-67's stamp, D-68's choose, and every `'sampling'` book. §17.18 is the normative text.

Added on 2026-11-25 for R12:

- **D-66 (R12) — one defect, one pass: the batch dial caps BATCHES, never DEFECTS.** A finding naming several phrase groups is fixed by ONE `draft_groups` pass covering every group it names, and the loop may never schedule, install or report a part of it. Three mechanics, all pinned: (a) **formation** — `phrase/exclusion.ts:findingGroupSets` groups the owed targets into indivisible sets (a union-find over shared finding ids, so two findings that meet on one group become one pass over their UNION) and `planGroupDraftBatch` takes those sets whole: a set touching a live hold waits whole (which REPLACES §17.6's old shrink), a set missing one of a finding's still-owed groups (a parked group) is withheld whole, and `max(1, summary_sampling)` caps only the sets AFTER the first one, which is taken at any size; (b) **install** — `phrase/revisions.ts:parseGroupDraftAnswer` refuses WHOLE a `GroupDraft` that skipped a group the job carried, and `loop/engine.ts` installs only on `ok`, so one group moving without the others is not a state the store can reach; a batch's `findingRefs` are DEDUPED, so a finding is stamped once per revision however many of its groups the pass rewrote, and `work_item_ref` names the whole-set family on every text it wrote; (c) **closure** — the R3 join (§17.7) is untouched, because a refused answer and a parked group still leave a finding open, and D-66 says nothing about closing a row on less than its whole `targeted` set. What it supersedes: the §17.6 shrink, and the three-sweeps-per-defect reading of §17.7's "batch of one" example. §17.15 is the normative text.

### 17.13 How this is verified

```bash
# from the repo root — the grouped suite
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/phrase/catalog.test.ts
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/phrase/revisions.test.ts
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/phrase/exclusion.test.ts   # R4's hold + D-66's cut: the three rules IN ORDER, merged sets, the dial between sets, the catalog re-sort
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/phrase/findings.test.ts
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/loop/phase1-groups.test.ts
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/loop/phase1-groups-restart.test.ts   # D-66: a lapse releases exactly its own group, and the finding's SET waits for the sibling instead of being cut around it
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/loop/phase1-groups-drain.test.ts   # D-62: the four group drain sentences, probe keys == generator keys, sign-off opens only once the seats close
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/loop/group-seed-reset.test.ts      # D-63: Seed edit resets the rows in place; backtrack keeps texts, drops history; catalog re-press is a no-op
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/loop/group-findings-batch-cap.test.ts   # D-64: three groups under a batch of 1, late approval, and the vacuous legacy read that must not close
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/api/story-delete.test.ts           # D-63: the delete commit purges phrases / phrase_groups / group_revisions
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/api/phrase-routes.test.ts
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/integration/summary-groups.test.ts   # the §17.7 examples, end to end
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/tools/reader-group-attribution.test.ts   # R3's refusals
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/tools/group-mode-writes.test.ts   # D-61: the grouped `editSummary`/`selectRevision` refusals AND the exact `fix` copy each returns, with the legacy book's advice pinned beside them
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/phrase/section-scores.test.ts   # D-65: the claim key, every refusal sentence; D-67: the scored-vector read and the attempt cap
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/phrase/reviewed.test.ts   # D-67/D-68: the three-group fallback example, the stamp's two guards, the choose's five installs and its discards, and the `fixed` finding that must survive them
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/loop/section-scores.test.ts   # D-65: one seat per vector, five rows in one commit, an unclaimed seat never holds sign-off; D-67: the clean-selection gate, the refusal re-arm, the cap
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/api/phrase-scores.test.ts   # D-65: newest-first rail order, the id tie-break, the pinned wire shape
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/shared/schemas/section-scores.test.ts   # D-65: the SectionScores contract the provider is held to
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/phrase/locks.test.ts   # R-lock: every refusal code with its sentence, the two writes and their CAS order, the voided-scope rule (an unscoped claim voids only with the LAST group), the unlock that resurrects nothing
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/api/phrase-lock-routes.test.ts   # R-lock: the status codes and the exact 409 sentences, the ten group-row columns plus the four lock keys on a locked row only, the `view` envelope, the unlock's three `null`s
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/loop/editor-lock.test.ts   # R-lock: the five eligibility gates, the seat key and its underscore split, the spent-seat read, the answer validator, the settle guard's sentences
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/loop/phase1-editor-lock.test.ts   # R-lock: five seats per clean vector on the real server, the exact Work Item, the settle that writes ONE flag and no draft, verdict, task or counter
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/tools/reader-group-locks.test.ts   # R-lock: the wholly-locked `raiseFinding` refusal with both `fix` branches, the mixed scope narrowed to the movable groups, the unscoped claim left as reported
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/templates/templates.test.ts   # R-lock: the catalogue row's shape, and the seat's copy pinned DIGIT-FREE — no threshold can hide in prose an Author edits

# the whole package, and one-off probes against the catalog/composition

yarn vite-node Temp/create-storyline-orchestrator/<task>/<probe>.ts   # scratch is in the OUTER repo root's Temp/ — never `node`, never `tsx`
```

### 17.14 D-65 — the Reader's section scores (display-only, the P6 exception)

**The requirement, in the Author's terms.** After the Overview is installed the Author wants one more read of it: *does the Summary hold together — is the internal logic sound, is each section consistent with the others, is any of it implausible for the world the Seed sets up?* — and they want it **as a number out of 100 per section, one Reader run per Summary revision, visible as a history on the rail**. A boolean criterion answers "may this ship?"; it does not answer "how close is this to shipping, and is it moving?", which is the question the desk is for (U-19).

**Why it is allowed at all, given P6.** P6 forbids a score as a *decision input*: a fresh context grading 1–10 is noise, and a loop that steers by a slider has no fixed point. A display score breaks none of that, because the loop never reads it. That is the whole of the exception, and it is enforced structurally rather than by discipline:

| the score may | the score may never |
| -- | -- |
| be read by the Author, on the rail, per section, newest first | enter the criteria engine (§5) or the Goal's levels (C-14/C-15) |
| accumulate one row per judged vector, so a trend is visible | move the drain probe (`status.ts:phase1Unmet`) or the sign-off gate (`decideStory`) — no owed field exists to move |
| carry its own rationale and quoted line (D-15) | feed the hill-climb, the plateau, the dial (D-35/D-35b) or the write allowance (R4) |
| be named in the in-flight drain sentence, because its RUN is an open thread | file a finding, open a task, submit a verdict, or write an artifact (§7: a reader writes nothing) |
| be waited for by a human | be waited for by a gate on the SCORE — an open run is waited for exactly as any other open thread is |

**Stated precisely, because "display-only" is easy to overstate:** **no score value, and no scoring seat in any
owed count, enters the criteria engine, drain threshold, hill-climb, sign-off gate or goal math.** The one
thing that DOES reach a gate is the run's existence, not its number: `wi_summary_score_` sits in
`phrase/exclusion.ts:GROUP_PIPELINE_RUN_PREFIXES`, so an **in-flight** scoring run still counts as in-flight
group-pipeline work — it appears in `status.ts`'s open-thread line (`"…in flight (1 scoring the installed
Overview)…"`) and in the sign-off gate's live-run set. What never happens is a score, or an unclaimed or
refused seat, being counted as owed work: `groupPipelineOwed` has no scoring field and
`isGroupHoldingRun` refuses the kind, so a closed scoring run holds nothing.

The mechanical check that keeps this honest is a grep, not a comment: `grep -rn "summary_scores" src/server/loop` matches exactly one file — `engine.ts:settleJob`, the module that WRITES the rows. The camel-cased accessor `store.summaryScores` appears under `loop/**` in two files and both are known: `engine.ts` writes, and `loop/editor-lock.ts` reads one row's **`rationale`** as evidence for the Editor's lock judgement (R-lock, §17.18 — it amends this sentence's original claim that nothing in the loop reads the collection, and it amends nothing else). What stays true, and is what the sentence was protecting: **no score NUMBER is read anywhere in the loop**, no code compares one to a threshold, and the seat §17.18 describes therefore cannot degrade into the cutoff the Author refused.

**The seat.** `loop/work.ts:phase1GroupCandidates` offers candidate 2b — kind `score_sections`, roleKind `reader`, template `reader-consistency`, `toolMode: 'none'`, response schema `SectionScores` (`shared/schemas/section-scores.ts`), `groupRefs` = ALL FIVE groups. It was originally gated on `phrase/section-scores.ts:isScorableSummary` — every group holding non-blank text — because a section score judges how the sections fit TOGETHER, and a half-written Overview has no such thing to judge. **D-67 replaced that precondition** with the complete CLEAN selection (§17.16): the same "never score half a Summary" intent, plus the half that was missing — the text must be one the crew has tried to break and failed to. A complete clean selection implies five non-blank texts, because `installGroupRevisions` refuses an empty group text outright, so nothing about the old gate's reason is lost. The claim key is `wi_summary_score_<vectorHash>`, i.e. the SAME hash the reading seats use (§17.5) — as of D-67, the hash of the CLEAN selection rather than of the live vector — so:

- an accepted install re-arms the score by itself, with no counter, no engine memory and nothing to reconcile on restart (L-3) — and since §17.16, only an install the clean selection actually FOLLOWS (a newest revision that still carries a defect changes what is current, not what is reviewable);
- one vector gets one pass. **D-67 amended the spending rule**: §17.14 originally spent the seat on any CLOSED run, which let one provider that could not answer `SectionScores` strand a clean vector until a Writer happened to rewrite it. The seat now re-arms while that hash has no score rows, bounded by `phrase/section-scores.ts:SECTION_SCORE_ATTEMPT_CAP` (2) — so an answered vector is never offered again, two refusals park the vector, and the §2 P6 exception's "a refused seat is never owed work" clause is what makes the cap need no escalation;
- the pass is offered at `priority: 2` with **`rank: -1`**, which puts it ahead of the Critic seats inside the same priority class. The rank is load-bearing, not cosmetic: under the rigs' ceiling of 1 dispatch per sweep, a seat ranked LAST in the class would only ever score the FINAL revision of a book, so "one run per Summary revision" would silently degrade to "one run per book". Its cost is one extra dispatch slot and one extra Run row per vector, which the grouped integration ledger pins.

**The answer and the settle.** `phrase/section-scores.ts:parseSectionScoreAnswer` validates before the commit — the same shape as the two group passes, for the same reason: a half answer must write NOTHING rather than three rows of five. On an `ok` answer `engine.ts:settleJob` commits five `summary_scores` rows in ONE commit (`phrase_id`, `vector_hash`, `group_id`, `group_key`, `score`, `rationale`, `evidence`, `run_ref`, `work_item_ref`), and writes nothing else. `vector_hash` is read back off the claim key, never re-derived from the rows that exist when the run lands: the score describes the text the Reader was SHOWN. Since §17.16 the same read is what the PROMPT is compiled from — `run/executor.ts` passes `sectionScoreVectorHashOf(item.id)` into `run/phase1-prompt.ts:compileSectionScorePrompt`, which refuses (throws, so the run fails and the seat re-arms) unless the clean selection still matches that hash — so **shown text == scored text == recorded `vector_hash`** is a compile-time property, not a hope. The rows accumulate, so `scores` is a history and `scores[0]` is the current number.

**The read.** `api/projections.ts:phraseView` transposes the rows onto their groups once per view and exposes, per group row, `scores: { value, vector_hash, at, run_ref, rationale? }[]` sorted `created_at` DESC with the row id as the tie-break — the tie-break is required because one commit stamps all five rows with the SAME instant, and without it the rail's "current score" would be whatever order the store happened to iterate (L-3). `evidence` stays server-side; it is reachable from the row's `run_ref` thread. `scores: []` is absence, not zero, and `rationale` is OMITTED when empty rather than shipped as `""`. Since §17.16/§17.17 the same rows are ALSO reachable per reviewed section (`reviewed.groups[].scores` and `reviewed.chosen.scores`, filtered to the hash that block reports) — one join, two views, so the number beside a reviewed text provably judged that text and not the newest one. Wire shape pinned by `api/phrase-scores.test.ts` and `api/phrase-routes.test.ts`; the rail that draws it is U-18's workspace.

**What it does NOT change.** No new route, no new tool, no new collection permission (the engine writes the rows, as it writes `sample_reviews` from a screening answer), no new counter on `stories`, no new phase, no new criterion, and no new template — the Reader's existing `reader-consistency` angle is the seat's system prompt because it is the built-in whose role is about the contract chain holding across artifacts. The legacy `'sampling'` model gains nothing: §16 has no group vector to key a score on, so the pass simply does not exist there.

### 17.15 D-66 — one defect, one pass (R12: the dial caps batches, not defects)

**The requirement, in the Author's terms.** A finding that names the Premise and the Ending is ONE complaint: "the ending pays off a confrontation the premise never set up". The Author who filed it does not want a book in which the ending has been rewritten and the premise still says what they objected to, and §4.10 has no state for that book — there is no `partly fixed` in the finding ladder. **D-66: a finding naming several phrase groups is fixed by ONE `draft_groups` pass covering every group it names, and the loop may never schedule, install, or report a part of it.** The batch dial does not get an exception to that: `summary_sampling` is the Author's knob for HOW MANY PASSes the queue may buy in a sweep, and it was never a knob for how much of one defect a pass may answer.

**What the old rule did, and why it was wrong rather than merely untidy.** `planGroupDraftBatch`'s predecessor cut the owed group list and took `slice(0, max(1, summary_sampling))` groups. On the default dial of 1, a two-group finding became two sweeps, and each sweep committed a real install: the first settle wrote `premise` number 2, stamped it `finding_refs: [that finding]`, and the rail answered `resolved: [premise], missing: [ending]`. Three things went wrong at once. The Author's read showed a defect with evidence against it while a group still said the objected-to thing; the second pass spent the group's SECOND write attempt on a defect whose first half nobody had read yet (R4's cap counts closed passes against the current text, so `{premise}` then `{ending}` is a pair of attempts spent before the pair of texts could be judged together); and the composition was a real Overview of a half-answered book (`phrases.content` recomposes per install), so anything downstream that reads the Overview — a scoring seat, a review seat, the mirror — read a Summary in which one half of a named contradiction was already patched. The old §17.6 shrink made it worse: `{premise, tone}` with a live writer on `premise` offered `{tone}` rather than waiting, which is the same partial install arriving from the scheduling side.

**The rule, as the code states it.** The cut lives in `phrase/exclusion.ts:planGroupDraftBatch`, beside the R4 exclusion and `groupPipelineOwed`, because the queue and the drain probe must answer the owed question with one set of rules. It reads the owed targets as `groupWorkTargets` produces them and applies three rules in a STATED ORDER — the order is the rule, and each rule answers a different question:

1. **R4's exclusion defers a set WHOLE.** A set with any group held by a live `draft_groups` run waits this sweep. This replaces the shrink. The cost is one sweep — a closed run or a lapsed lease releases the hold with no bookkeeping of ours — and a set whose groups are all free is untouched, so disjoint work still runs concurrently (§17.6).
2. **D-66's completeness check.** A set must be the WHOLE still-owed group set of every finding it answers, measured against `findingGroupsStillOwed` on the FINDING rather than against the trimmed candidate list. A group parked at `GROUP_WRITE_ATTEMPT_CAP` was already dropped upstream, so a set missing one is a defect the loop can no longer answer whole; installing the rest would bank a partial fix under a `finding_refs` claim. The set is withheld, the finding stays open with its attempts visible, and that is the allowance's designed escalation (§17.6's "the escalation is the finding") rather than a new one.
3. **The dial caps BETWEEN sets.** Sets are taken whole, in order, while they fit under `max(1, summary_sampling)`; the FIRST set of the sweep is taken at any size, because a dial that could split a finding would rank a knob above the rule the knob exists to serve. Independent sets are rationed exactly as before, so a five-group first draft under a dial of 2 remains three passes, and a book with five independent single-group defects under a dial of 1 still buys five passes.

The sets themselves come from `findingGroupSets`: a union-find over the shared FINDING ids of the owed targets, so two findings that meet on one group are ONE set over the union of their groups — one pass, because two passes over that group would spend its second write attempt before its first new text had been read. The batch is returned in CATALOG order, because the group-set hash, the prompt's GROUPS block, and the install's per-group numbering all key off that order.

**The install edge, which already refused.** D-66's other half was in place before it: `phrase/revisions.ts:parseGroupDraftAnswer` validates the `GroupDraft` against the JOB, and an answer that skipped a group the Work Item carried is refused WHOLE — `loop/engine.ts:settleJob` installs only when the parse says `ok`, so "one group moved, the others did not" is not a state the store can reach even with a crew that answered lazily. The refusal holds the fix task open with the refusal sentence in its history (`its Writer answer was refused whole (…)`) rather than closing the row because a run finished. What D-66 added at this edge is the DEDUPE: a batch lists a finding once however many of its groups it rewrote, so each installed revision carries `finding_refs: [finding_0]` — one claim per finding per revision, not one per group — and `work_item_ref` names the whole-set family on every text the pass wrote, which is what lets a reader tell one commit from three.

**What D-66 does NOT touch.** The closure join (R3, §17.7): `findingResolution`'s `{targeted, resolved, missing, resolved_all}` and the `approved` state gate still decide when a row flips to `fixed`, because a refused answer, a parked group, and a hand edit can still leave a finding half-resolved and must not close it. The claim FAMILY rule (one family per group set, the attempt number as the retry counter, L-3's restart replay). R2's independence — the groups a finding never named keep their text, their number and their allowance. R4's exclusion, the write-attempt cap and its two exclusions, the drain's four sentences, the sign-off gate, and the scoring seat. And nothing about a legacy `'sampling'` book, which has no groups to batch: `planGroupDraftBatch` is only ever called on a group-managed Story.

**Pinned by.** `loop/group-findings-batch-cap.test.ts` (a three-group set under a dial of 1 dispatches as ONE seat, three revisions land on one settle with one claim each, an answer covering one group of a two-group batch is refused with nothing installed and nothing archived, two independent single-group findings under a dial of 1 remain two passes, and the late-approval reconcile still closes what the install owed); `loop/phase1-groups-restart.test.ts` (a lapsed lease releases EXACTLY its own group, and the finding's set waits for the reporting sibling rather than being cut around it — one lapse, one replacement pass over both groups); `integration/summary-groups.test.ts` (the same pair on the real server: one Run row over both groups under the set's claim key, two number-2 Group Revisions naming that run, and no `_2` attempt anywhere in the draft families); `phrase/exclusion.test.ts` (the three rules in order, the merge, the re-sort).

### 17.16 D-67 — the Clean stamp, the reviewed Overview, and the gate on the score (R13)

**The requirement, in the Author's terms.** §17.14 put a Reader's number on the rail the turn the five groups had text. The Author who asked for it meant *"how good is my Summary"*, and a number against text nobody has tried to break answers a different question: the phase's whole method is Writer → Critic → fix, so the first drafts are the ones most likely to be wrong, and certifying them before the first rejection is the same mistake as the selection gate the redesign removed. **D-67: a group's revision is Clean when a deep cross-group review of the combination it is part of closes with no unresolved defect on that group; the Reviewed Summary is the NEWEST Clean revision of every group; and the Reader scores that, not the newest text.** The user's own example is the rule, not a special case: Ending r1 has an issue, r2 is clean, r3 has an issue, r4 is clean, r5 has an issue → the reviewed Ending is **r4, "because Revision 5 has issue"**, and a Summary whose Premise has no clean revision yet has no reviewed Overview at all (so it is not scored, whatever its groups' text).

**The stamping hook, and why that seat.** The verdict is written by `loop/engine.ts:settleJob`, inside the commit that closes a `check_unit` run whose claim key is the group deep-review family (`wi_group_check_<vectorHash>_<pass>`, `phrase/exclusion.ts:groupCheckSeatKey`). Three reasons, and the seat has to be the one that carries all three:

| the seat must | why this one qualifies |
| -- | -- |
| have READ the combination, not one text | it is keyed by the group vector (§17.5), so the claim key names the five texts it was shown |
| be capable of FILING a defect | it is the group model's only tool-carrying review — `tools/reader.ts:raiseFinding` with `group_refs` (§17.7, D-61); the tool-less `review_groups` seat answers a `GroupReview` of notes |
| have its findings already committed when the verdict is written | a tool writes in its own commit, before the settle, so the open-issue read at settle sees everything the pass filed |

Treating the *absence of a note* in a structured review as a clean verdict would certify text nothing tried to break, which is what D-33's ladder exists to prevent (a review's complaint becomes a FINDING before the loop acts on it — and a review that files nothing is one of the two legal endings of the review's job, so it can also be its laziness). The stamp is written in the SAME commit that closes the run, so a crash between the two cannot leave a closed review that reported nothing and a claim key already spent (§17.5's re-arm argument, applied to the verdict).

**The two conditions, and why only one is checked.** (a) the pass filed no `kind: 'issue'` finding scoping to group G, and (b) no unresolved issue scopes to G when the pass closes. At that instant **(b) implies (a)** — a row `raiseFinding` writes starts `unconfirmed` (D-33), so anything the pass filed is still unresolved while its own settle runs — and (b) additionally covers the older defect (a) would miss. So one read decides it: `phrase/reviewed.ts:openIssueGroupIds`, over D-33c's `loop/records.ts:isOpenIssueFinding` (the holding set `loop/types.ts:ISSUE_HOLDING_STATES` — `unconfirmed`, `confirmed` and `approved` hold a group open, and **`disputed` does not**, 2026-11-25: an Editor's "not a defect" cannot veto a Clean verdict over text the pipeline will never rewrite for it) and R3's `findingGroupScope` (a finding with no `group_refs` scopes to all five groups, and therefore blocks all five — the legacy whole-Overview reading is the conservative one, and the loop has no way to know it was meant narrowly).

**What the stamp is stamped ON, and the vector guard.** The rows that were the group's text in that vector, by number (`group_revisions.clean = true`, `clean_run_ref = <the pass's run>`). If a Writer install moved the vector while the pass ran, the rows it read are gone from the current line, and stamping the rows that replaced them would credit a clean read that never happened — so nothing is stamped, the result says `vector-moved-mid-pass`, and the new vector simply owes its own pass. The stamp is IDEMPOTENT (an already-stamped row is skipped: a replayed settle writes no row and no journal line).

**The selection is a read, which is why there is no revocation machinery.** `phrase/reviewed.ts:cleanSelectionOf` answers, per group: (1) the group currently carries an unresolved issue → NOTHING is selectable for it, stamped rows or not; (2) otherwise take the newest row that carries the stamp and is not `discarded`, ordered by NUMBER rather than by `state` — because the newest clean revision of a group whose latest text failed review is an `archived` row, and reaching it is the entire point. A finding raised after the fact therefore invalidates a selection with no bookkeeping at all, and the fall-back to the earlier clean revision needs no code either: "newest clean row that is not currently blocked" IS the fall-back (L-3 — the rows are the state, and the answer is identical after a restart). A PARTIAL selection still has a hash, but only a `complete` one names an Overview.

**The compiled reviewed Overview.** `phrase/catalog.ts:composeReviewedContentFrom` joins EXACTLY the rows it is handed — never a `latest`, never `PhraseGroup.content`, never a fallback to the live text — through the same `shared/phrase.ts:composePhraseContent` rule as the live Overview (§4.19), so the reviewed text and the chosen text are byte-identical the moment the Author chooses. A helper that quietly preferred the current text would put an unreviewed sentence inside the Summary the Reader is being asked to grade.

**The gate on the score (this amends §17.14's precondition).** `loop/work.ts` candidate 2b now arms on `cleanSelectionOf(...).complete`, and its claim key is `wi_summary_score_<cleanVectorHash>` — the SELECTION's hash, not the live vector's. Three consequences, all intended:

- a Writer install whose newest revision still carries an unresolved defect does **not** re-arm the seat: nothing reviewable changed, and a score of unreviewed text is the number this requirement exists to stop showing;
- the seat re-arms the first time the clean selection **advances** (a deep pass clears a group, or a later clean revision supersedes an older one), because the key is that selection's hash — the §17.5 re-arm rule, moved one level up;
- **a refused pass no longer strands the vector.** §17.14 spent the seat on any CLOSED run, so one provider that could not answer `SectionScores` left a Clean Overview unscored until a Writer happened to rewrite it. The seat now re-arms while the hash has no score rows, and `phrase/section-scores.ts:SECTION_SCORE_ATTEMPT_CAP` (2 — one pass and one retry, the same number as `GROUP_WRITE_ATTEMPT_CAP`) is what stops a permanently broken provider from buying a Reader every sweep. Two refusals and the vector simply stays unscored, which §17.14 already establishes costs nothing: the score gates nothing.

The text the Reader is SHOWN is the same selection, enforced at compile time rather than by convention: `run/phase1-prompt.ts:compileSectionScorePrompt` takes the hash off the Work Item id (`phrase/exclusion.ts:sectionScoreVectorHashOf` — the identical read the settle uses to stamp `vector_hash`), composes the reviewed Overview behind it, and THROWS if the selection has gone or has since moved. A throw costs one attempt and re-arms the seat on the new hash; a silent substitution would file a judgement under a book the Reader never saw. So **shown text == scored text == recorded `vector_hash`**, which is the property §17.14's `vector_hash` exists to guarantee.

**What D-67 does NOT change.** P6 and §17.14's table: no score value and no scoring seat in any owed count enters the criteria engine, drain threshold, hill-climb, sign-off gate or goal math, and `groupPipelineOwed` deliberately gains NO scoring/review field (adding one would put a scoring seat in an owed count, which §17.14 forbids, and would rewrite the pinned `{drafts, structuredReviews, deepReviews}` shape). `lockPhase` is untouched; the legacy `'sampling'` book stamps nothing (its deep review claims `wi_review_<revision>_a<pass>`, which the hash reader answers `''` for); `lockPhase`, the sign-off bar, the write allowance and R3's closure rule all read the same rows as before.

**Pinned by.** `phrase/reviewed.test.ts` (the user's three-group example reproduces r3/r3/r4 exactly; a clean pass stamps every group of the vector; an open issue on one group stamps the other four; a moved vector stamps nothing; a replayed settle adds no second stamp; a `fixed` finding stays `fixed` after the discard), `loop/section-scores.test.ts` (the seat does NOT arm on five installed texts alone; it arms on the clean selection; a refusal re-arms and the cap stops it at two; the score rows carry the reviewed hash), and `run/phase1-prompt.test.ts` (the score prompt's whole user turn — the Seed, the Knowledge, and the five SELECTED sections, so a group's newer unstamped text is nowhere on the wire while every heading prints the selected number; it refuses a selection that has gone, naming the group whose stamped row an unresolved issue still holds; and it refuses the hash whose selection has moved, then compiles the newer selection under its own hash).

### 17.17 D-68 — the Author chooses the Reviewed Summary (R13)

**The requirement.** Once a complete clean selection exists the Author must be able to TAKE it: the reviewed Overview becomes the book, and the texts the choice passed over stop being candidates. One Author-only verb does this — `POST /stories/:id/phrases/reviewed/choose` — and it is a route, not a tool: no crew member chooses the Summary, the Author does (§7's one-writer rule is about prose, and this is the Author's decision surface, the same one the per-group revise route lives on).

**The choose is an INSTALL, not an adoption in place — and R2 is why.** The obvious implementation is "flip the selected rows to `latest`". It breaks §4.19's sentence twice over: a group's line is "created in order, one per accepted text, the HIGHEST number is `latest`", so adopting r4 while a discarded r5 sits above it leaves a `latest` that is not the highest number; and the next install numbers from the GROUP row (`installGroupRevisions`, R2's per-group line), which would mint number 5 a second time. So the choose performs an ordinary install of the selected texts: **five new `latest` rows at each group's next number**, each carrying the chosen text, the text it replaces archived, the Overview recomposed from the rows as they now are, and §4.2's mirror rewritten **in the same commit** (R6). After that line the live Overview and the reviewed Overview are the same bytes, which is what makes "choosing" a decision rather than a second definition of current. The new rows carry `run_ref: null` and `work_item_ref: 'phrase_reviewed_choose'` (no run wrote them, P11) and deliberately **no `finding_refs` and no `task_refs`**: a choice resolves nothing on R3's evidence rule, exactly as on the hand-edit route. They are also **not** clean-stamped — they never sat through a review pass — so the book has no new reviewed Overview to choose until the next cross-group pass earns one, which is the honest reading of a Summary nobody has re-read.

**The discard state, and the one thing it must not do.** `'discarded'` joins `GROUP_REVISION_STATES` (`archived`, `latest`, `discarded`): terminal, hidden from every current read, never selected again. The rows **PERSIST**, and that is load-bearing rather than tidy: `phrase/findings.ts:groupResolvesFinding` and `findingResolution` walk the whole history with no state filter, so a finding that a discarded revision closed stays `fixed`. The obvious alternative — delete the losers, which is what D-22 does to competing drafts — would quietly un-fix half the book, and R2's retention rule (§4.19, the one place P13's deletion does not reach) forbids it anyway. `deleteStory`'s purge of the three Phrase collections still owns these rows (D-63).

**The one place a discarded row is still read: the diff's "before" side.** §10's group row carries `previous_content` so the pane can show latest-against-previous, and it has always taken that from the newest `archived` row. A choose's install archives exactly ONE row per group — the one the new number replaced — and the discard step then flips that row along with every older one, so a post-choose group holds NO `archived` row and the text the Author actually passed over (Ending's r5) is `discarded`; a choose would therefore leave the pane without a "before" side on the one transition worth reading. So the projection falls back to the NEWEST discarded row of that group when the group has no `archived` row to name — after a choose that is every group, and the newest discarded row is by construction the text the installed one replaced — and `groups[].history` still hides discarded rows from the list. This is a read of RETAINED history for a diff, not a candidate: nothing that decides what is current consults a discarded row, and the fallback can only ever name text the book already published.

**The choice record, and the read that refuses to lie.** The Phrase row gains `reviewed_vector` (`{groupId: number}`), `reviewed_vector_hash` (`groupVectorHash` of it) and `reviewed_at`. Reading it back (`chosenSelectionOf`) re-validates every named row: each must still EXIST and still carry its Clean stamp. That single check is what makes a Seed reset or a scrap honest without touching the invalidation code — `editSeed` resets the five group rows to `''`/`0` and `scrapPhasesAfter` deletes revision history, so a persisted `{groupId: 4}` could otherwise point at a brand-new unreviewed text and be reported as the Author's pick (D-63's reset semantics, seen from the read side).

**The state the rail shows** (`phraseView.reviewed.state`): `pending` — no complete selection and no valid choice; `ready` — a complete reviewed Overview the Author has not chosen; `chosen` — the recorded choice stands. Immediately after a choose the state is `chosen` even though the selection reads empty, for the reason above (the chosen rows are the live rows and carry no stamp); `stale: true` is the rail's "there is newer reviewed text to take" line. Scores join on `vector_hash` and nothing else — the reviewed block reports the hash it is describing and the chosen block reports its own, so the number shown beside a section is the number that judged the text shown beside it. The full wire shape is §4.19's `PhraseView` and `src/storyline-server.swagger.yaml`.

**What the choose refuses, and what it does not do.** 409 on a `'sampling'` book (no catalog, R6), 409 when no complete clean selection exists — naming each group and whether it is unwritten, has no clean revision, or is held open by an unresolved issue — and 409 while a live Writer holds ANY group, naming the holding run ids (R4: the choice installs five texts at once, so one live hold anywhere is enough; the rail's disabled button reads the same `liveGroupRefs`). It does **not** touch `lockPhase` (choosing is not locking — §6.3a's lock is the Author's separate declaration, and the loop keeps running after a choose), it opens no task, files no finding, and never writes `summary_scores` (§17.14's boundary runs the other way too: the score reads the selection, the selection never reads the score).

**Persistence: snapshot v4, one-directional.** `SNAPSHOT_VERSION` moves 3 → 4 because a new STATE is persisted, not merely new fields: a v3 build has no reading for `state: 'discarded'` — its projection filters on nothing, so a text the Author rejected would come back as a live member of the group's line. The migration itself is nothing: absent `clean` means "never earned a clean pass", absent `reviewed_vector` means "the Author has never chosen", and no v3 book can hold a discarded row, so a v4 build reads a v3 book as if the feature had never run. The version number is what refuses the unsafe direction.

**Pinned by.** `api/phrase-routes.test.ts` ("Reviewed Summary on the wire" — the `ready` block's exact key set and gap ledger, the choose's 200 body with its `sources` ledger, the chosen text on `overview`/`content`/`reviewed.chosen.overview`, `writer_busy` naming the holding run, the three 409s with their sentences, the post-choose group rows whose `previous_content` comes from the group's NEWEST `discarded` row while `history[]` still lists only the installed row, and the `Object.keys(phraseView(...))` list gaining `reviewed`), `phrase/reviewed.test.ts` (five new `latest` rows at the next numbers; every other row `discarded`; the Phrase row records the vector; a `fixed` finding stays `fixed`; a reset clears the recorded choice), and `store/store.test.ts` for the snapshot version.

### 17.18 D-69 — the group lock (R-lock: settled text stops being work)

**The requirement, in the Author's terms.** §17's loop has one direction: a group is rewritten until nobody can name a defect in it. The Author needs the OTHER direction — *"this section is finished: stop rewriting it, and stop bringing it up"* — and needs it as a fact the book holds, not as a mood the next sweep happens to share. The Author also decided, when D-65 put a Reader's number on the rail, that **a number would not be allowed to make this decision**: the crew may recommend a lock, and the recommendation must be a judgement over evidence rather than `score >= n`. So there are two ways to lock a Summary group, and they are the same write with different hands on it: the Author's verb, any time, for any reason; and one optional Editor seat that asks the question section by section when the Story grants the permission.

**What a lock IS, stated where it is written** (`phrase/locks.ts`, whose file header is the authority every other module reads out of these two writes):

1. **The text is signed off.** The group keeps the revision it has, and the lock NAMES that revision (`locked_revision`) and who fixed it (`locked_by`) and when (`locked_at`) — so an audit can tell a lock from a later text that merely carries the flag. A lock writes no `group_revisions` row, never touches `content` or `revision`, does not recompose the Overview and does not write the mirror: the words are byte-for-byte what they were, before and after.
2. **Nothing may rewrite it.** `phrase/revisions.ts:installGroupRevisions` refuses a batch that names a locked group — WHOLE batch, before the first write, so a Writer answer that arrived after the lock lands as a refusal rather than as a half-applied install — and `phrase/exclusion.ts:groupWorkTargets` never offers a locked group in the first place, which makes the install check the guard rather than the routine path.
3. **A claim against it is VOID, not owed.** At lock time, a finding whose EVERY named group is now locked moves to `discarded` (§4.10's established void state — D-33c already reads `fixed`/`discarded` as closed, and nothing else in the ladder means "about text that cannot change"). A claim that also names an unlocked group stays open and stays work, for those groups only: `phrase/findings.ts:findingActionableScope` is the read that subtracts the frozen part, and every read that decides WORK (`findingResolution`'s `missing`, `findingGroupsStillOwed`, and through them the batch planner and the drain probe) uses it. That is what keeps a cross-group defect off a locked group's back instead of parked on it forever — and it is why an all-locked claim is discarded rather than left `approved` with nothing to do.
4. **The other groups move; this one is the fixed point.** Consistency work belongs to the unlocked side, and D-66 keeps one defect in one pass, so a defect crossing a locked group becomes one pass over the groups that can still answer it. `phrase/reviewed.ts:cleanSelectionOf` composes the reviewed Overview OVER the locked text for exactly that reason, and every brief that shows a locked section says so in the same sentence (`run/phase1-prompt.ts:renderLockedPeerBlock` / `LOCKED_PEER_NOTE`: the text is signed off, no pass may rewrite it, treat its facts as the facts your own sections must agree with, and answer a FIX TASK that names it in the unlocked groups you were shown, not in the locked text — one branch in one renderer, so the Writer, the reviewer and the Reader cannot disagree about what the badge means).

**A flag on the row, never an edit dressed up as one.** The four fields are the entire mechanism, and they are ADDITIVE: `locked` absent or `false` means unlocked, which is the reading of every book written before the feature and of a group whose lock the Author lifted. Clearing means writing `null` into `locked_revision` / `locked_by` / `locked_at` (a store `update` merges through JSON and cannot delete a key), while a never-locked row holds no key at all — which is why §4.19 says ABSENT means unlocked and an unlock says NULLS means unlocked. Two operations write them and nothing else does: `lockPhraseGroup` (the four fields, under a CAS against the row the caller read, so an install that moved the text mid-flight loses rather than being locked mid-image) and `unlockPhraseGroup`. **A repeated lock is a refusal, not an idempotent no-op**: `ensurePhraseCatalog`'s idempotence is a read-that-writes-nothing reaching the same state twice, and a second lock is an action with an audit line and a timestamp; silently re-dating a lock the Author already set is the quiet rewrite this package refuses elsewhere.

**Who may act, and why the unlock has one owner.**

| actor | may lock | may unlock |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **Author** | any group-managed Story, always — `POST /stories/:id/phrases/groups/:groupId/lock` needs nothing beyond a written, unlocked group | always, whoever set the flag — `POST …/unlock`, and `phrase/locks.ts:unlockPhraseGroup` refuses any other actor outright |
| **Editor** | only on a Story with `stories.editor_group_locking` `=== true` (absent is OFF, and OFF means the module produces no candidate, no run and no lock), and only from its judgement seat — the settle calls `lockPhraseGroup` with `actor: 'editor'`, and there is NO crew tool that reaches the flag: an Editor holding a lock tool would be a second, unaudited path to the same write | never — `editor_unlock` |

Lifting a lock is the decision that REOPENS signed-off text, and a crew that just closed a defect against it should not be able to sign the book back out. That asymmetry is why the Editor's seat has no unlock, why the Author's route always acts as `author` (a dashboard verb cannot honestly report an Editor lock it did not take), and why flipping `editor_group_locking` off lifts nothing: the Setting governs whether the seat is armed, never whether a flag on a group row stands.

**The seat — five gates, one per section, optional by construction.** `loop/editor-lock.ts` owns the crew's read of "is this section lockable right now", and the generator (`loop/work.ts` candidate 2c), the compiler and the settle all ask the same function, because three call sites with three opinions is how the rail and the loop start disagreeing about what is settled. In the order that costs least to ask: (1) the Setting; (2) a COMPLETE clean selection (`phrase/reviewed.ts:cleanSelectionOf`) whose entry for this group IS the group's current text — not "the group has text", because a lock fixes a revision and the revision the Editor judged must be the revision the flag names, so a group whose newest revision is not yet clean simply waits, and the seat re-arms by itself when the clean selection ADVANCES because the key IS that selection's hash; (3) a Reader score row for THIS group at THIS vector with a non-blank `rationale` — no reason written down means no evidence, and the seat is not bought on a number nobody explained; (4) the group written, unlocked, and NOT held by a live Writer (`phrase/exclusion.ts:liveGroupWriters`, the fail-closed read, so a holding run that names no groups still holds this one); (5) no closed run already under the key, so a `false` stands until the clean selection moves — the same spending rule the structured Critic seats use, and one lock can never spend another section's seat. The key is `wi_group_lock_<cleanVectorHash>_<groupId>` (`groupLockSeatKey`), spelled like the review and deep-review families because it is a vector-keyed seat; the group id is in it because five sections need five answers, and five judgements collapsed into one pass is how a lock would stop being a judgement about one section. It is offered at `priority: 2` with `rank: 1`, LAST in the class, because an unlocked section keeps being rewritten — the loop's healthy state — so when two seats are eligible the freeze is the one whose absence costs least. **And it is OPTIONAL work on purpose**: `groupPipelineOwed` has no lock field and `GROUP_PIPELINE_RUN_PREFIXES` does not know `wi_group_lock_`, so the drain, the sign-off gate, the hill and the goal never wait on it, and a Story that turns the Setting off mid-round is not left owing a seat it can no longer take.

**The evidence, and the judgement that replaced the cutoff.** The prompt (`compileGroupLockPrompt`) shows the ONE candidate section at that clean revision, the Reader's row for that text (number, rationale, quoted line), the open claims that name it — issues AND Proposals, from the lock's own open-claim read (`loop/editor-lock.ts` over `loop/types.ts:OPEN_FINDING_STATES`: `unconfirmed`/`confirmed`/`approved`/`disputed` — a wider list than D-33c's holding set, because evidence of a live disagreement is worth showing even where it gates nothing), read through `findingGroupScope` so an UNSCOPED whole-Overview claim is visible rather than silently missing — the claims about it that already closed, the locked peers with the revisions standing behind them, and the unlocked peers. Each bucket is capped at `GROUP_LOCK_CLAIM_RENDER_CAP` lines in id order, because the seat runs up to five times per vector and an unbounded block would put the whole finding history in front of the Editor five times (§5's cache rule wants one state to compile one byte sequence). The binding contract is engine-owned (`GROUP_LOCK_INSTRUCTION`): `lock: true` requires TWO quotable halves — **no remaining issue** (every open claim naming the section is settled by words you can quote, or is about material phase ③ writes and the Summary never owed) and **no major improvement missing** (the Proposals and the Reader's rationale name nothing that would change what the section commits to, its own logic, or how the others depend on it) — and the number is refused as a decision in BOTH directions: a high mark whose rationale quotes a seam still owes work, and a modest mark whose rationale names only a preference is not by itself a reason to withhold the lock. `false` is stated as ordinary and unquotaed, because the seat only opens on text a review already passed and a seat that feels free locks things by accident. **The Author's no-cutoff rule is enforced structurally, not by discipline:** `GroupLockDecision` carries no score and no threshold field; no eligibility rule compares a number to anything; and the catalogue row's copy (`templates.ts:editor-group-lock`) is pinned digit-FREE, prose an Author edits being exactly where a cutoff would creep back in. D-65's boundary is not moved by any of this — §17.14's mechanical sentence is amended to record the ONE thing the loop reads from `summary_scores` (a section's `rationale`, as evidence) and nothing else: no score value enters the criteria engine, the drain, the sign-off gate, the hill or the goal math.

**The settle, and the three refusals that all write nothing.** `loop/engine.ts:settleJob` validates the answer against the SEAT (`parseGroupLockAnswer` — the group named must be the group the seat was claimed for; a blank `rationale` is refused because the in-process validator ignores `minLength`), then re-runs the guard ONE line above the write, because the item was queued whenever the generator ran and an Editor pass may sit in the queue for a whole capacity window: in that window the Author may flip the Setting off, lock or rewrite the section by hand, or let a Writer install land under the pass, and a lock fixes a REVISION, so executing a stale judgement would freeze words nobody judged. The guard is the cheap sentence-returning half; `lockPhraseGroup` re-checks the same state and throws before writing, so the pair cannot leave a half-applied lock. An refused answer, a moved state and a domain race all write nothing and throw nothing out of the settle: the run is already CLOSED, so the seat is spent, the section simply stays unlocked, and the generator re-arms for the NEXT clean selection. What moves is exactly one row plus the voided claims — no verdict, no task, no finding, no draft, no counter — and the journal line carries the Editor's own reason first (`run <id>: <rationale>; the Editor's judgement pass closed <group> as finished text`), because "locked by the loop" is not the claim the Author reads.

**Findings are voided, not resolved, and an unlock resurrects nothing.** The states a lock may void are the four `phrase/locks.ts:VOIDABLE_FINDING_STATES` lists (`unconfirmed`, `confirmed`, `approved`, `disputed`); `fixed` and `discarded` are terminal and are left alone. A `disputed` row is voidable HERE even though it left the issues-first HOLDING set on 2026-11-25, because the two lists answer different questions: D-33c's holding set (`loop/types.ts:ISSUE_HOLDING_STATES`) asks what still counts as work the flow must wait for, and `disputed` is out of it — the Editor's verdict releases a proposal and a Clean verdict. This list asks what a lock may retire, and a lock stops text from ever being rewritten again, so an unproven claim about it goes void rather than standing unanswerable. The scope is read AFTER the row is written, which is what makes an UNSCOPED claim — "the whole Overview" — void exactly when the last of the five groups locks and not earlier. Nothing un-voids on unlock: `discarded` is terminal in §4.10, and an unlock that un-discarded a row would invent work the Author already watched close; a defect the crew still believes in is filed again by the next Critic pass against the now-writable text. The ATTRIBUTION itself is never rewritten — `findingGroupScope` still names every group the claim was about, because the record says what the defect concerns even where the book stopped being able to act on it — and the crew's filing surface follows the same rule in two answers: `tools/reader.ts:raiseFinding` REFUSES a claim whose every named group is locked ("a locked group has no next revision to answer it") and, on a mixed scope, stores the UNLOCKED subset, which is the domain's own subtraction stated in the form the planner and the rail act on. An unscoped claim is left exactly as reported: narrowing a scope the crew never typed would invent an attribution.

**On the wire.** A group row carries `locked`, `locked_revision`, `locked_by` and `locked_at` **only while a live lock stands** (`api/projections.ts`): the store keeps nulls after an unlock, and a null on the wire would be a number-shaped or string-shaped lie about an absence, so "absent" is the unlocked reading and no consumer may cache the fields across reads. The lock report answers `{story_id, phrase_id?, group_id, locked: true, locked_revision, locked_by, locked_at, voided_findings: {finding_id, previous_state, scope}[], view}` and the unlock answers the same envelope with `locked: false`, the three metadata fields literally `null` (the route states the state it left the row in, as the row states it) and no `voided_findings`; both ride the fresh `view` so the rail's badge moves with the write rather than with a poll (the revise/choose envelope, D-68). `voided_findings` is reported because the lock just shortened the Author's queue and an unexplained silence there reads as a bug. The Setting sits on the Story RECORD (`PATCH /stories/:id` as a strict boolean, refused with its own sentence for `"false"` or `0`, since the domain reads the permission as `=== true`) and the Story's row-scoped PATCH answer does not carry it — read it back from `GET /stories/:id`. **Snapshot v4 is unchanged:** four additive fields, no new state, so a build before the feature and a book after it read each other exactly as §4's preamble requires.

**A Seed reset lifts a lock, and so does the scrap that deletes the rows a lock names.** `loop/engine.ts:editSeed` resets the five group rows to `''`/`0` and clears the four lock fields with the same nulls an unlock writes, because the Seed reset is the one flow that rolls a group back to revision `0` with no content — a lock left standing there would freeze an empty group nobody ever judged. A **backtrack** clears them too, for a reason one layer down: `loop/invalidation.ts:scrapPhasesAfter` deletes the scrapped phase's `group_revisions` rows, and `locked_revision` is a fact ABOUT one of them (`phrase/reviewed.ts:cleanSelectionOf` resolves a locked group's Overview entry through its `latest` row). The section's text and revision number survive, exactly as L-31 requires — only the dead flag goes, written as the four `false`/`null` fields an unlock writes, and the claims the lock voided stay void (§4.10 makes a discard terminal). Keeping the flag was the failure mode: a locked group with no revision backing can never re-enter the reviewed Overview, and `phrase/revisions.ts:installGroupRevisions` refuses a batch naming a locked group WHOLE, so one stale flag stopped the loop re-installing all five sections and rebuilding the Overview until an Author unlocked a lock they never set. What clears NOTHING is a scrap that deletes no revision backing — a backtrack to `chapters` — where every lock still names a live `latest` row and stands. Pinned by `loop/group-seed-reset.test.ts`.

**What D-69 does NOT change.** No new collection, no new snapshot version, no new phase, criterion, tool or permission row (the crew has no lock tool; the seat's product is a settle-time write, exactly as D-65's score rows are). §6.3a's `POST …/lock` phase sign-off is a DIFFERENT decision on a DIFFERENT record — and the two locks point in opposite directions on purpose: `summaries.locked` freezes the legacy MIRROR and still lets the group rows install, while `phrase_groups.locked` freezes the GROUP row and refuses the batch. Phases ② and ③ are untouched, a `'sampling'` book has no group rows and never sees a lock field, a seat or a refusal, R2's revision lines and R3's closure join keep their semantics (a void is a state change, never a claim of resolution), and the Reviewed Summary still composes over whatever the rows hold — including locked text.

**Pinned by.** `phrase/locks.test.ts` (every refusal code and its sentence, the four-field write and the CAS, the voiding rule including the unscoped-scope-means-last-group-locks case and the `disputed` row the lock may void even though it stopped counting as an open defect on 2026-11-25 (`VOIDABLE_FINDING_STATES` answers what a lock may retire; `ISSUE_HOLDING_STATES` answers what holds work — see the paragraph above), and the unlock that clears to nulls and resurrects nothing); `api/phrase-lock-routes.test.ts` (the 404-before-verb rule, the sampling-book 409 naming import, the live-Writer 409 naming run ids, the `group_unwritten` / `already_locked` / `not_locked` sentences, the ten group-row columns with the four lock keys appended ONLY for a locked group, the lock's `view` envelope and the unlock's three nulls); `loop/editor-lock.test.ts` (the five gates each in isolation, the key and its first-underscore split, the foreign-key reads that answer "no seat", the spent-seat scoping to one Story, the answer validator, the guard's sentences); `loop/phase1-editor-lock.test.ts` (five seats on one clean vector, the exact Work Item including `templateRef: 'editor-group-lock'` and `toolMode: 'none'`, the settle that writes ONE flag and no draft/verdict/task/counter, and the moved-state window); `tools/reader-group-locks.test.ts` (the wholly-locked refusal with both `fix` branches, the mixed scope narrowed, the unscoped claim untouched, the legacy book inert); `templates/templates.test.ts` (the catalogue row's shape, the seat's copy citing the Critic's claims and the Reader's rationale, `false` as a complete and ordinary answer, and the DIGIT-FREE pin that keeps a threshold out of the copy);
