`loop/group-seed-reset.test.ts` (a Seed reset lifts the lock, so no empty group stands frozen; a backtrack to `summary` clears a lock whose revision rows it deleted while the section's text survives byte-for-byte, the void it wrote stays void, and the cleared group installs again — whole batch, Clean stamp, composed Overview — where the stale flag used to strand all five sections; and `scrapPhasesAfter(…, 'chapters')`, which deletes no revision backing, leaves every lock standing); and the dashboard's `*.locks.*` suites (the rail's badge, the Author's two verbs, the Writer-hold refusal on BOTH of them, and the Setting's own wording).

---

## 18. The Plotlines Phrase — Phrase ② on the same engine (2026-11-26, R11)

**Status: normative, and it supersedes §6's phase-② machinery for a Story whose `stories.plotlines_mode` is `'groups'` AND whose Author has started Phrase ② — the two facts together are the lane, and the one read that answers them is `phrase/plotpoints.ts:plotlineLaneIsLive(store, story)`.** Every other book runs §6/§16's phase ② exactly as documented there, with the crew's plotline tools live: every Story written before this date (the flag is ABSENT, and ABSENT means `'classic'`, R6's rule applied one phrase down), every Story whose Author sets `'classic'`, and every Story born after this date whose phrase the Author has not started. Nothing was migrated, `SNAPSHOT_VERSION` is still **4**, and the dial predicate is `phrase/catalog.ts:isPhraseGroupManaged(story, 'plotlines')` — a STORY SETTING read, never an inference from the presence of `phrases` or `phrase_groups` rows.

**Why this wave happened, in one paragraph.** §17 left two sentences that were promises rather than facts: R1's "the catalog is data, so a second phrase kind is a catalog entry, not a loop change", and §3's "the one phrase kind this build enables". Phase ② is where the promise gets tested, because it is the one later phase that already has a record-shaped loop of its own — one contract per chapter, drafted, read, locked. What Phrase ② turns out to need is not a second pipeline but the SAME pipeline with different answers: the writable part is an entry (`chapter_<order>`) rather than one of five fixed groups, an entry's answer is a set of **plotpoints** rather than a prose string, the entry set is DECIDED (by a brief and a chapter count) rather than enumerated in the catalog, and acceptance moves the Author's `chapter_count` as well as the texts. So the loop stopped naming a phrase: every question it used to answer with the word "summary" is now a hook on a controller, and the two phrases coexist on one book — the Summary's five groups under one `phrase_id`, the chapter entries under another, sharing the revision-line, exclusion, findings, lock, score and reviewed machinery underneath.

### 18.1 The requirement ids this section answers

| id | the contract, in one line | where it lives |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **R11** | Phase ② becomes a PHRASE on the engine §17 built, and the phrase layer stops naming phrases. Five parts, all pinned: **(a)** one Phrase Controller per enabled kind answers every phrase question the loop and the prompt compiler ask; **(b)** the phrase's entries are a counted `chapter_<n>` set generated from the brief's count, with their own key namespace and their own composition rank; **(c)** the crew's answer contract is per-phrase (`ChapterDraft`, plotpoints, phrase-scoped arity); **(d)** the lane's authoring replaces phase ②'s `write_unit`/`select` divergence, and the classic plotline tools are refused on entries the lane owns; **(e)** the Author starts an on-demand phrase with a verb, reads it with a per-key rail read, and the dial is a separate flag validated like `summary_mode`. | `phrase/controller.ts`, `shared/phrase.ts`, `phrase/controllers/plotlines.ts`, `phrase/plotpoints.ts`, `shared/schemas/chapter-draft.ts`, `loop/work.ts:phase2GroupCandidates`, `run/phase1-prompt.ts:promptLaneOf`, `tools/plotlines.ts`, `api/routes.ts` |

**What the code cites, and where each id really lives (T-W6 citation pass).** The wave's comments, tests and swagger carried `R8`, `R9`, `R15`, `P9` and `D-30` in places this file does not support: `R1`–`R7` are §17.1, `R12`/`R13`/`R14`-era ids and `R-lock` are §17.15–§17.18, and **`R8`, `R9` and `R15` were never defined anywhere in this file** — they are the outer plan's requirement numbers, and this section refuses to invent meanings for them. Every one of those citations now points at a rule this document owns:

- the "absent means `'classic'`" rule, cited as **P9** in `src/storyline-server.swagger.yaml`, `api/routes.ts` and the start-route test, is **R6's ABSENT rule applied to `plotlines_mode` (§4.1)** — which is what §4.1 already says, and what those comments now cite;
- the copied-Seed rule and the one-way Start verb, cited as **D-30** (which is §8's Critic-instruction decision, and is still exactly what the loop's Critic copy cites), are **D-72**; the per-key rail read and the Start route's shape are **D-76**; and the dial that shuts the moment the row exists is **D-71** — the routes, the swagger, the desk copy and their pins say so;
- the rail's phrase-header disclosure, cited as **R8**, is **U-21** in §10.2; the employee thread surface (Running by default, Archived an explicit per-Story opt-in and a filter), cited as **R8**/**R9**, is **U-22** in §10.2 beside **U-7**; the harness-rank copy in `run/story-engine.ts` and the Agents rail, cited as **R15**, is **D-38a**/**V-2**; and the phase-① refinement gate, cited as `R8/D-49` in `loop/phase1-gates.test.ts`, is plain **D-49**.

Two ids still cited in comments are deliberately left as what they are: the wave's own task labels `T-W1`…`T-W6`, and the older dashboard comments' `R4`–`R7`/`R10`/`R14`, which predate §17 and name outer-plan steps rather than requirements this file defines.

### 18.2 D-70 (R11) — the Phrase Controller: one object per kind, and no new `if (phrase === 'summary')`

`src/server/phrase/controller.ts` is the seam, expressed the way this package expresses everything else: **a controller is a plain object of functions bound to one catalog definition, not a class hierarchy.** The registry `PHRASE_CONTROLLERS` maps a catalog key to its controller; `phraseController(definition)` is the pure read the generator and the compiler use, `phraseControllerOf(store, storyId)` is the read that also checks the phrase row EXISTS on this book, and `startedPhraseControllers(store, storyId)` is what the sweep walks when it needs "every phrase of this book that has a row".

The hooks are the whole contract: `isManaged` (does this Story author this phrase in the grouped model), `phraseOf`, `groupsOf`, `start`, `ensureGroups`, `answer` (the name and JSON Schema every job of this lane carries), `parseDraft` (the in-process checks the schema cannot express), `install` (what an accepted text does beyond writing rows), `composeEntryContent` (how one entry's text is rendered — prose for the Summary, labelled plotpoints for a chapter), `draftClaimRef` (the claim family the drafts are scoped to), and `instructions` (the four engine-owned instruction sets the prompt compiler appends: draft law, review, score, lock).

**The rule is written as a check a reviewer can run**, and it is the reason this section exists: *a loop or run change that supports a third phrase must not need a new `if (phrase === 'summary')` — it asks the registry and calls the hook.* The phrase-name branching that remains lives inside the install's downstream sync (`phrase/revisions.ts`), and that branch is allowed to name kinds **because its whole job is to refuse an unmapped one rather than guess**: the Summary branch writes the §4.2 mirror through a compile-time-checked identity map, the Plotlines branch REQUIRES the entry's `plotpoints` and writes §4.3's contract through `phrase/plotpoints.ts:syncPlotlineContract`, and a kind that is neither gets a reported `reason: 'not_summary_phrase'` on the install result instead of a silent empty mirror. **An unknown phrase id is a refusal, not a fallback** — `phraseController` throws rather than treating an unmapped kind like the Summary, which is the failure mode that would let a third phrase half-work: rows written, no contract parsed, mirror guessed.

The Summary controller (`phrase/controllers/summary.ts`) is one instance of the same shape: it overrides nothing and inherits nothing, and it answers the same questions with the Summary's answers. The refactor's obligation was to leave phase ①'s bytes alone, and `run/phase1-prompt-plotlines.test.ts` pins that literally — including the two lock-seat sections (`## CANDIDATE SECTION`, `## LOCKED PEERS`) where a lane refactor would most quietly have rewritten the wording.

### 18.3 D-71 (R11) — the catalog entry, the dial, and the lane being TWO facts

`shared/phrase.ts:PHRASE_CATALOG` holds **exactly two definitions**, and the second is the point of this section:

| field | `summary` | `plotlines` |
| -- | -- | -- |
| `order` / `enabled` | 1 / true | 2 / **true** |
| `groups` | the five, enumerated | **`[]`** — "the entry set is decided by the phrase's brief, not enumerated here" |
| `entry_policy` | `static_groups` | **`chapter_entries`** — a counted `chapter_1…chapter_N` set generated for the brief's N |
| `brief_kind` | `seed_brief` | **`plotline_brief`** |
| `seed_source` | `story_idea` | **`supplied`** — text handed to the start step: the compiled Phrase ① output, or the Author's own text |
| `starts_on_demand` | false | **true** |

**`enabled` and `starts_on_demand` are different questions and the wave kept them apart on purpose.** `enabled` answers "does this phrase exist as a concept the product supports"; `starts_on_demand` answers "is its row created before anyone has handed it a seed". Conflating them would have forced Phrase ② back to `enabled: false`, which would have made the dial predicate unable to answer for it and the loop unable to plan its work. So `ensurePhraseCatalog` now walks `autoCreatedPhraseDefinitions()` — enabled AND not on-demand — and story creation still mints exactly the one `phrases` row and five `phrase_groups` rows it always minted, idempotently, with the same UUIDs (D-63, R1's addresses outlive the text). Pinned at the data layer by `src/shared/phrase.test.ts`, because every one of those values is read by a prompt, a route or the create path, and a range check would pass on a wrong value.

**The entry key namespace is per-phrase, and that is what makes two phrases safe on one book.** `chapter_<order>` is generated by `phraseChapterDefinitions(entryCount)` under the `chapter_entries` policy; composition takes the phrase's own rank map (`phraseGroupRankOf`, `composePhraseContent(entries, rankOf)`), so the Summary's call sites compose byte-identically while a chapter phrase composes in chapter order. A chapter's number is read back off its key (`phraseChapterNumber`) — an entry whose key the policy did not mint cannot be mirrored, and the parse refuses it rather than writing a contract at order 0.

**The dial.** `stories.plotlines_mode` (§4.1) is checked by the same two-words rule on both Story routes — `POST /stories` (799, checked at 864-871) and `PATCH /stories/:id` (1721, checked at 1814-1822); each copy names the two pipelines in its own words, and both say that omitting the field leaves the Story as it is. A Story is born `'groups'` unless its caller asks for `'classic'` (`loop/engine.ts:2887`). **The dial is Author-movable only while the phrase row does not exist:** a PATCH whose value differs from the EFFECTIVE one (`story.plotlines_mode ?? 'classic'`) on a Story with a started Phrase ② is a **409** naming the row that made it unmovable (1834-1844), because the copied seed, the chapter entries and the claim-family keys were all written under the pipeline chosen at the start. A write that changes nothing is NOT refused — a rail that re-PUTs the row it read must stay idempotent.

**The lane is the dial AND a started row, and the AND is load-bearing, measured rather than reasoned.** Every Story the engine creates is born with `plotlines_mode: 'groups'` (`loop/engine.ts:2887`), so a guard that reads the dial alone refuses §8.1's plotline tools on every book whose Author never pushed a Summary into Phrase ② — the predicate's own note records the cost as 18 failures across `loop/{findings,drain,trend,slice,verbs}.test.ts` and `integration/slice-{phases,chapters,ops}`, green again with the started-row clause (`phrase/plotpoints.ts:plotlineLaneIsLive`, 338-360). Read the lane through `plotlineLaneIsLive`; read the dial through `isPhraseGroupManaged`; and never write the two as one predicate, because the honest states are "classic", "groups but not started" and "live".

**One consequence deserves saying out loud, because it changes what an Author sees on a fresh Story:** `loop/status.ts:phase2Unmet` (423) runs only for a Story that is already in `phase: 'plotlines'` (the call at 193) and then reads the DIAL (426), so a Story born `'groups'` whose Author never starts Phrase ② reports phase ② as unmet with the sentence that the compiled Summary (or the Author's own text) has to be pushed into the phrase first — the classic phase-② work still RUNS (the lane suppressions need the lane), but the phase cannot sign off while the drain says the phrase is missing. An unstarted phrase is not a broken phrase; the drain says who has to move.

### 18.4 D-72 (R11) — start → brief → entries: the copied seed, and the three writes

**Starting is an Author act, and the loop cannot do it.** `phrase/controllers/plotlines.ts:startPlotlinesPhrase` writes the row — `title`/`description`/`order` from the definition, `content: ''` (nothing composed yet, and the same "empty is not a placeholder" rule `ensurePhraseCatalog` states), `revision: 0`, `group_refs: []` — and the one field this wave added to §4.19's row, `seed`: the text handed in, **copied** through `phraseSeedInitOf`, which reads the definition's `seed_source` so a third on-demand phrase copies its own input without this call site changing. The seed is copied, not referenced, and that copy is the independence rule: `phrase/catalog.ts:phraseSeedOf` reads `phrases.seed` and falls back to `stories.idea` for a row written before the field existed, and every later read of THAT phrase — the brief's `seed_hash`, the structuring claim key, the law text in the prompt — goes through it. So editing the Story's Seed moves Phrase ①'s hashes and leaves this phrase's gates untouched, which is what the start-route test asserts first ("the Summary moved afterwards and the phrase did not"), against the rows and against the rail's `seed_head`. **The ONE hand that has moved (2026-11-26, D-78):** the Summary's **accept** is an Author act too, and it performs this start on the Author's behalf — `api/routes.ts:startPlotlinesFromAcceptedSummary` reaches this controller (through the idempotent `PhraseController.start` wrapper) after the lock commits, with `seedText` set to `api/projections.ts:compiledSummaryOf` trimmed, i.e. the same `source: compiled` arm the Author's own Start verb takes and the same bytes the Handoff card previews. What did NOT move is the sentence this paragraph is a title for: **the loop still cannot do it** — no sweep, no candidate, no drain sentence and no `phase2Unmet` line starts the row — and the manual verb remains the only way to hand Phrase ② the Author's own `text`, the verb that starts it on a book the accept skipped (the dial moved to `'groups'` only after the accept ran), and the verb that still answers its one-time `409`; an accept that cannot start REPORTS the reason and leaves the lock rather than refusing one. And the seed is still **copied**, not referenced — which is exactly why the accept copies the compiled bytes instead of pointing Phrase ② at the Summary: the Summary may keep moving, gaining sections and re-scoring, while this phrase's gates stay keyed to the words it was seeded with.

**A brief is what turns a seed into a chapter set** (§4.20), and the accept is the moment records appear: `acceptPlotlineBrief` writes (1) the entries through `ensureChapterGroups` — `chapter_1…chapter_N` from the COUNT, all N at once, because the entry set has to be whole the moment it exists; (2) `summaries.chapter_count`, the primitive only; (3) the brief's `status`, last. Idempotent by skipping, and it refuses a brief whose `seed_hash` no longer matches the phrase's seed BEFORE any of the three. `chapter_count_synced` and `created_group_ids` come back so a route and a test can tell a first accept from a re-press.

**D-3b survives the new hand on the field.** The number is still one no WRITER chooses: `structurePlotlines` is the Editor's structuring pass — the same authority the classic phase ② pipeline always had, and the same one §3.3's Seed Brief has for phase ① — and a `ChapterDraft` cannot move it, because the answer has no `order` field and no arity choice: the arity it is checked against IS the materialised set (§18.5). The consequence for the Goal is mechanical rather than a new criterion: on the lane, "exactly `chapter_count` plotlines" holds because one commit wrote the count and the entry set together, and the contract mirror runs off that set.

### 18.5 D-73 (R11) — the `ChapterDraft` contract, and an arity rule that is phrase-scoped

`shared/schemas/chapter-draft.ts` is the pipeline's fifth no-tool answer contract, and the first whose product is a set of fields rather than a text: `{groups: [{group_ref, heading?, must_happen, must_change, pov?, setups?, payoffs?}], knowledge_used: []}`. It deliberately does NOT reuse `GroupDraft`: a Summary group's answer IS its prose; a chapter entry's answer is the plotpoints §4.3's record already holds and `createPlotline` already takes, so installing an answer is updating that chapter's contract and the mirror stays lossless. **`order` is not an answer field**, for the same reason `GroupDraft` has no `group_key`: the chapter number IS the entry the job named by id, and an answer that also carried one would let the model disagree with the entry it was asked about and make the loop choose.

`phrase/plotpoints.ts:parseChapterDraftAnswer` is where the real rules live, because the in-process validator ignores schema keywords it does not implement (`minItems`, `minLength` are provider-side hints). It refuses, WHOLE, and in this order: an answer with no `groups` entries; an entry that names no group; a `group_ref` that is not one of **this phrase's** entries (never a key, never a Summary group, never an invented id); a group this job did not ask for ("writing a chapter nobody requested revises an untouched one", R2); a group answered twice ("two contracts for one chapter is an ambiguous batch"); an entry whose key is not a chapter key, because the mirror could not say which chapter it writes; an entry with a blank `must_happen` or `must_change` ("an entry that says neither is a chapter title, not a contract"); and a requested chapter left uncontracted — **a batch that skips a chapter it was asked for installs nothing**. The accepted contents come back in the PHRASE's order from the book's rows, not the answer's, so two identical answers journal identical rows (D-44/D-45's cache rule).

**The arity rule is phrase-scoped, and that is the seam this whole section turns on**: "answer every part the job carried" means five Summary groups on one lane and N chapter contracts on the other, and neither number may be hard-coded in a schema or in a shared module. `ChapterDraft` is a JSON Schema and a TS mirror, not a §4 record, so there is no zod mirror to keep in step (V-2's rule is about §4 rows) and the snapshot does not move.

### 18.6 D-74 (R11) — one loop, one prompt compiler, per-phrase instructions

**The lane's work is generated by `loop/work.ts:phase2GroupCandidates`, which returns NOTHING for an unstarted phrase** (the loop cannot invent the seed the chapters are read from). In order:

| step | job | kind · crew | the rule |
| -- | -- | -- | -- |
| 1 | `structure_seed` | Editor · `editor-plotlines-structure` (bound explicitly, because the unbound Editor pool pick is `editor-general`) | priority 0, claim `wi_structure_plotlines_<phrase seed hash>` (`loop/records.ts:plotlinesStructureKeyOf`), and it REFUSES everything below it: with no accepted brief there is no chapter count, no entry set and no arity. The seed and its hash ride in the job note because the tool refuses a hash it did not print. |
| 2 | `draft_groups` | Writer · `writer-plotlines-sampler`, `toolMode: 'none'`, answers `ChapterDraft` | the same three cuts phase ① uses — R4's deferral, the per-entry allowance, the dial cut BETWEEN finding-sets (`groupWorkTargets` / `planGroupDraftBatch` / `groupDraftClaimRef`, all phrase-scoped) — the only difference being that the claim ref is **the phrase row id** (`controller.draftClaimRef`) so the two phrases' families never collide. Its unit is `undefined` = the whole-Story artifact: the chapter lane's artifact is the SET of contracts, and no single row covers it. |
| 3 | `review_groups` | Critic · the four `DEEP_REVIEW_TEMPLATES` angles, rotating | `summary_critics` seats per **chapter vector**, keyed by the shared `groupReviewSeatKey` on that vector, so an install re-arms every seat by itself. Every chapter is in the brief, not just the batch that moved the vector: a setup planted in chapter 2 and paid in chapter 7 is one issue naming both. Phase ② has no `critic-screening` in its pool (that row serves phase ①), which is why the seats rotate the deep-review angles. |
| 3b | `score_sections` | Reader · `reader-consistency`, `rank: -1` | one seat per **CLEAN vector of THIS phrase** — `cleanSelectionOf(store, story, phrase.id)` is the whole difference from phase ①, because the Summary's clean selection says nothing about whether chapter 4's contract survived a cross-chapter pass, and the hash the seat claims must be the hash of the chapters it renders. D-65's display-only exception applies unchanged: the number is the Author's, and it holds nothing (§18.6's drain). |
| 3c | `group_lock` | Editor · `editor-general`, `rank: 1` | R-lock on chapter entries, armed only while `stories.editor_group_locking` is on and scoped to this phrase — an unscoped read would arm a chapter seat against the SUMMARY's clean selection and refuse every one of them. The seat runs on `editor-general` because `editor-group-lock` serves phase ①; the half that BINDS the answer is the controller's Phrase ② lock instruction the compiler appends. An offer, never a debt, and skipped for an entry this sweep is already drafting. |
| 4 | `check_unit` | Critic · the deep-review angles, tools ON | once every structured seat for the vector has reported. This is the pass that turns "the Critic noticed a broken chain" into a FINDING carrying `group_refs`, and its engine-owned note says the part the role copy cannot: these are CONTRACTS, not prose, the phrase seed is general rather than an outline, added detail and an unplanted setup are not defects, a defect that crosses chapters is ONE issue naming both chapters, and an empty pass is valid. |

**The prompt compiler picks the lane from the JOB, never from the phase** (`run/phase1-prompt.ts:promptLaneOf`, reading the job's group rows), and then all four group compilers (`compileGroupDraftPrompt`, `compileGroupReviewPrompt`, `compileSectionScorePrompt`, `compileGroupLockPrompt`) render the controller's law text for that lane. The settlement does the same: `loop/engine.ts` resolves the owning phrase from the item's group refs and routes the validator and the instructions per phrase, so `draft_groups` means one job kind with two answer contracts — and the Clean stamp accepts either dial.

**The template rows are assigned LAST** (`templates.ts`: `editor-plotlines-structure` then `writer-plotlines-sampler`, both `serves_phase: ['plotlines']`, at the end of `DEFAULT_ASSIGNMENT`), for the ordering reason §17's lock row also obeys: `records.ts:templatesFor` returns a role's assigned templates in assignment order and an unbound job binds the FIRST, so a phrase row placed at the front would put every unbound Editor or Writer job on a phrase prompt.

**What the lane does not get.** No research leg: the Seed's pass ran before the phrase was started, and the brief's `research_questions` are notes for the Writers, not D-51's gate (§4.20). And no seat holds the drain that phase ①'s does — `loop/status.ts:phase2Unmet` reports exactly five things: the phrase not started; no accepted brief declaring the chapter count; chapter-pipeline runs in flight (naming how many are scoring); owed drafts/fix passes; owed structured seats; owed deep passes. The score seat and the lock seat are absent from that list, as in phase ①, and the one honest gap is named in the code rather than hidden: **a phase-② `fix` task with NO finding behind it** (a hand-armed row) drives no group work, because `groupWorkTargets`' two reasons are "never written" and "an open finding owes it" — the classic path would have handed it to a `write_unit` Writer. In group mode the row holds the drain through the shared open-task clause, so the Author sees a queue row nothing is consuming instead of a silent re-draft.

### 18.7 D-75 (R11) — ownership: the lane's writes, and the reader pass that has to count an install as a write

**Three classic legs go away on a live lane, and the filter is `laneWritesContracts = plotlinesGroups && plotlineLaneIsLive(...)`:** phase ②'s `write_unit` pair for `plotline` units, the `wi_fix_*` fix legs aimed at plotline units, and the Editor's `select` over competing plotline drafts (the whole D-22 divergence, since an accepted chapter answer installs immediately, R5's argument one phrase down). **What stays is deliberate:** the classic reading and verification legs keep running for `plotline` units, because a lane install is not a run the reader ledger can see — `needsReader`'s test is "did a Writer run for this unit", and a phrase batch writes rows without one. So `loop/work.ts:contractInstalledByPhrase` exists: the reader candidate treats an installed contract as a write, scoped to `unit.kind === 'plotline'`, which leaves every chapter (phase ③) answer and every classic-book answer byte-for-byte unchanged.

**The crew's two refusals are the backstop, not the routine path** (§8.8): `createPlotline`/`editPlotline` answer `R6` for an order an entry covers, with that entry's id, key and revise route in the `fix`; `raiseFinding` answers `R3` by ROUTING a `plotlines` finding to the chapter entry that covers its order (`phrase/findings.ts:findingGroupNamespace`), refusing only when no entry covers it. Because the fix legs are suppressed, an `R6` refusal should be rare — the guard exists so a run that reaches for the retired door is told where the current one is, exactly as D-61 did for the Summary.

**The mirror write, in the install's commit** (`phrase/revisions.ts` → `phrase/plotpoints.ts:syncPlotlineContract`): **create-or-update BY CHAPTER ORDER**, because P4's "one chapter carries one contract" is the same rule `createPlotline` enforces by refusing a duplicate order, so a redraft of chapter 3 is chapter 3's contract rewritten, not a second one. **Every field is written, blanks included, on both paths** — a complete answer is a complete statement, and keeping a payoff the installed text no longer claims would break C-5/C-6's mechanical checks. The **three engine-owned flags** (`contract_locked`, `aligned`, `needs_recheck`) are set exactly as `createPlotline` sets them on a CREATE and are left ALONE on an UPDATE: the lock is the Author's (L-7), `aligned` is the last verdict's, `needs_recheck` belongs to invalidation (L-11).

### 18.8 D-76 (R11) — the wire: one phrase per read, and the Start verb

| verb | what it says |
| -- | -- |
| `GET /stories/:id/phrases/:key` | ONE phrase's rail: its `key`, `mode` (widened to `PhraseMode`, which now includes `'classic'`), `title`, `revision`, the composed `overview`, `entries[]` with their revision lines and scores, the phrase's `brief`, and `seed_head` (the first 400 characters) with `seed_chars` — the text the Author is shown when they press Start. A key the catalog does not enable is a **404**; an enabled phrase the Author has not started is **not** a 404 but an EMPTY view, because "nothing has been started here" is a state with a door attached and the Start panel has to be able to say so. |
| `POST /stories/:id/phrases/:key/start` | The Author hands the phrase its seed: `{source: 'compiled' \| 'custom', text?}`, default `'compiled'`. `compiled` takes what `api/projections.ts:compiledSummaryOf` says is compiled — the complete CLEAN selection if there is one, else the phrase's composed content — which is the SAME read the rail previews, so a button cannot offer what the verb then refuses. The refusals are product copy and are pinned verbatim (L-13): `custom` with no text, an empty compiled Summary, a second start on the same phrase, a key the loop starts by itself (the Summary is not on-demand), and — on the group verbs — a `'classic'` Story asked to revise a chapter entry, whose sentence names both doors: PATCH the dial, POST the start. |
| `POST …/phrases/groups/:groupId/revise` | Now phrase-aware. For a chapter entry the body carries `plotpoints`, never raw `content` — the entry's prose is generated from the fields, so a hand edit that rewrote the label lines would put the entry and its `plotlines` mirror out of agreement — and the response returns the inline `plotpoints` alongside the composed text. The Summary's `{content}` leg is unchanged. |
| `PATCH` / `POST /stories` | `plotlines_mode` on both under the same two-words rule (each copy names the two pipelines in its own words); once the phrase row exists, ANY change to the dial is a 409 naming that row, and a write that changes nothing stays legal. |
| The Story view | Gains `plotlines_mode` (echoed, never derived), `phrase_started: {summary, plotlines}` and `summary_compiled` — the last two are what let a Start panel disable itself for the right reason instead of guessing from row counts, and `summary_compiled` is the SAME predicate (`projections.ts:summaryIsCompiled`) the `source: 'compiled'` start reads. |
| The OpenAPI document | `/phrases/{key}` and `/phrases/{key}/start`, plus `plotlines_mode` and the plotpoints schema. |

**The desk leg has landed and its proof landed with it (re-verified 2026-11-26), and this stays a file-and-probe record for the one thing a vitest run cannot pin — the LIVE browser — not because the tree is still moving.** The transport and the state layer are in: `src/dashboard/api.ts` declares `getPhraseDetail`, `startPhrase` and `revisePhraseEntry` and widens the Story view with `plotlines_mode`, `phrase_started: {summary, plotlines}` and `summary_compiled`; `state.ts:refreshPhraseDetail` fetches Phrase ②'s read LAZILY — `phraseDetailWanted` asks for it only when the dial says the entries are the surface or that surface is already open, so a `'classic'` Story never pays for a read nobody is looking at — and caches its failure in its own per-Story slot, so the Plotlines pane can never print the Summary read's sentence; `shell-binding.ts` binds the verbs; `story-contract.ts` answers the phrase's questions (`plotlinesEntries`, `plotlinesBrief`, `plotlinesStarted`, `plotlinesHandoffRefusal`, `plotlinesSurfaceView`, and `isPlotlinesGroups` as the one dial-plus-read question). The surface arrived with it: the phrase-generic entry pair `components/PhraseEntries.tsx` (`PhraseEntryRow`, `PhraseEntryRows`, `PhraseReadState`, `PhraseEntryDetail`, driven by a `PhraseSurface` so Phrase ① passes `SUMMARY_SURFACE` and Phrase ② passes `PLOTLINE_SURFACE`), `components/PlotlinesPhrasePane.tsx` (the brief card, the chapter entries, the contract editor), and `components/PlotlinesHandoff.tsx` — the Author's Start card, enabled by `story.summary_compiled`, which `api/projections.ts:compiledSummaryOf` computes as the SAME text the start route copies its seed from, so a disabled button and an accepted start stay one fact. `StoryView` routes on `isPlotlinesGroups(view)`, which is why a classic book keeps §4.3's records pane byte-for-byte, and `story-nav.ts` numbers the pipeline Summary ① → Plotlines ② → Chapters ③ — `ALL_STORY_PHRASES` carries the numbering and the pane titles, while the rail's own rows are the two in `STORY_PHRASES`, so Chapters has no rail row and the rail reaches a chapter from a pane's own link — with Characters listed OUTSIDE the numbering as a supplement (`STORY_SUPPLEMENTS`). **What was open — the proof — is CLOSED (verified 2026-11-26):** the grep that used to answer nothing now matches THIRTEEN desk test files (`api.plotlines`, `state.plotlines`, `StorylineApp.plotlines`, `StoryView.plotlines`, `StoryOutline`, `PlotlinesPhrasePane`, `PlotlinesHandoff`, `story-folders.plotlines`, `story-nav.groups`, `story-nav.seed`, `PhraseSeedPane`, plus the U-25 correction pass's two: `real-server-seed`, the rail-click integration file, and `DirectiveBar`, which now also runs on the grouped dial), `yarn vitest run src/dashboard` answers `736 passed` across 59 files and `yarn typecheck` answers clean — both from the package directory, because the same desk suite scoped from the repo root answers `44 failed | 692 passed` tests over `6 failed | 53 passed (59 files)` on a missing `__APP_VERSION__` define in `components/TopBar.tsx`. **What stays open is the LIVE-browser behaviour**, which no vitest run substitutes for; a reader who quotes this paragraph still re-runs that grep before repeating the sentence about it. §10.2's U-20 carries the requirement and this status.

### 18.9 Superseded, retained, and untouched

| superseded **for a live Plotlines lane** | retained, and why |
| -- | -- |
| phase ②'s `write_unit` divergence and the Editor's `selection_rules` choice over competing plotline drafts (D-22/D-23/D-24 for the PLOTLINES unit only), the phase-② `wi_fix_*` legs, an UNSCOPED phase-② `plotlines` finding (a `plotlines` finding now routes to the entry that covers its order), the crew's `createPlotline`/`editPlotline` on an `order` an entry covers, and §3/§17.1's sentence that the Summary is the only enabled phrase kind. | **Every other book, whole.** A Story with `plotlines_mode` ABSENT or `'classic'`, or with no started phrase row, keeps §6's phase ② and §8.1's plotline tools with none of these refusals in its path — and every guard keys on `plotlineLaneIsLive` / `isPhraseGroupManaged(story, 'plotlines')`, never on the phase, so a chapter (phase ③) unit on a lane book sees none of it either. The classic reading and verification legs stay; the classic Plotlines pane stays as the `'classic'` reading surface. |
| §4.2's "`chapter_count` is written by the Author's hand" as the ONLY hand. | **D-3b itself.** The number is still declared by the structuring step and never chosen by the drafting step; §18.4 says which authority the accept carries, and D-3c's rule (change the count after the lock ⇒ backtrack reopens phase ②) is untouched. |
| The reader pass's "a Writer run wrote this unit" test, as the only way a `plotline` unit becomes judgeable. | **`needsReader` itself**, plus its verdict and rotation rules: `contractInstalledByPhrase` adds one fact to one unit kind, which is why a chapter unit's answers are unchanged. |
| The install's mirror block as a Summary-only side effect. | **R6's mirror rule.** The Summary's five prose fields are still written by one commit and nothing else; the Plotlines branch ADDS a second downstream sync and reports `reason: 'not_summary_phrase'` when it is not the Summary's install, rather than pretending the mirror moved. |
| Phase ②'s drain sentences, as the classic pipeline's counts. | **§6.4's step vocabulary and the sign-off gates**: the lane's five sentences name phrase/brief/entries/seats instead of rounds, the Goals, criteria (C-0…C-8), the dial (D-35), the Ideas tray, L-7's lock gates and D-33's ladder are unchanged, and `plotlines_locked` still means exactly what §6.3a says. |
| — | **§17's machinery, shared rather than forked**: R2's per-entry revision lines, R3's attribution and all-or-nothing closure, R4's row-derived hold and attempt allowance, D-66's one-defect-one-pass, D-65/D-67's clean-vector score (display-only, P6's one exception, no new wording), D-68's choose and D-69's lock semantics (a lock is a fact about a revision row, voids claims, and is never un-voided). Phase ③, the Digest, knowledge, the employee roster, sessions and the run lifecycle are untouched. |

### 18.10 New requirement ids introduced by this section

- **D-70 (R11)** — the Phrase Controller: one plain object per enabled kind answers `isManaged` / `phraseOf` / `groupsOf` / `start` / `ensureGroups` / `answer` / `parseDraft` / `install` / `composeEntryContent` / `draftClaimRef` / `instructions`; the registry is `PHRASE_CONTROLLERS` and an unmapped phrase kind is REFUSED, never treated like the Summary; the checkable rule is that a third phrase must not need a new `if (phrase === 'summary')`, and the only surviving kind-naming branch is the install's downstream sync, whose job is to refuse an unmapped mapping (§18.2).
- **D-71 (R11)** — the catalog entry and the two-fact lane: `PHRASE_CATALOG` enables `plotlines` with `entry_policy: 'chapter_entries'`, `brief_kind: 'plotline_brief'`, `seed_source: 'supplied'`, `starts_on_demand: true` and `groups: []`; `ensurePhraseCatalog` walks `autoCreatedPhraseDefinitions()`, so story creation still mints one phrase row; `stories.plotlines_mode` (absent ⇒ `'classic'`) is the dial, `plotlineLaneIsLive` (dial AND a started row) is the lane, and a guard that conflates them is a bug (§18.3).
- **D-72 (R11)** — the copied seed and the accept's three writes: `Phrase.seed` is additive and read only through `phraseSeedOf` (fallback `stories.idea`), so the phrase's gates are immune to a Story-Seed edit; `acceptPlotlineBrief` writes the `chapter_1…N` entries, `summaries.chapter_count` and the brief's `status`, in that order, idempotently, refusing a stale `seed_hash` first — and D-3b is unbent because the authority is the structuring pass (§18.4, §4.20). **Amended 2026-11-26 (D-78, §6.3a):** the START that mints the row is still an Author act and still impossible for the loop, but it is no longer always the Author's OWN click — accepting the final Summary performs it, copying `compiledSummaryOf` onto the new row's seed in a second commit behind the lock.
- **D-73 (R11)** — `ChapterDraft` and phrase-scoped arity: plotpoints, not prose; no `order` in the answer; the eight whole-batch refusals in `phrase/plotpoints.ts:parseChapterDraftAnswer`; results ordered by the phrase's rows; a schema and TS mirror with no §4 row, so no zod mirror and no snapshot change (§18.5).
- **D-74 (R11)** — one loop and one compiler with per-phrase instructions: the lane's six candidates in the order above, the phrase-scoped clean selection behind the score seat, the phrase-scoped lock seat on `editor-general`, the lane chosen by the JOB's group rows in the compiler and by the item's group refs at settlement, the two new template rows assigned LAST, and the drain's five sentences with the finding-less-`fix`-task gap named (§18.6).
- **D-75 (R11)** — ownership: `laneWritesContracts` suppresses phase ②'s `write_unit`, fix and `select` legs, `contractInstalledByPhrase` keeps the reader pass honest about an installed contract, the `R6`/`R3` tool refusals carry the grouped door in their `fix`, and `syncPlotlineContract` writes §4.3 by ORDER with every field written and the three engine flags touched only on create (§18.7, §8.8).
- **D-76 (R11)** — the wire: `GET …/phrases/:key` (404 for an unknown key, an EMPTY view for an unstarted phrase), `POST …/phrases/:key/start` (`compiled` | `custom`, the compiled source read by the same projection the rail previews), the phrase-aware revise route taking `plotpoints`, the two Story routes validating `plotlines_mode` with one sentence, and the Story view's `plotlines_mode` / `phrase_started` / `summary_compiled` (§18.8).
- **D-77 (2026-11-26, T-W6) — the Phrase ② scrap radius: a Seed edit deletes ② WHOLLY, and backtracking to ① does the same.** Phrase ② was built AFTER §17.9 wrote the invalidation spine, so `editSeed` and `backtrack('summary')` tore up the Summary (reset in place, ids kept, D-25) and left ②'s phrase row, catalog rows, `group_revisions` rows and `plotline_briefs` rows standing on words that no longer exist. The rule, in both directions. **(a) A Seed edit is a FULL clear (D-25a), so ② is DELETED** — phrase row, groups, revisions, briefs — whatever the two dials say, and **backtracking to ①** deletes it the same way. ② is DELETED rather than reset like ① because starting it is a one-way, one-time action (D-71, D-72: `startPlotlinesPhrase` refuses a phrase that already exists — `phrase-start-routes.test.ts:41` pins it), so a surviving row would dead-end the book with no route, no UI and no sweep able to reopen it; a reset would also be unreadable, since the `plotlines` surface describes an OPEN list and reset entries would read as "the Author accepted a blank book". **(b) Backtracking to ② is the narrow cut:** the phrase row, its entries and its accepted brief STAY (D-71's "what it already has is kept", read one level down) and only the locks whose `locked_revision` the wipe scrapped are CLEARED (D-69). **(c) The gate is the PHRASE ROW, never the dial:** `loop/invalidation.ts:scrapPlotlinesPhrase` runs on `rank < phraseRank('plotlines')` and checks `storyPhraseByKey`, because a book whose `plotlines_mode` was flipped to `'classic'` mid-flight still has rows to honour, and a dial-gated cut either deletes state the Author can still see or keeps rows the Summary no longer supports. **(d) `deleteStory` purges `plotline_briefs` with the rest (D-63)** — an FK row that outlives its Story has no reader left. **(e) A copy is unaffected:** the Seed edit runs on the original, which is exactly what D-72's independence pin observes. What the radius does NOT touch: `chapter_count`, a legacy book's sampling rows, and ③'s own invalidation — all as §17.9 already describes. One implementation, `loop/invalidation.ts:scrapPlotlinesPhrase`, called by `scrapPhasesAfter` at rank 0; pinned by `loop/phase2-blast-radius.test.ts` and by `api/story-delete.test.ts`.
- **U-20** (§10.2) — the rail is per-phrase and the desk must read it that way; recorded with the desk's honest mid-wave status (§18.8) rather than as a claim that the surface exists.

No new principle, criterion, phase, permission or snapshot version was introduced, and §2's P6 exception (D-65) is reused verbatim rather than widened: a chapter score row is the same display-only fact as a Summary section's.

### 18.11 What this wave did NOT do (the honest list)

- **CLOSED (T-W6): `plotline_briefs` no longer outlives the Story.** `engine.deleteStory` purges `phrases`, `phrase_groups`, `group_revisions` AND `plotline_briefs` in its one commit (D-63, D-77 (d)), and `api/story-delete.test.ts` proves it by naming every collection and counting the neighbouring Story's rows, so a later cleanup cannot reopen the hole without a failing test.
- **CLOSED (T-W6): a Story-Seed edit deletes Phrase ② entirely (D-77).** The fix took the explicit RULE rather than a re-copy. `editSeed` — and `backtrack('summary')`, which runs the same spine — DELETES ②'s phrase row, catalog rows, revision rows and briefs, whatever the dials say, because starting ② is one-way (D-71, D-72) and a reset with a surviving row would dead-end the book. The hole was worse than the old note described: the sweep reached `phrase_groups` through the Summary-dial-gated `phraseGroupsOf`, so on a `summary_mode: 'sampling'` / `plotlines_mode: 'groups'` book there is no Summary phrase at all and a Seed edit left the WHOLE of ② standing. The old defence — "the phrase's own seed governs it, so the brief stays current" — is withdrawn: the phrase's `seed` copy is now made by the Start verb and never left behind, so D-72's independence applies to the only case that still has a copy. `loop/phase2-blast-radius.test.ts` pins the whole delete (both dial combinations, a sibling Story, and the Summary's own reset staying byte-for-byte as §17.9 describes) and the narrow cut (`backtrack('plotlines')` keeps the row, the entries and the accepted brief, drops the revisions the wipe scrapped, and clears only the locks that named them).
- **The Summary controller's instruction slots duplicate the compiler's constants.** `phrase/controllers/summary.ts` carries the four Summary law texts that `run/phase1-prompt.ts` also carries; the controller is now the intended single owner and phase ① has not been moved onto it. The code says so; §17.10's R7 sentence ("the compiler owns the binding sentences") is the one that has to change when this is closed, not before.
- **Comment drifts, listed so nobody re-derives them as behaviour.** CLOSED (T-W6): `shared/schemas/chapter-draft.ts` pointed at `phrase/controllers/plotlines.ts:parseChapterDraftAnswer` while the function is in `phrase/plotpoints.ts` (the controller imports it) — the pointer now names the real file, as `knowledge.md`'s two references to it already did. CLOSED (T-W6): `loop/work.ts`'s phrase-lane comment claimed a lane is materialized "once `plotlines_mode` is 'groups' and the chapter entries exist"; the gate is a started Phrase ② ROW (`engine.ts:createPlotlineEntriesForPhrase`), which is what `plotpoints.ts` always said. STILL OPEN: `shared/types.ts`'s note on `PlotlineBrief.research_questions` claims §3.3's waiver semantics while `tools/plotlines.ts` — correctly, per §4.20 — treats the list as descriptive notes.
- **CLOSED (T-W6): the ids this wave cited that the spec did not own.** Every loose `R8`/`R9`/`R15`, the wrong `P9` and the wrong `D-30` are re-cited to ids this file defines — `D-71`/`D-72`/`D-76`/`D-77`, `V-2`/`D-38a`, `D-49`, `R6` applied one phrase down, and the new `U-21`/`U-22` (§10.2) — with the map in §18.10. `R4`–`R7`/`R10`/`R11`/`R14` stay spelled that way on purpose: each carries its wave marker, and §18 is the record that names them.
- **The desk leg is pinned; the live-browser probes are not** (§18.8, U-20): the transport, the state layer, the shared entry components, Phrase ②'s pane and the Start card now have dashboard tests naming them (U-20 lists them), which closes the "written, not pinned" line. What remains is §18.8's checklist of what vitest cannot reach — the real browser's focus rings, scroll behaviour, and the rendered look of the phrase panes.

### 18.12 How this is verified

```bash
# from the repo root — the Phrase ② suite (vitest only; `node` is not a runner here)
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/shared/phrase.test.ts                       # the catalog AS DATA: two kinds, the on-demand flag, the per-phrase key namespace, the phrase-scoped composition
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/phrase/plotlines.test.ts             # the controller registry, the START (a copied seed), the per-phrase dial, the brief (a count becomes N entry rows), the install (an entry text AND that chapter's `plotlines` contract, updated BY ORDER on a redraft), each phrase's arity, the two phrases' claim-key and exclusion DISJOINTNESS, and the refusal an unmapped kind gets instead of a silent no-op — the `plotline_brief_0` id scheme is pinned here
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/loop/phase2-groups.test.ts           # the PLANNER in isolation: which candidate exists in which state, under which key, with which unit, and what the drain row says — the five sentences verbatim, and the SILENCE of the same probe on a `'classic'` book
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/run/phase1-prompt-plotlines.test.ts  # the BYTES of the four chapter-lane turns, and the Summary lane's bytes UNCHANGED through the lane refactor
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/api/phrase-start-routes.test.ts      # the Start verb and its refusal sentences, the per-key rail read, `plotlines_mode` on both Story routes, and a Start that leaves the Summary alone
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/integration/plotlines-groups.test.ts # the real server, HYBRID: the lane adds the structure/draft/review/score/lock legs and KEEPS the classic verification legs, the chapter set is DECLARED by an Editor run's tool call, the loop cuts the Writer batch itself, the deep review FILES the defect, and the Author's sign-off opens phase ③
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/loop/drain.test.ts                   # the CLASSIC phase-② drain preconditions, untouched by this wave — that is the point a reviewer confirms
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/tools/catalogue.test.ts              # the catalogue at 53 tools, `plotlines` as its OWN group (`plotlines: ['structurePlotlines']`, because §8.2 is a transcription of the spec's Editor table), and the tool's shape transcribed off `tools/plotlines.ts`
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/tools/group-mode-writes.test.ts      # the tool-layer refusals for BOTH phrases: the Summary's D-61 set, and Phrase ②'s `R6` ownership refusal keyed on the PER-PHRASE dial and on the order being phrase-OWNED — with the `fix` copy that names the entry's revise route
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/tools/reader-group-attribution.test.ts # R3's attribution on both phrases: the Summary's group refs, Phrase ②'s chapter routing, and the chapter defect and legacy-book defect that stay untouched
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/templates/templates.test.ts          # the two Phrase ② rows, their `serves_phase`, and the LAST-in-assignment order that keeps unbound jobs off them
# the D-77 radius (T-W6) — the two suites §18.11's CLOSED notes name
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/loop/phase2-blast-radius.test.ts     # a Seed edit and a backtrack to phase ① deleting Phrase ② WHOLE on both dial combinations, the narrow cut a backtrack to ② keeps, and the Summary's own reset staying byte-for-byte (§17.9)
yarn vitest run packages/erosire/create-storyline/src/server/api/story-delete.test.ts             # the delete purge (D-63, D-77 (d)): every story-scoped collection named and purged in one commit, with the neighbouring Story's rows counted
# the reads a reviewer should grep rather than test
grep -rn "plotlines_mode\|plotlineLaneIsLive" src/server --include='*.ts' | grep -v '\.test\.'    # every guard, and the one predicate each of the two questions has
grep -rn "if (phrase === 'summary')" src/server/loop src/server/run                              # the R11 rule: empty, by design
grep -rn "plotlineBriefs" src/server/loop/engine.ts                                               # ONE hit, by design: the purge inside `deleteStory` (`loop/engine.ts:3051`, D-63, D-77 (d)) — §18.11's CLOSED note
```

### 18.13 The desk's per-phrase Seed row (2026-11-26, U-25) — the rail moved, and nothing under it did

**Status: a desk-only wave, recorded in §18 because it is the same Phrase ② handoff this section is about, and it changes no server fact.** The Author's ask, verbatim: "The Seed (The Author's Idea) belongs to the Phrase 1, so it should be under Phrase 1 as seed. Phrase 2 have different seed (which is really the compiled version of Phrase 1 used as seed, but Author can paste his own seed in). Each Phrase are discrete, seperate from each other, but access through tool call that specific to the create story platform, such as 'summaryTool' and 'plotlineTool'." The rail now says what the engine has known since D-72: **each phrase header carries its own `Seed` child row, first in the fold** (U-25, and U-21 amended so the fold takes it), and U-4's story-level pinned card is **amended out of the record rather than deleted from it** — for the reason §18.13's own dead-code note gives: the card was never mounted.

| moved (the desk, and only the desk) | did NOT move, and why |
| -- | -- |
| **The Seed row's parent.** One story-level leaf — the rail's `seed` panel key, whose justifying sentence read "the Seed is pinned truth, not a phrase" (the pre-wave `components/story-nav.ts:63`, whose comment this wave replaced) — becomes **one `Seed` child per phrase header**, first in the fold and folded with it (U-21, amended). Phrase ①'s row opens the read-only pane over `stories.idea` (`components/story-folders.tsx:SeedPane`, fed by `view.story.idea`, which is the Story row and not the phrase read); Phrase ②'s row opens that phrase's OWN seed (`components/PhraseSeedPane.tsx`, a pane that never reads `story.idea` at all, and that asks for the phrase read only on the grouped dial — on a `'classic'` Story it states that no read is coming, in its own words, rather than waiting on one); the rail prints no Seed anywhere else, and the row navigates by a child ref (`story-nav.ts:phraseSeedRef()`), not by a panel key. | **The store and the reads, entire.** `phrases.seed` is still the one additive column §4.20 describes and still has exactly one reader, `phrase/catalog.ts:phraseSeedOf` (D-72 — the copy IS the independence rule, and it is why Phrase ② survives a Phrase ① edit). `stories.idea` is still where the Author's idea lives, and nothing was copied into the Summary phrase's `seed` column, because `phraseSeedOf`'s `stories.idea` fallback already IS Phrase ①'s seed. The hashes move exactly as written today: Phrase ①'s gates hash `seedHashOf(story.idea)` (`loop/records.ts:currentSeedBrief`, 1384 — because that IS Phrase ①'s seed) and Phrase ②'s hash `plotlinePhraseSeedHashOf` → `phraseSeedOf` (`loop/records.ts:1402`). A rail row is a location; a column is a fact; only the first one moved, so there is no migration to write and `SNAPSHOT_VERSION` is still 4. |
| **Where the Author decides ②'s seed, and what the pane can show.** Phrase ②'s Seed pane prints the copy's head — `seed_head` (the first `SEED_HEAD_CHARS` = 400, `api/projections.ts:1489`) and `seed_chars` from `GET …/phrases/:key` (D-76, §18.8) — never the full copied seed, which is not on the wire and was not added to it. The compiled-vs-pasted choice stays exactly where D-76 put it: the Start card's two arms (`components/PlotlinesHandoff.tsx`, U-20) sending `POST /stories/:id/phrases/{key}/start` with `{source: 'compiled' \| 'custom', text?}`, one-way — after the start there is no verb that replaces the seed, because a new seed means a new phrase (D-71's one-time start, D-72's copy, **D-77**'s rule that a Story-Seed edit deletes ② wholesale instead of re-seeding it). | **The tools, none of them.** The ask named `summaryTool` and `plotlineTool`; the catalogue's names are `readSeed` (§8.4) → `structureSeed` (§8.7) → `editSummary` (§8.1) on Phrase ①, and `structurePlotlines` (§8.8) with `readPlotline` (§8.4) and `createPlotline`/`editPlotline` (§8.1, refused `R6` on a live lane, §8.8) on Phrase ②. Nothing was added, renamed or re-scoped, and nothing needed renaming: a crew tool is how an AGENT reaches a phrase, while the desk is a client of the per-phrase HTTP verbs (U-20, D-76). No route, no wire field, no OpenAPI key and no §6/§17/§18 gate moved — the accept, the lock receipt, the reviewed selection and every phrase hash are §6.3a's (D-78), §17.16–§17.17 (D-67/D-68) and §18.4/§18.6's, unchanged. |

**The two factual corrections this section owes, because the requirement it amends described a surface the desk never had.** (1) **`components/SeedCard.tsx` is unmounted dead code**: its only importer is `components/SeedCard.test.tsx`, so U-4's "pinned card" never reached the screen, and the Seed surface the Author actually used was the rail's row plus the read-only pane. The component is left in place — deleting it is a different wave's business, and this file records facts rather than tidying them. (2) **The Author has no edit affordance for the Seed's text**, recorded ONCE at §10.2's U-25 follow-through — the `NewStoryForm.tsx` capture at create and `loop/engine.ts:editSeed` reachable only as `PATCH /stories/:id`'s `idea` — and this section points at that paragraph instead of restating its reasoning, because a hole written in three places is a hole the next reader is asked to fix three times. It predates this wave and survives it, and it does not touch U-12: that requirement rules about WHO may write the Seed, not about whether this desk owns a control for it.

**New requirement id: U-25 (§10.2)** — the per-phrase `Seed` rows, Phrase ①'s Seed as the Author's idea, Phrase ②'s Start-only copy, and the pane's 400-char window — with **U-4 amended** (the story-level pinned card, and the card's own dead-code status) and **U-21 amended** (the fold takes the phrase's `Seed` child). It is recorded in §10.2 and here rather than added to §18.10, which stays R11's own list — the same placement `U-24` got. **Proof, honestly:** this section was written from the reads, not from the render — `projections.ts:phraseDetailView` (the `seed_head`/`seed_chars` window), `catalog.ts:phraseSeedOf` (the one reader and its fallback), `records.ts:1384`/`:1402` (the two hashes) — and the pins are the rail's and the content area's own suites, which cite U-25: `components/StoryOutline.test.tsx` (the `Seed` child on each phrase, its first row, folding with the phrase, taking no attention mark) and `components/StoryView.test.tsx` (the phrase-child selection opens Phrase ①'s Seed through the synthetic ref, and its heading names the phrase). **The desk half of the wave has since landed, and it is pinned** (re-verified 2026-11-26 against the finished tree): `components/PhraseSeedPane.tsx` has its OWN suite now, and `components/story-nav.seed.test.ts` pins the ref, the retired `seed` panel key, the per-phrase title, the absence of an attention mark and the directive bar's focus. **The correction pass added the proof this paragraph could only claim.** `real-server-seed.test.tsx` is an integration pin over a booted server: it clicks ①'s `Seed` row and ②'s `Seed` row on the REAL rail inside the real `StoryView` and asserts the content area that answers each click, so the routing this section describes is no longer carried by stubbed panes — `StoryView.plotlines.test.tsx`'s Summary and Plotlines panes are stubs BY DESIGN, and stay stubs, because that file's subject is routing, not content. The same file answers the classic dial honestly: on a `'classic'` Story `getPhraseDetail` — Phrase ②'s read, and the one read this file spies — is never called at all, and ②'s Seed pane says in its own words that NO read is coming; that classic-dial test never opens ①'s row, so ①'s own text is pinned on the GROUPED book, where the same spy proves the grouped dial asked for ②'s read exactly once. `components/DirectiveBar.test.tsx` now also runs on a GROUPED dial, pinning that the bar focuses Phrase ②'s real ref there (the empty `refs` its first case pins is that surface's real answer, not a gap), and `components/StoryView.test.tsx` prints ①'s P5 badge VERBATIM, so the pane's promise about every agent is a pinned string rather than a sentence in this file. Observed, this tree: a focused run of the three rail/seed files answered `52 passed` — 3 files, 52 tests (`StoryOutline.test.tsx` 32, `PhraseSeedPane.test.tsx` 12, `story-nav.seed.test.ts` 8) — the desk suite answered `736 passed` across 59 files, and `yarn typecheck` answered clean. The `8 failed | 23 passed` this section recorded mid-wave is superseded, not reinterpreted: the same rail file answers `32 passed`. **Desk suites run from the package directory** (`packages/erosire/create-storyline/`): the same desk run scoped from the repo root picks up the root vitest config and answers `44 failed | 692 passed` tests over `6 failed | 53 passed (59 files)` on a missing `__APP_VERSION__` define in `components/TopBar.tsx:155`, which is a runner context, not a finding. What stays open is U-20's LIVE-browser probe, which no vitest run substitutes for.
